# Global Lessons Learned — Cross-Project Best Practices

> Accumulated from real project sessions. Apply these across ALL projects.
> Last updated: April 29, 2026 (567 lessons, 106 categories)
>

## Report Writing

1. **Be specific, not vague** — "Udemy training completed" > "Core training completed". Name the platform, tool, or system explicitly.
2. **Reports must stand alone** — The reader (CEO, Operation Manager, Engineering Manager) shouldn't need the author in the room to explain what a cell means.
3. **Every cell should be self-explanatory** — If someone reads a table cell and asks "what does this mean?", rewrite it.
4. **Same state = same label** — Use identical wording across all rows/departments for the same status. Don't say "Phase completed" for one and "Training done" for another.
186. **Pearl Pixels Quarterly Report and Q1 Report are complementary, not duplicates** — The quarterly report contains unique PM observations (execution-vs-planning mode, untracked study hours, QA validation gaps, zero-activity team members) not in the main Q1 report. Always read both when building Q2 plans or executive summaries.
187. **"Split" means separate into individual entries, never remove** — When user says "split" two items combined in one row, create separate entries for each. "Split" preserves all items, just separates them. Never interpret it as removing the second item.
227. **Verify team roles from the authoritative team directory before assigning action item owners** — Never assume roles from email CC lists or job title signatures. RC Hosting Investigation assigned backend tasks to an Implementation team member (El-afify) and an unlisted person (Badawy) based on email CC assumptions instead of checking DevOps_Teams.md. Always cross-reference the team directory first.
253. **Deep-study all repos and wikis before creating a dashboard** — A shallow pull-and-list misses wiki content (SOPs, architecture decisions, project plans, test reports). Clone wikis, read every file, and explore all branches before claiming a "complete" report. The Taqat dashboard missed 35 wiki pages (5,461 lines) on first pass.

## Status Labels & Completion Markers

5. **"Ongoing" is a lazy status** — Replace with specific next steps: "Phase 2: To Be Defined", "Certification prep continues independently (lab practice & exam)". Show intentional planning, not vagueness.
6. **Completion markers must be consistent** — If one department shows "Training completed" in a phase, ALL completed departments must show it. Never leave blank or `--` for a completed item.
7. **Explain what "independently" means** — If something continues outside the plan scope, say what specifically continues (e.g., "lab practice & exam"), not just "continues independently".

## Bilingual Documents (EN/AR)

8. **Always update both languages for every text change** — Never edit English without updating the Arabic span. Easy to forget on small fixes.
9. **Verify RTL visually** — Arabic layout can break borders, alignment, and table padding. Always open the HTML and toggle to Arabic after changes.
10. **Check both languages after toggle** — A change that looks fine in English might overflow or misalign in Arabic RTL mode.

## Stakeholder Communication & Emails

11. **Give email options, not just one draft** — Provide 3 versions: formal (detailed), concise (professional), action-oriented (direct). Different audiences need different tones.
12. **"Dear All" for multiple managers** — Avoids hierarchy issues. Let the To/CC fields handle the addressing.
13. **Always include what's attached and why** — Don't just say "please find attached". List what the attachment contains (bullet points).
282. **Record email directives into deliverables immediately** — Semir's Mar 16 email changed Flow 2 ownership (Mohammed Agha → Ashik Mohamed/TaqaTechno) but sat unrecorded for 3 weeks, leaving the V2 summary stale with wrong RACI. Always update execution documents when new email directives arrive.
283. **Always verify and use correct job titles in formal documents** — "IT Project Manager" vs "Project Manager & Scrum Master" is a small detail but a big difference in formal deliverables. Cross-check titles against the latest org chart or email signatures before publishing.

## HTML Deliverables

14. **Open HTML after every change** — Code edits can look correct but render wrong. Always verify in the browser.
15. **Version control in filenames** — Use `_v2`, `_v3` suffixes. Keep old versions in versioned folders (`v1/`, `v2/`, `V3/`).
277. **Assessment files for candidates must NOT contain evaluator content** — When cloning assessment tabs into standalone HTML for email, exclude scoring rubrics, reference solutions, interview questions, scenarios, and PM decisions. Candidates see only requirements + deliverables. Evaluator content stays internal. Cost: leaking the scoring rubric lets candidates game the assessment.
284. **Changelog sections are internal tracking, not for stakeholder deliverables** — V2→V3 diff tables help the PM track what changed, but management-facing HTML should not include version changelogs. Remove them from final outputs presented to the board or stakeholders.
285. **Always reference organization identity explicitly in deliverables** — Include the Azure DevOps org name (TaqaTechno) and URL (dev.azure.com/TaqaTechno) in HTML reports. Never leave organization references generic when the audience needs to know which tenant/org is affected.
308. **Hardcoded candidate names make assessment pages non-reusable** — team-assessment-phase0.html had "Shehab & Hala" in 30+ places. When new candidates join, every reference breaks. Use generic terms ("candidates", "current backend team") from the start. Names belong in runtime data (contenteditable fields), not static HTML.
309. **Every HTML deliverable must include a favicon** — Missing favicons make browser tabs show a generic icon, making it hard to identify dashboards when multiple tabs are open. Use inline SVG data URIs (`<link rel="icon" type="image/svg+xml">`) so favicons work without external files. Check all linked HTML files during dashboard reviews.

## Data Validation

16. **Always validate email/source dates first** — Before merging versions, check if source files actually changed. File size comparison catches identical files.
17. **Cost model is the #1 change trigger** — Major version changes are usually driven by pricing/subscription model changes, not content changes.
18. **Use PowerShell COM for .msg extraction** — `Outlook.Application` works but files must not be open in Outlook.
286. **No assumptions on source data — always read the actual files** — Never assume PDF/MD content matches what you remember. The V3 Recommendation had exact user lists (14 users, 2 teams), cost breakdowns (3 scenarios), and 6 role definitions that all had to match the email summary precisely. Always re-read the source before generating derivative documents.
292. **Always verify live system data before finalizing deliverables** — The V3 recommendation (Feb 3) said 24 users. Live Azure DevOps API check (Apr 7) showed 33 users — a 37% growth in 2 months that no document reflected. Always query the live system before publishing user counts, costs, or scope.

## OKR & KPI Dashboards

19. **OKR scoring must be automatic** — Don't rely on manual status entry for KR progress. Use editable "Current" fields that auto-calculate progress percentage, bar color, and status badge. Manual scoring drifts and becomes inconsistent.
20. **Inverse metrics need special handling** — KPIs where lower is better (bug escape rate, carry-over tasks) need inverse progress calculation: if current <= target, it's 100%. Don't use the same formula as "higher is better" metrics.
21. **Separate OKR (strategic) from KPI (operational)** — OKRs are quarterly aspirational goals with key results. KPIs are ongoing team metrics. Mixing them in one table confuses the audience. Use separate tabs/sections.
183. **Dashboard team rosters must stay in sync with actual assignments** — When a team member is added or moved between teams in DevOps, the dashboard roster (HTML arrays, WIQL member lists, team card tables, member counts, and header totals) must ALL be updated together. A member missing from any one array silently drops their metrics from queries. Check all data arrays — dashboards often have 3-4 redundant member lists that must match.

## Search Index Maintenance

22. **Rebuild search index after every pull** — When new HTML pages are added to a repo with a client-side search engine, the search index (e.g., `search-index.js`) won't include them automatically. Always check if new pages need to be added to the file list AND rebuild the index.
23. **New pages must be registered in build script** — Adding a new HTML file to the repo doesn't make it searchable. The file must also be added to the FILES array in the build script (e.g., `build-index.js`). This is a two-step process: (1) add to list, (2) rebuild.
312. **New root-level HTML files are not auto-indexed in the Sessions Index** — Creating a new HTML file in a project folder does not make it searchable in the Claude Sessions Index. A session card must be manually added to both `Claude_Sessions_Index.html` and `.md` with the correct path, badges, and date for the file to appear in search results.

## Project Estimation & Story Points

24. **Always verify project duration across all files** — Different documents (backlog, proposal, basis of calculation) may show conflicting durations. Cross-check all sources after every pull — the latest versioned file (v2) is the source of truth.
25. **Story points need a documented scale** — If a backlog uses SP without defining what each value means, derive the scale from actual story examples. Group stories by SP value, analyze complexity patterns, and document the scale for team alignment.
26. **Person-months ≠ calendar months** — Always calculate effective person-months (allocation % × duration) not headcount × months. A team of 11 at mixed allocations (20%-100%) may only deliver 15.93 person-months in 4 calendar months.
27. **AI acceleration changes all estimates** — When a project uses AI-assisted development, traditional effort estimates don't apply. Document the multiplier per role (QA gets highest ~4x, PM gets lowest ~1.5x) so stakeholders understand the compressed timeline.

## DevOps API Integration

28. **Use real DevOps states, not made-up labels** — Dashboard metrics must match the exact states in Azure DevOps (To Do, In Progress, Resolved, Done, Closed). Using generic terms like "Active" or "Carry-over" creates confusion when comparing with the DevOps board. Always query actual states first with the States API before building queries.
29. **Query by Assigned To when Area Paths don't exist** — If a DevOps project doesn't have team-specific area paths (Backend, Frontend, etc.), query by `[System.AssignedTo] CONTAINS 'member name'` across all projects. This is more flexible than area paths and works cross-project.
30. **Always provide a Data Source tab** — When building dashboards that fetch from APIs, include a transparent "Source" tab showing the exact queries used, results returned, and verification links. The user should never have to trust the numbers blindly — they need to verify against the source system independently.
31. **Discover projects via API before hardcoding** — Don't assume which DevOps projects exist based on email analysis or conversation context. Use `/_apis/projects` to discover all projects, then let the user confirm which ones to include.
32. **Never expose PAT tokens in chat or files** — PAT tokens should only be entered in browser modals and stored in localStorage. Never save them in code, memory files, or conversation. If a user shares one, remind them not to.
33. **Arabic toggle must be planned from day one** — Every PM dashboard needs EN/AR toggle. Adding it after the fact requires touching every text element. Plan for `data-i18n` attributes from the start to avoid rework.
34. **"Last updated" means fetch time, not current time** — Dashboard timestamps should show when data was last retrieved from the API, not a live clock. A live clock gives false confidence that data is current when it may be hours old.
35. **Show full project names, not abbreviations** — In dashboards for CEO/Board, use full project names (e.g., "KhairGate (BMS)") instead of abbreviations (e.g., "BMS"). Abbreviations are internal shorthand that external stakeholders won't recognize.
293. **Azure DevOps API does not store job titles** — The user list API (`az devops user list/show`) only returns display name, email, and license type. Job titles must come from the user, HR system, or Microsoft Entra ID. Don't assume you can pull titles from DevOps.

## Session Workflow & Git Hygiene (Mar 16, 2026)

36. **Always pull before analyzing** — Never analyze or report on data without pulling latest from remote first. Stale local data leads to wrong conclusions (e.g., reporting 9-month plan when v2 already shows 4 months).
37. **URL.txt is the session start ritual** — For repos with a URL.txt, always run it first: fetch → compare → pull if needed → open HTML. This prevents working on outdated files.
38. **Check git diff after pull, not just commit message** — Commit messages like "design modifications" are vague. Always check `git diff --stat` to know exactly which files changed and how much, so you can assess impact.
39. **Version files may have internal inconsistencies** — A v2 file may still contain v1 data in some sections (e.g., team member durations showing 9 months in a 4-month plan). Flag these to the user rather than silently assuming one is correct.
278. **Remove project-specific context from standalone assessment files unless PM explicitly wants it** — When creating assessment HTML for email, candidates need technical requirements — not PM governance context (project names, phase numbers, sprint references, hiring gaps). Only include project context if the PM explicitly confirms it belongs in the candidate-facing file.
306. **Always run git pull first, not just git log** — `git log` shows fetched commits but doesn't update the working tree. Today's session checked `git log` (saw 4 commits) but didn't pull, leaving local 2 commits behind. The drift fix was applied correctly but the repo was stale. Always `git fetch` + `git pull` as the first action.

## Data Analysis & Presentation (Mar 16, 2026)

40. **Derive what's not documented** — If a backlog has story points but no scale definition, reverse-engineer it from the data. Count distribution, group by SP value, read actual stories at each level, then present the derived scale for validation.
41. **Show the math, not just the result** — When presenting person-months, show: Allocation % × Duration = Person-Months for each member. Stakeholders trust calculations they can verify.
42. **Compare before and after** — When two versions of a plan exist (v1 vs v2), always present a variance table. Decision-makers need to see what changed and by how much (e.g., -56% duration, -66% effort).
43. **Define jargon when asked** — Terms like "person-month", "story point", "sprint velocity" are not obvious to all stakeholders. When the user asks "what does X mean?", give a concrete example from their own project data, not a textbook definition.

## Memory & Lessons Discipline (Mar 17, 2026)

44. **Save lessons at end of every session** — Don't wait for the user to ask. At the end of each working session, proactively capture lessons learned into `global_lessons.md` and project-specific memory files.
45. **Memory files are for future conversations, not current tasks** — Don't save ephemeral task details (current debug state, temp file paths). Save things that help future-you understand the project: team composition, estimation methodology, key decisions, file relationships.
46. **Update session log after every pull** — The session log in MEMORY.md is the project timeline. Record: date, commit range, new/updated files, key actions taken. This helps trace when changes were introduced.

## Consolidation & Multi-Source Architecture (Mar 24, 2026)

47. **Never modify source files in consolidation projects** — Keep all source analyses untouched (e.g., S1: 468 files, S2: 21 files). Build an independent consolidated output (HUB v3.0). This preserves original analysis integrity for audit trails.
48. **Track conflicts explicitly with IDs and side-by-side display** — Number conflicts (CF-01 to CF-12) and show both positions without auto-resolution. Let governance decide. Transparent conflict management builds stakeholder trust.
49. **Color-coded source attribution on every data point** — Use consistent color tags (S1=blue, S2=orange, Merged=purple, New=green) so readers can trace any claim to its origin. Critical for multi-source projects.
50. **3-tier gap classification prevents analysis paralysis** — Classify gaps as Blocking / Important / Deferrable. Simple 3-tier system distinguishes urgency without overcomplicating prioritization.

## Portal & Pipeline Architecture (Mar 24, 2026)

51. **Zero-dependency static HTML is the most durable deliverable** — No build system, no bundler, no server. Works offline, on any machine, forever. Essential for board presentations and long-term archival.
52. **Modular CSS from day one saves rework** — Separate CSS files (variables, base, layout, nav, components, RTL, print) allow targeted changes without breaking unrelated pages. Invest in CSS architecture early.
53. **Numbered scripts enforce execution order** — Name pipeline scripts 01→22 to make execution order self-documenting. Anyone can run them in sequence without reading a README.
54. **Large JSON intermediates enable independent downstream scripts** — Files like `db_objects.json` (20MB) are large but let any downstream script work without re-extracting from source. The storage cost is worth the decoupling.
55. **FOLDER_OBJECTIVE_MAP.html serves as the project atlas** — A visual map of all folders and their purposes is essential when projects exceed 10 subfolders. New team members can orient themselves immediately.
56. **Recommendations stay as recommendations until governance approves** — Never auto-apply analysis recommendations to source files. Present findings; let the board decide. Technical teams provide analysis; governance decides.

## Dashboard Maintenance & Adoption (Mar 24, 2026)

57. **Maintenance burden kills dashboards** — If a dashboard requires > 30 manual inputs per week for one person, it won't be used. v1 had ~113 manual inputs and was never populated. v2 cut to ~24 by auto-fetching everything possible from DevOps APIs. Design rule: if an API can provide it, never ask the PM to type it.
58. **KRs must be outcomes, not activities** — "Weekly agendas with DevOps metrics pre-populated" is a task, not a key result. "Decision turnaround time < 3 days" is a measurable outcome. Before finalizing OKRs, test each KR: "Could someone else observe and verify this was achieved?" If not, rewrite it.
59. **PMs need their own scorecard for CEO visibility** — Team KPIs (sprint velocity, bug rate) measure the team, not the PM. Add a dedicated PM Scorecard with metrics the CEO evaluates the PM on: decision turnaround time, scope change control, meeting compliance, stakeholder satisfaction pulse. Without this, the PM is invisible in their own dashboard.
60. **localStorage snapshots enable trend tracking without a backend** — Save a weekly snapshot object into a history array on every "Save" click. Use inline SVG sparklines (no Chart.js dependency) to show week-over-week progress. Cap at 13 weeks (~500KB). Add Export/Import JSON for backup. This gives trend visibility with zero infrastructure.

## Enhanced Standalone Auto-Updater Rules (Mar 24, 2026)

61. **Surface updates are NOT enough** — After every Hub V2 pull, audit internal content (Document Control tables, assumptions, risk descriptions, internal text references), not just headers/footers/KPIs. V5-V10 missed this completely.
62. **Each pull with drift = new standalone folder** — Never edit existing version in place. Copy folder, increment version, apply fixes. Each version is a frozen snapshot.
63. **Create own reports, never copy Hub V2's** — PM creates own variance/story reports based on previous template. Hub V2 reports are Mohamed's internal documents with different branding.
64. **Hub V2 pages don't belong in standalone** — Only PM-created artifacts go in the standalone folder. Hub V2 pages (health-dashboard, etc.) are linked via nav, not copied.
65. **Document Control tables must match folder version** — Version field, Updated date, Data Source (with commit hash) must be current in every file after every update.
66. **Footer must show specific date** — "Modified: DD Mon YYYY" not generic "March 2026". Every file, every version.
67. **Phase 0 vs full program must be distinguished** — Budget, Timeline, Hiring show full 22-month program but kickoff is Phase 0 only. Both must be shown.
68. **Old reports don't belong in kickoff package** — Only latest variance + story variance versions. Remove historical snapshots (v10, v11, v1, v2).
69. **BA Review is a loop, not one-shot** — Mahmoud reviews → Mohamed fixes → Mahmoud verifies. Loop until no critical issues. Track pass number.
70. **Read lessons BEFORE executing** — At start of every session, read feedback files before touching any code. Prevents repeating past mistakes.
71. **Never assume git author = current user** — Check git log. MahmoudEl-Afify ≠ Mahmoud Elshahed.
72. **Validate Hub V2 claims against actual data** — Don't trust page counts, story counts, or status claims. Count actual files. Read actual KPI strips.
73. **Interactive buttons must sync via same localStorage key** — All files using status buttons must share `kg-action-progress` key. Labels must match across files (3 states for ADRs).
74. **Resolved sections should be collapsed by default** — `<details>` without `open` for ALL RESOLVED / CLOSED sections. Focus on pending items.

## OKR Dashboard v2 Lessons (Mar 25, 2026)

75. **Tab-based navigation beats sidebar for dashboards under 10 views** — v1 used 12 sidebar items; v2 consolidated to 7 tabs. Fewer navigation choices = faster cognitive load for executives. Only use sidebar when you have 10+ sections or need persistent visibility of all options.
76. **Bilingual i18n must use data attributes, not duplicate HTML** — v2 uses `data-i18n` keys with a JS translation map (~223 keys). Duplicating entire pages for EN/AR doubles maintenance and guarantees drift. Plan for `data-i18n` from the start so every text element is toggle-ready.
77. **Print CSS must show all tabs, not just the active one** — Dashboard print styles that hide inactive tabs produce blank pages. Use `display: block !important` on all `.tab-content`, add `page-break-before: always` between sections, and inject section titles via `content: attr(data-tab-title)` pseudo-elements. Always test Ctrl+P after adding print styles.
78. **Metric cards should be clickable drill-downs, not dead-end numbers** — Showing "To Do: 12" is useless without knowing who owns what. Every aggregate metric card should open a per-member or per-project breakdown modal on click (`showTeamDetailModal`). This was the key UX improvement in Team Pulse v2 — all 6 teams, 4 states each, 24 clickable drill-downs.
79. **Declare cached variables BEFORE loadData() runs** — JS `var` hoisting hoists the declaration but not the `= {}` initializer. If `var cache = {}` appears after `loadData()`, the initializer overwrites whatever `loadData()` restored from localStorage. Always declare cache variables before any load function that populates them.
80. **Never duplicate team members across teams** — Double-counting inflates metrics silently and makes drill-downs misleading. One person = one team. If they work across teams, pick their primary. OKR v2 had 4 members in both Mobile and Qatar — split them to eliminate overlap.
81. **WIQL returns work item IDs for free — don't discard them** — `data.workItems` from Azure DevOps contains `[{id, url}]`. The original `runWIQL` threw away IDs and returned only `.length`. `runWIQLWithIds` preserves them at zero extra API cost. Cache IDs in localStorage so drill-down links survive page reloads.
82. **Dashboards need a built-in user guide** — A PM won't use a dashboard they don't understand. Embed a step-by-step weekly workflow, a timeline with deadlines, and an auto-vs-manual field reference directly in the dashboard. Don't rely on external documentation — the guide must live inside the HTML.
83. **Always open HTML after edits — no exceptions** — Never leave the user to open files manually. Run `Start-Process` or equivalent as the final step after every HTML edit session. This was explicitly called out as a failure and saved as a feedback memory.

## Auto-Updater & UX Redesign Lessons (Mar 25, 2026)

84. **PowerShell em-dash crash** — Unicode characters (em-dash, curly quotes) in PowerShell string literals cause silent parse failures. The window closes instantly with no error. Always use ASCII alternatives (`--` not `—`, straight quotes not curly).
85. **Always provide .bat launcher for PS scripts** — "Right-click → Run with PowerShell" closes the window on any error. Create a `.bat` file with `powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -NoExit -File script.ps1` so errors stay visible.
86. **Disable update buttons when no drift** — "Full Update" when already up-to-date is misleading and risky. Separate into 3 buttons: Check (always available), Pull+Update (only when drift), Audit Only (always available). Grey out unavailable actions.
87. **Automate recurring failures** — If a check is missed 3+ times across sessions, build a tool. The auto-updater script exists because manual drift fixes repeatedly missed Document Control tables, assumptions, and internal content. Human checklists fail; scripts don't forget.
88. **3 page types define nav UX** — Action pages (green, interactive buttons for decisions), Reference pages (blue, read-only for board review), Audit pages (purple, health history snapshots). Organize navigation by type, not by arbitrary categories. Directors need to know: "where do I click?" vs "where do I read?"
89. **Merge overlapping pages** — Two pages covering the same topic (KICKOFF_READINESS_GUIDE + KICKOFF_READINESS_VALIDATION) confuse the Director. One source of truth per topic. If you create a summary page, merge it into the original rather than adding another file.

## Cross-File Consistency & Audit Lessons (Mar 25, 2026)

90. **Deleting a file breaks all relative links to it** — When we deleted root-level HUB_PULL_HISTORY.html, 14 V11 sidebar nav links (`../HUB_PULL_HISTORY.html`) broke silently. Always grep for all references BEFORE deleting any file, and fix links in the same commit.
91. **Don't duplicate files across locations** — Having HUB_PULL_HISTORY at root AND inside V11 caused staleness (root was March 18, V11 was current). One copy, one location. If other files need it, link to it — don't copy it.
92. **Location-specific terms don't belong in hiring risks** — "Qatar market" implies on-site only. Hiring may be hybrid/remote. Use skill-based language instead: "Odoo 19 skill scarcity" not "Qatar market scarcity". Applies to all R-08 references across PM_RISK_REGISTER, ADR_APPROVAL_REQUEST, and any hiring-related text.
93. **Health dashboard verdict must reference cards by name** — A flat list of 7 pending items is unreadable. Group pending items under the card they belong to (e.g., "POC Readiness V.2 — ADR-1, Budget, Odoo hiring"). Board can then click the right card directly.
94. **Visual badges (ACTION NEEDED / CLEAR) save board reading time** — 7 cards with no visual priority = Board reads all 7. Amber badge + green badge = Board reads only the 3 that matter. Always add status badges when cards have mixed states.
95. **Track review loops explicitly** — The BA review between El-Afify and Mohamed had no visible status on the dashboard. Pass 2 was pending but nowhere documented. Add "Review Loop Status" tracking boxes to both the health dashboard card and the readiness page.
96. **Purpose tips in headers orient the reader** — Every page header should have a one-line description of what this page does. Board members open 15+ files — a tip below the title saves them from scrolling to understand context. Use `.summary` div pattern.
97. **Auto-updater covers KPIs but not consistency** — The PS1 server catches number drift (stories, SP) but misses text mismatches ("Qatar market" in 2 files), broken nav links, file count disagreements, and version tag staleness. Build a 6-check audit: KPIs + text + nav + file count + status sync + version tags.
98. **Sprint-plan-phase0 and phase0-poc-readiness are a pair, not duplicates** — Sprint plan = developer execution guide (Mohamed's, 2,196 lines). POC Readiness = PM status dashboard (ours, 227 lines). Different audiences, same data. Keep both but verify data consistency between them.
99. **Session handoff must include tracking file updates** — HUB_PULL_HISTORY, FOLDER_OBJECTIVE_MAP, and MEMORY.md must be updated at end of every session. Save a "FIRST TASKS Next Session" section in memory if context runs out.
203. **Supporting documents drift silently during version bumps** — Approval emails, cover letters, and non-artifact HTML files are not included in version-bump agent scope. KICKOFF_APPROVAL_EMAIL had V5 data (commit 2f321a3, March 9) while all 13 PM artifacts were at V13 (March 30). Stale numbers (18 vs 19 risks, 21 vs 28 actions, 200+ vs 937 stories) went unnoticed for 4 versions. Include ALL HTML files in audit-check scope.
204. **KPI smart cards must match source file buttons and KPI strips** — ADR_APPROVAL_REQUEST showed "1 Approved / 2 Pending" in KPI cards while all 3 ADR buttons were already "Approved." Manual review missed this; only the audit-check agent caught it. Smart card values are high-visibility — board sees them first — so mismatches create credibility risk.
205. **Internal summary text drifts independently from KPI cards in the same file** — PROJECT_STARTUP_CHECKLIST summary said "21 actions / 3 phases" while its own KPI cards correctly showed "28 / 4." PM_RISK_REGISTER meta said "18 Risks" while its KPI card showed "19." Always audit both the KPI strip AND the subtitle/meta/section headers — they are updated by different processes and diverge silently.
206. **Never expose internal PM governance file names on Hub V2 pages** — V13 Standalone file names (PM_RACI_MATRIX.html, HIRING_TEAM_PLAN.html) were added to phase0-poc-readiness.html on Hub V2. The team sees Hub V2 only; V13 references leak authority structure and create confusion about which files are canonical. Always use Hub V2 paths on Hub V2 pages.
207. **Hub V2 technical files created by engineering are NOT PM governance files** — sprint-plan-phase0 Key Contacts table (4 people) was treated as the project team structure. It's only a Phase 0 quick-reference. Hub V2 raci-matrix.html and hiring-team-plan.html are NOT approved by IT PM. Always confirm approval status before treating any file as authoritative for roles and responsibilities.
208. **Discrepancy reports need owner assignments + clickable source file links** — Review summary of sprint-plan-phase0 found 9 issues but initially had no owner or file references. Adding "Responsible: Mohamed Basha" + hyperlinked source files made it actionable. Without these, findings sit unresolved because nobody knows who should fix what or where.
209. **AI-generated placeholder names in Hub V2 files must be caught and flagged** — pbi-validation-phase0.html listed "Dr. Sarah Pemberton (BA)" as reviewer — a fabricated name. The actual reviewer is Mahmoud Afifiy (El-Afify). Placeholder names erode credibility if they reach board review and can cause confusion in formal audit trails.
210. **Separate internal evaluator content into local-only files before pushing** — Reference solutions (code answers) and interview questions (with expected answers) must NOT be in files pushed to shared repos. Candidates can see anything in the repo. Extract to V13-only files (e.g., ASSESSMENT_REFERENCE_SOLUTION_INTERNAL.html) and replace with locked placeholder in Hub V2 version.
211. **When moving files between folders, grep for ALL relative path references** — Moving team-assessment-phase0.html from Hub V2 to Team Assessment folder broke 8 CSS paths, 4 JS paths, and 3 cross-file links silently. Page rendered unstyled. Always grep for the filename across ALL files and fix paths in the same step as the move.
219. **After a drift fix, re-fetch origin before pushing** — Hub V2 moves fast. The E19 fix took hours, during which E2 expanded (+1 story, +3 SP), making V14 stale before it was even pushed. Always re-check for new commits after completing a drift fix.
220. **version-bump agent misses body-text prose with version numbers** — It handles version-tag spans, headers, footers, but NOT inline text like "all 5 operational docs complete in v13.0". Always grep for the old version in body text after version-bump completes and fix manually.
221. **Review summary fixes must be on SOURCE pages, not the tracking dashboard** — Basha marked C1-C5/H1-H4/D1-D3 resolved on phase0-poc-readiness (our PM dashboard) but sprint-plan-phase0 and pbi-validation-phase0 (source files) were not updated. Dashboard fixes are cosmetic — source files remain broken.
307. **Check all cross-tab dependencies before adding content to one tab** — Adding .gitignore, screen recording, and AI policy to Tab 1 (Assessment) required updates in Tab 3 (Scorecard scoring rows), Tab 4 (Deep Review anti-patterns), Tab 5 (Interview AI policy note), and Overview (description). Editing one tab without mapping dependencies first means follow-up edits that could be missed.

## HR & Recruitment (Mar 31, 2026)

212. **Always check the email thread for ALL active JDs before evaluating any candidate** — A single recruitment folder ("Senior Odoo Developer") contained TWO separate active positions: (1) Senior Odoo Developer and (2) Sr. Backend Engineer — Django & Python. First evaluation scored Amr Deep against the Odoo JD (15% match); the correct Django JD yielded 35-40%. Cost: entire interview form had to be rewritten. Always read the .msg files to identify ALL open positions before building any evaluation.
213. **One recruitment folder can contain multiple positions with separate criteria** — Don't assume one folder = one role. The Basha email added a second position (Django & Python) alongside the original Odoo role. Both are active simultaneously with different JDs, different criteria, and different candidate pipelines. Always ask which position a candidate is being evaluated for.
214. **Color-code interview forms by role type for visual distinction** — Odoo interviews use purple (#714B67), Django/Python interviews use blue (#1a5276). When multiple role types share a recruitment folder, visual distinction prevents confusion during panel reviews.
215. **"Revised" or "new JD" doesn't always mean replacing the old one** — When an email says "refine position JD not to be Odoo BE developer, instead it should be Sr. Backend", it may mean adding a second position, not killing the first. Both the Odoo and Django roles were active simultaneously. Always confirm: is this a replacement or an addition? Assuming replacement when it's an addition means you stop evaluating valid candidates for the original role.
216. **Match each candidate to their target position at intake, not during evaluation** — When a new CV arrives, the first question should be "which of our open positions does this candidate map to?" before any scoring begins. Amr Deep's CV was initially scored against the wrong JD because the position mapping wasn't established upfront. Establish position → candidate mapping before building any evaluation artifact.

## Git, Branching & Deployment Lessons (Mar 26, 2026)

100. **`git rm -rf .` deletes actual files, not just tracking** — When creating orphan branches inside the working folder, `git rm -rf .` permanently deleted V12 files. All session changes had to be re-applied from scratch. Always work from a DIFFERENT directory when creating orphan branches, or use `git worktree`.
101. **PowerShell arrays of hashtables don't serialize with ConvertTo-Json** — `@() += @{...}` pattern produces empty JSON arrays. Use `[System.Collections.ArrayList]::new()` with `.Add()` instead. Also increase `-Depth` to 10+ for nested structures.
102. **Wrong Hub V2 path = wrong KPIs** — `KhairGate-Hub-Analysis` (V1, stale) vs `KhairGate-Hub-Analysis-V2` (current). The auto-updater pointed to V1 for months without anyone noticing. Always verify the exact folder name when configuring paths to data sources.
103. **Greedy regex causes false positives in audits** — `Financial Readiness.*100%` matched "To reach 100%" text instead of the actual 80% value on the same line. Fix: use tight patterns that match specific HTML structure (e.g., `progress-label` div value), not greedy wildcards across the whole line.
104. **Multilingual regex needs wider context windows** — Arabic "العودة" (fallback) was 40+ chars before "Odoo 18" but the 20-char lookback missed it. Expand context windows to 80+ chars when checking multilingual content for contextual exclusions.
105. **Historical files must be excluded from audit checks** — HUB_PULL_HISTORY, variance reports, and ADR contain intentional references to old tech (Camunda, Odoo 18, USD). They document history, not current state. Audit rules that flag "stale content" must have an exclusion list for historical/audit files.
106. **Hub V2 i18n uses data-i18n attributes + inline JS dictionary** — Not JSON files. ~550+ terms in a JS object (`var T = {...}`) inside i18n.js. `toggleLang()` swaps `textContent` via `querySelectorAll('[data-i18n]')`. New pages need both the data-i18n attributes AND the dictionary entries to translate.
107. **Push ALL repos after changes, not just the one you're focused on** — Hub V2 changes (Arabic translations) were committed but not pushed until the user asked. When a session touches multiple repos, push all of them before ending.
108. **Orphan branches preserve version snapshots** — Each Enhanced Standalone version (v1-v12) as its own orphan branch with descriptive commit messages preserves the full evolution history. Use `git checkout --orphan v{N}` pattern.
109. **Auto-update lessons from audit findings with dedup** — Build the pipeline to write back to global_lessons.md automatically. Check for existing keywords before appending to avoid duplicates. Stale lesson files are worse than no lesson files.
217. **Create git branch in the SAME step as pushing a new standalone version** — V14 was pushed to main but v13/v14 branches were not created until the next day. Branches are the team's way to access frozen versions. Always: push commit → create branch → push branch — one atomic step.
218. **Archive the old version folder in the SAME step as creating the new one** — V13 folder couldn't be moved to archive for 2 days because .git was locked by VS Code. If deferred, file locks accumulate. Always: create V14 → move V13 to archive → verify — before any other work.
254. **Never assume repo state — verify with actual commands** — When asked "do we need to pull?", don't rely on environment metadata saying "not a git repo". Read reference files (URL.txt), check actual folders, run git status. The Taqat folder had 10 cloned repos inside it despite the root not being a repo.

## Dashboard & KPI Design Lessons (Mar 26, 2026)

110. **Separate work item types for accurate metrics** — Grouping Bug + Enhancement under "Task" hides defect trends. Each type needs its own WIQL query, KPI card, and column in drill-down tables. Without separation, you can't answer "how many bugs are open?" from the dashboard.
111. **Auto-calculate derived KPIs — don't let users manually set what can be computed** — Overall health = worst-of(Schedule, Quality, Scope) + blocker penalty. Manual "Overall" toggles drift from reality. Formula: min(S,Q,Scope), downgrade if blockers > 0, force Red if blockers > 3.
112. **Merge tiered tables into one unified view** — Tier 1 (full G/A/R) + Tier 2 (active items only) confused readers. One table, same columns, all 11 projects. Tiers are for prioritization logic, not UI separation.
113. **Column tooltips explain scoring criteria to non-technical readers** — Board members don't know what "Quality = Amber" means. Add `title` attributes: "Consider bug count, rework rate, QA pass rate." Every subjective column needs a tooltip.
114. **localStorage migration between dashboard versions** — v2→v3 key change (`okr-kpi-q2-2026-v2` → `-v3`) requires auto-migration logic on load. Users lose data without it. Always check for old key, transform data shape, write to new key, delete old.
294. **Cost scenario cards must show the phased ramp, not just the final number** — "$1,704/year" means nothing without showing it ramps $70→$106→$142/month across 3 batches. Management needs to understand cash flow timing per batch, not just the annual total.

## Document Consolidation Lessons (Mar 26, 2026)

115. **Identify the primary source before merging** — When 3 files cover the same meeting, one is always the most comprehensive. Use it as the skeleton; others are supplements for gaps only. Don't merge line-by-line — that creates duplicates.
116. **Email-ready HTML beats DOCX for meeting distribution** — Inline CSS, no attachment issues, renders in every mail client. For MoM/agenda distribution, HTML is the final format. DOCX is the input, not the output.
295. **Implementation roadmaps must evolve with the execution plan** — A 6-phase single-batch roadmap (Feb) became stale when execution shifted to 3-batch phased rollout (Apr). Both the summary tab and the recommendation tab must stay in sync — if one changes structure, the other must follow.

## Modal, Audit & i18n Lessons (Mar 26, 2026)

117. **Modal popups beat inline results for audit output** — Inline `<div id="results">` pushes page content down and gets lost on scroll. Modal overlay with backdrop = focused attention, dismissible, no layout shift. Include summary bar (pass/fail counts) + individual check cards.
118. **Remove vendor names from architecture documents** — "Camunda" → "external BPM tool". Docs survive tool changes without rewrites. Historical files (ADR, variance reports) keep vendor names as they document decisions made at that time. Only current-state docs use generic terms.
119. **Title consistency is a cross-file audit check** — "Project Manager" vs "IT Project Manager" appeared in 6+ files across V12. Title mismatches erode credibility with board readers. Add title verification to the audit engine. Exclude external stakeholder entries (e.g., Semir's role stays as-is).
120. **Batch i18n: attributes + dictionary in one commit** — Adding `data-i18n` attributes to HTML without updating the JS dictionary (`var T = {...}`) means keys resolve to raw key names. Always add both in the same commit. For Hub V2, that's 9 HTML files + i18n.js = one atomic commit.

## OKR & KPI Dashboard v3 Lessons (Mar 26, 2026)

121. **Numbers must reconcile across dashboard views** — If Executive Summary shows 26 Bugs total, Team Pulse must also total 26. Add an "Unassigned" card to catch work items with no owner. Formula: Project total = Sum(all teams) + Unassigned. If they don't match, users lose trust in the dashboard.
122. **Consistent drill-down pattern across all metrics** — Every clickable number must follow the same flow: Card number → Breakdown table → Work Items popup (with DevOps links) → Back button. Don't skip levels for Bugs/Enhancements while keeping them for To Do/In Progress. Inconsistency confuses users.
123. **Modal max-height prevents off-screen Close button** — A project with 18+ work items pushes the Close button below the viewport. Always set `max-height: 85vh; overflow: auto` on modal content. The Close and Back buttons must always be reachable.
124. **Don't add redundant summary strips** — A live data strip in the header repeating KPI card values adds clutter, not clarity. Use header space for report purpose/benefits instead. Data lives in KPI cards; identity lives in the header.
125. **G/A/R toggles need deselect capability** — Click to select, click again to deselect back to "none". Without this, once you set a value you can never clear it. Check `el.classList.contains('selected')` before toggling.
126. **Separate work item types in WIQL queries** — Mixing Task+Bug in `IN ('Task', 'Bug')` hides bug counts. Use separate queries for Bug-only and Enhancement-only counts. This enables auto-populating KR2.1 (production bugs) and gives PM visibility into defect vs improvement work.
127. **Persist in-memory data to localStorage** — `lastProjectData` was only in JS memory — lost on page reload, breaking detail modals ("Click Fetch first"). Save runtime data structures to localStorage in `saveData()` and restore in `loadData()` if the UI depends on them.
128. **Use the Blocked field, not tags** — Azure DevOps has a dedicated `Microsoft.VSTS.CMMI.Blocked` field (Yes/No). Don't rely on `[System.Tags] CONTAINS 'Blocked'` which requires manual tagging. The field is built into the work item form.
129. **Move action buttons to sidebar footer** — Fixed bottom bars (Save/Export/Clear) waste 50px of vertical space on every page. Sidebar footer is always visible and doesn't consume main content area. Also move Fetch/PAT buttons there.
130. **Two-column header: purpose left, identity right** — Title + subtitle + benefits on the left; name + org + quarter + meta tags stacked right-aligned on the right. No wasted horizontal space. AR toggle goes top-right.
131. **Always add tooltips and legends for Board audience** — G/A/R buttons, column headers (Schedule/Quality/Scope), KPI cards — every element needs hover `title` explanation. Board members won't guess what "Quality = Amber" means. Add a visible legend box above the table.
132. **Spell out acronyms in the title** — "OKR & KPI" means nothing to a CEO. Show "Objectives & Key Results / Key Performance Indicators" beside the short title. The abbreviated form is for navigation (sidebar), the full form is for comprehension (header).
133. **Use correct job titles consistently** — "IT Project Manager" not "PM" or "Project Manager". Check every occurrence: header, print summary, PM Scorecard title, meta tags. Title mismatches erode credibility in Board-facing reports.

## Email Analysis & Weekly Reporting Lessons (Mar 26, 2026)

134. **Verify topic categorization by participants, not keywords** — "Terms and Conditions (English)" was placed under Relief Center based on keyword match. Checking participants (Dr. Bahaa, Hacene, Syed) immediately revealed it belongs to BMS/KhairGate. Always check the participant list in topic_summary.csv before assigning new topics to project categories.
135. **Email volume ≠ project status — never set badges from data alone** — A project with 12 new emails might be on track; a project with zero emails might be critically blocked. Status badges (URGENT, ACTIVE, MEDIUM, etc.) must come from the PM's manual input, not automated email counts. Email data provides trends and counts; human judgment provides status.
136. **Checkboxes without persistence are decoration** — HTML checkboxes that reset on page refresh provide no tracking value. Either add localStorage persistence or accept that status tracking is manual. For weekly snapshot reports, manual Thursday review is sufficient — don't over-engineer interactivity.
137. **Embed the update process in the deliverable itself** — If a report has a recurring update workflow, document the process steps inside the report (e.g., info card on the Dashboard tab). Anyone opening the report should understand how it gets updated without needing external documentation.

## File Management & Deletion Safety Lessons (Mar 27, 2026)

138. **Never use bash `rm` on Windows — always use Recycle Bin** — Bash `rm` permanently deletes files with no recovery. Always use PowerShell safe delete: `[Microsoft.VisualBasic.FileIO.FileSystem]::DeleteFile('path', 'OnlyErrorDialogs', 'SendToRecycleBin')`. This applies to all user files, no exceptions. Lost 3 Skilljar HTML source files (~4 MB total) because `rm` was used instead of Recycle Bin.
139. **Always confirm which specific files to delete before any deletion** — When asked to "delete files not needed," never assume which files the user means. List all files with numbers and ask the user to pick. Assumption led to deleting original source files instead of generated ones.
140. **Source/original files are sacred — never delete originals** — Downloaded files, user-created content, and original sources may be irreplaceable. Only delete generated/derived files (and only after explicit confirmation). Generated files can always be recreated; originals cannot.
141. **Separate destructive actions from other work** — When a request includes both "delete X" and "do Y," handle the deletion as a standalone step with its own confirmation. Chaining "delete + open" led to rushing through deletion without proper verification.
222. **Archive old versions, keep only the latest in the root folder** — When versioned deliverables accumulate (v1–v12+), move older versions to a dedicated archive subfolder and keep only the current version in the working directory. This reduces clutter, prevents accidental edits to old versions, and makes the current deliverable immediately identifiable. Apply to all projects with iterative HTML/DOCX/MD deliverables.

## Course Dashboard & Content Extraction Lessons (Mar 27, 2026)

142. **Verify stats from the actual source HTML, not assumptions** — Course landing pages have exact lecture/video/quiz counts in `<span class="clp__stat-value">` tags. Claude Code in Action was 15 lectures (not 91 as assumed). Always read the HTML stats before hardcoding numbers. Wrong numbers propagated across 14 dashboard versions before being caught.
143. **Benefit cards must contain actionable content, not marketing language** — "Master context management strategies" is useless. `/init`, `CLAUDE.md` (3 locations), `#`, `@filename`, `/compact`, `/clear` — these are what the user actually needs. Every bullet should be a command, technique, or decision rule they can apply immediately.
144. **Always auto-open HTML deliverables in browser after creation or update** — Users want to visually verify every change immediately. Don't wait for them to ask "open it." This applies to new versions AND edits to existing versions. Code edits can look correct but render wrong.
145. **Course URL pages are the source of truth for stats, downloaded files are for content extraction** — The Skilljar URL page shows correct lecture count, video hours, and quiz count. The downloaded lecture HTMLs in subfolders are for extracting "What You'll Actually Use" content and "Lecture Breakdown" detail cards. Don't conflate the two sources.
146. **Track completion status accurately — "In progress" if any lecture is downloaded** — If even one lecture file exists for a course, the status is "In progress," not "Not started." Status should reflect actual evidence from the file system, not assumptions about user activity.
147. **All lessons in a completed course must show "Saved" tag** — When a course is marked Completed with a certificate, every lesson in that course's detail card must have the "Saved" badge, even if the individual lecture HTML wasn't downloaded as a separate file. The user completed the course = they consumed all content. Never leave the last lesson without a "Saved" tag in a completed course.
148. **Never trust sidebar stats in saved HTML pages** — Skilljar pages render OTHER courses' stats in JS sidebars. Claude Code in Action showed "15 lectures" which was actually a different course's stats. The real count was 21. MCP showed 16 but was actually 14. Always verify from the actual Skilljar URL page or user-provided screenshots.
149. **YouTube playlists are the source for video durations, not Skilljar sidebar data** — Subagents course showed "0.4h" (~24 min) from Skilljar sidebar, but the actual YouTube playlist totaled 15 min (4:44 + 3:42 + 2:49 + 3:45). Always check the video source directly.
150. **"Estimated time" on Skilljar = reading time, not video duration** — Each lecture page shows two separate values: "Estimated time: 20 minutes" (total reading time) and "(4 minutes)" below the heading (video duration only). Don't confuse or merge them.
151. **Keep the Recommended Learning Path section in sync with course status** — When a course is marked Completed or In Progress in the Course Comparison table, the Learning Path cards must be updated too (green border + "COMPLETED" or blue border + "IN PROGRESS"). This was missed for Course 2 and Course 3 initially.
152. **Use full course names everywhere in the dashboard** — Tab names, Topic Coverage Matrix headers, Course Comparison headers must all show the full official course name. Abbreviated names like "Claude API" or "MCP" cause confusion. All course name headers should also be hyperlinks to the official Skilljar URL.
153. **Record certificate earned date/time in 3 places** — When user reports a certificate: (1) Learning Path card with timestamp, (2) Certificate row in Course Comparison, (3) Status row in Course Comparison. Use the time the user reported it as the timestamp.
154. **Every recurring scan must complete the full update checklist** — Scan → compare → if changes: create version → update folder_snapshot.md → update CLAUDE.md → open browser. Skipping any step (especially snapshot and CLAUDE.md) creates drift that compounds across versions.
155. **Dashboard versions go in a subfolder, latest copy in root** — Store all versions in `Claude_Courses_Dashboard/` subfolder for history. Keep only the latest version copy in the root folder. When creating a new version: create in subfolder → copy to root → recycle previous root copy.
156. **Add external resource links (YouTube playlists, docs) to benefit cards** — When a course has associated YouTube playlists or reference URLs, add them as clickable links at the bottom of the "What You'll Actually Use" benefit card. Users need quick access to these resources.
157. **Hide redundant tracking rows from comparison tables** — If download/progress status is already visible in each course tab (Saved tags + lecture breakdown count), don't duplicate it in the Course Comparison table. Redundant information clutters the overview.
158. **Use Playwright for dynamic web data, not WebFetch** — Platforms like Skilljar hide duration/quiz stats behind dynamic rendering. WebFetch returns empty static HTML. Playwright sees the actual rendered page with all stats (lectures, hours, quizzes). Go straight to Playwright for any learning platform data extraction.
159. **Keep team columns alongside role columns in recommendation matrices** — Teams (Qatar, Mobile, Flutter) represent real organizational units with specific people who need personalized course guidance. Don't remove them — each team member needs to see their column to know which courses to take. Map team recommendations based on the actual roles of team members.
160. **Transpose wide tables — items as rows, not columns** — Any table with 10+ columns causes horizontal scroll and is hard to read. Flip it: put items (courses, projects, etc.) as rows and attributes/roles as columns. A 15-row × 8-column table is far cleaner than an 8-row × 15-column table.
161. **Never leave placeholder dashes when real data exists** — If a course page shows "1.1 hours of video" and "2 quizzes," the catalog must show those numbers, not "—". Leaving placeholders makes deliverables look incomplete and undermines credibility. Always scrape ALL available metadata before publishing.
162. **Sort comparison tables by difficulty level, not enrollment status** — Foundation → Beginner → Intermediate → Advanced with section headers. This shows the natural progression path at a glance. Grouping by "enrolled vs not enrolled" is self-centered; grouping by level is useful for everyone reading the catalog.
163. **Scan on demand, not auto-timer** — User prefers manual "scan" command over 15-minute auto-loop (/loop). Auto-scanning creates noise when the user is actively downloading lectures and wants control over when updates happen. Cancel the cron and wait for explicit "scan" instructions.
164. **Screenshots contain knowledge for benefit cards, not file tracking** — When a user downloads screenshots from course videos, READ the image content and add useful information to the "What You'll Actually Use" section. Do NOT track screenshots as per-lecture download badges — the user doesn't care about the file, only the knowledge in it. Only track actual downloadable resources (notebooks, zips, PDFs) per lecture.
165. **Quiz questions and answers are actionable knowledge — add to benefit cards** — Every quiz question + correct answer is a tested, practical rule that belongs in the "What You'll Actually Use" section. They're not just test results — they're the distilled, exam-worthy knowledge from the course. Include them as "Quiz Insights" in the benefit card.
166. **Scan before quiz, not after** — Always update the dashboard with new lecture downloads BEFORE the user takes a quiz. The user wants to see their progress current before starting the assessment. Don't wait for quiz completion to scan.
167. **In-place dashboard updates are OK during active learning sessions** — When rapidly iterating (downloading lectures, taking quizzes back-to-back), updating the same version (e.g., v31) throughout the day is fine and preferred over creating v32, v33, v34 for each small change. Create new version numbers only for structural changes.
168. **Track ALL downloadable file types, not just notebooks** — Scan for .ipynb, .zip, .pdf, .csv, .md, .py, .txt — any file the user downloaded separately as a course resource. Add them as blue badges (+1 notebook, +1 zip) on the relevant lecture with a "Download:" line showing the filename. Use code-tag styling for filenames.
169. **Course completion requires a full update checklist** — When a course is completed with certificate: (1) Course header → Completed + certificate timestamp, (2) Learning Path card → green border + COMPLETED, (3) Course Comparison table → status + certificate rows, (4) Page 2 catalog → status updated, (5) Stats bar → lectures saved count, (6) CLAUDE.md → version entry updated. Missing any step creates inconsistency.
170. **Deduplicate multi-language course listings** — When a course is offered in 10 languages, show it as ONE row with a "Languages" column listing all variants, not 10 separate rows. Keeps tables readable and prevents false inflation of course counts. Note the primary/English version link.
171. **Complement technical courses with PM-perspective courses** — Technical courses (Anthropic: API, tools, MCP) + PM courses (PMI: governance, ethics, adoption) together create a complete skillset. When building course catalogs, include both perspectives and explain how they complement each other.

### March 29, 2026 — Audit Engine Fix Session (KhairGate V13)

172. **Verify both sides of a PS1↔HTML API contract** — When PowerShell returns `items` but HTML reads `check.details`, the data silently disappears. Always grep both the server response field names AND the client rendering field names before declaring an API "working." Cost: 183 false warnings displayed to user.
173. **Never pre-escape AND use [regex]::Escape()** — PowerShell's `[regex]::Escape()` already handles special chars. If the whitelist array contains `v13\.0` (pre-escaped) and then `Escape()` runs again, it produces `v13\\.0` which matches a literal backslash, not a dot. Use plain strings in the array: `@('v13.0')`.
174. **Case sensitivity across language boundaries** — PowerShell returns `"PASS"` (uppercase) but JavaScript checks `==='pass'` (lowercase). Cross-language APIs must normalize case. Add `.toLowerCase()` on the JS side or return lowercase from PS1.
175. **Health dashboard push requires FULL content sync, not just badges** — Workflow G rule: when pushing to Hub V2 after a drift fix, update ALL health-dashboard content (stories, SP, prerequisites, dates, cards) — not just the verdict line. Partial updates create inconsistency that erodes board trust. Cost: 3 follow-up commits to fix what should have been 1.
176. **Add automated date consistency to audit engine** — Document Control "Updated" dates drifted 4 days behind footer "Modified" dates across 7 files without anyone noticing. Automate: extract footer date, compare with DocControl date, flag if DocControl is older. Added to Check 6 (Cross-File Data Consistency).
177. **SP/Story regex must use global matching with "any match" logic** — `-match` returns only the FIRST occurrence. Files with Phase 0 subset values (133 SP) before the total (3,882 SP) trigger false positives. Use `[regex]::Matches()` to find ALL occurrences, then flag only if NONE match the expected value. Also exclude files that legitimately contain only subset numbers.
178. **Status buttons must persist to HTML file, not just localStorage** — Board members opening the standalone file see DEFAULT states, not the PM's reviewed states. Solution: updater server `/api/save-status` endpoint writes `st-X` class + button text directly to the HTML file. Graceful degradation: if server not running, localStorage still works. Arabic text must be extracted from the file's own `data-labels-ar` attribute (not the JSON payload) to avoid encoding corruption.
179. **Bilingual span count must exclude CSS style blocks** — Audit Check 3 (bilingual balance) was falsely flagging 7 files with 10-27% AR > EN gap. Root cause: CSS selectors like `[lang-ar]{display:none}` and `body.ar div[lang-ar]` contain ~14 extra `lang-ar` references per file for the bilingual toggle. Fix: strip `<style>` blocks before counting. After stripping, all files are under 2.5% gap.
180. **Readiness progress cards must match status button states** — KICKOFF_READINESS_GUIDE showed "Technical Readiness 0%, 2 ADRs pending" while ADR buttons were all `st-1` (Approved). Stale readiness cards contradict the button states and mislead the Board. Added to audit-check agent Check 7: verify readiness card text matches button states.
181. **Hub V2 sync needs a dedicated subagent** — Manually syncing health-dashboard + phase0-poc-readiness after every drift fix caused 7 follow-up commits in one session (ADR-2 missing, prerequisites wrong, reports count wrong, R-08 not updated, variance reports stale). Created `hub-sync` agent to automate: reads V13 state, updates both Hub V2 pages + copies variance reports, returns diff summary.
182. **Browser caches PS1 server responses** — After updating the PS1 audit engine (6→11 checks), the Audit Only button still showed 6 checks even after server restart. Root cause: browser cached the old HTML page. Fix: Ctrl+Shift+R (hard refresh) after every server restart. Consider adding cache-busting headers to the PS1 HTTP response.

## Team Roster & Data Deduplication Lessons (Mar 30, 2026)

184. **One row per person in team rosters — combine groups into a single column** — When a team member belongs to multiple groups (e.g., Qatar Team + Mobile Team + Flutter), list them once with all groups in a comma-separated "Groups" column. Repeated rows inflate counts, cause confusion, and make updates error-prone. If you change a name, you'd have to find every duplicate row. Cost: DevOps_Teams.md had 15 rows reduced to 9 after deduplication.
260. **Always cross-reference team roles across ALL sources before answering** — The same person can have different titles in V15 RACI, Hub V2 pages, and DevOps Teams file. Example: El-Afify = "Odoo Consultant" (V15) vs "Business Analyst" (Hub V2) vs "Implementation" (DevOps Teams). Always read project_team_roster.md first — never answer from a single source. Cost: Multiple incorrect role attributions across sessions before full cross-reference was built.
261. **Maintain a team alias map for name fragments** — Team members are referenced by different name fragments across project files. Key aliases: "Basha"/"Salah"/"Mohamed Basha" = Mohamed Salah Basha (Tech Lead/EM), "El-Afify"/"Afifiy"/"Mahmoud Afify" = Mahmoud El-Afify, "Hatem"/"xhatem" = Hatem Abdelaziz, "Jassem" = Jassim Mohamed. Without the map, commit attribution and role questions produce wrong answers.
276. **Map every point in a team member's commit against PM artifacts before responding** — When reviewing a commit that proposes changes (like Hatem's Proposed Plan with 21 points), map each point against the original PM documents. Missing even one conflict (like Scenario C: BLOCKED vs Foundation proceeds) weakens the PM response and lets scope overreach go unchallenged.

## Localhost Server Management (Mar 30, 2026)

185. **Assign unique ports per project server and maintain a port map** — Multiple localhost PowerShell servers across projects (Outlook, KhairGate, Source-Root) can silently collide if they share the same port. Since many servers expose `/api/status`, an HTML dashboard will "connect" to whichever server grabbed the port first, then fail on project-specific endpoints. Maintain a port map (5050=Outlook, 5051=KhairGate, 5052=Scan, 5053+ next). Before creating a new server, grep all `.ps1` files for `$Port =` to verify no conflicts. Cost: Scan button returned "Unknown endpoint" because KhairGate server was on the same port.

## Executive Reports & One-Pagers (Mar 30, 2026)

188. **Pull from ALL data sources upfront when building executive reports** — Check OKR Dashboard, Outlook analysis, HR folder, DevOps audit, Source-Root index, Monthly Reports, and project-specific folders (e.g., Enhanced Standalone for HUB). Missing a single source means missing context. Cost: 4 additional sources (Source-Root, DevOps, HR, Pearl Pixels Quarterly Report) were missed in the first draft and had to be added iteratively.
189. **Two questions = two visually separate answer blocks** — When a director asks two questions in one request, structure the document with distinct labeled sections (Q1, Q2) and a visual divider between them. Don't blend answers into a single narrative — each question deserves its own clearly labeled block so the reader finds the answer immediately.
190. **Never duplicate data across sections in the same document** — Before finalizing, audit every section — each fact should appear in exactly one place. OKR targets stay in OKR section only. Portfolio items stay in portfolio table. Team development items covered by OKRs don't need their own section. Duplicates waste space and look unprofessional on a one-pager.
191. **Director-facing documents show only pending and planned, not completed** — Filter every item: if it's done, exclude it. The director wants a forward-looking view to understand what's coming, not a retrospective of what happened. Completed work belongs in quarterly reports, not in Q2 plans.
192. **"Hiring and assignment" — not just "hiring"** — When discussing resource needs, always consider both new hires AND reassignment of existing team members. The user corrected "Pending hiring" to "Pending hiring and assignment" for HUB project because some roles may be filled by moving existing staff.
193. **Verify position titles and candidate names with the user** — Don't assume role titles from earlier data. User corrected "Sr. Odoo Developer" to "Sr. Backend — Django & Python" and added Moqbel Elseaedy alongside Yousef Ashraf. Always confirm exact titles and full candidate lists.
194. **Handover completion doesn't mean process compliance** — When told "handover is done", the real issue may shift to process discipline. Hacene's handover was completed, but the actual problem was pushing to production and creating features without team alignment. Ask: "What's the current issue?" not just "Is it done?"
195. **OKR sections for directors need full KR numbers and measurable targets** — Compressed OKR summaries ("RC launch, BMS Sprint 5") lose the measurable specifics directors need. Show all 12 KRs with KR numbers (KR1.1, KR2.3) and exact targets (< 5 bugs, 80%, By Jun 30). The dashboard already has this data — don't compress it away.
196. **Check who's NOT on a platform — the gap matters more than who IS** — When user said "DevOps is mandatory", the key insight was naming the 5 specific people NOT on DevOps (Hossam, Muram, Walied, Ajay, Hacene). Listing the gap by name creates accountability. Don't just say "some members need onboarding."
197. **Risk levels must be confirmed with the user, not assumed** — I set Taqat Property to "Low" risk because docs were ready, but user corrected to "Medium." Risk perception depends on business context the PM understands better. Always default to user's assessment.
198. **8 iterations is normal for director-facing documents — plan for it** — The Q2 PM plan went through v1→v8 with corrections at each step. Each version fixed real issues (wrong status, missing projects, duplicated data, missing OKR details). Don't try to get it perfect in one shot — iterate, but track what changed in each version.
199. **Always save lessons to global_lessons.md, not just memory files** — Memory files are project-scoped and conversation-scoped. Global lessons persist across all projects and all future sessions. If a lesson applies broadly (report building, stakeholder communication, process discipline), it belongs in global_lessons.md.

## Cross-Project Dashboard & Query Copy (Mar 30, 2026)

200. **Use az CLI Bearer auth instead of PAT tokens for DevOps automation scripts** — PAT tokens expire, require manual rotation, and are easy to leak. `az account get-access-token --resource 499b84ac-1321-427f-aa17-267ca6975798` gives a short-lived Bearer token using existing login. Add a `-UseAzCli` switch to scripts so both auth methods are supported. Cost: Every script run previously required pasting a PAT; now it's zero-friction with az CLI.
201. **Copy queries BEFORE dashboard widgets — widgets depend on query IDs** — Dashboard chart widgets reference query GUIDs in their settings. If you copy widgets first, they'll point to non-existent queries and show errors. Always: (1) copy/create queries, (2) capture new query IDs, (3) replace source query IDs in widget settings, (4) then create widgets. Cost: Widgets with wrong query IDs show "Last time you checked, there were no results" permanently.
202. **For targeted widget additions, use direct az REST calls instead of the full copy script** — The full `Query-and-Dashboard-Copy.ps1` copies ALL queries and ALL widgets. When you only need 3 specific charts (e.g., adding frontend team to an existing dashboard), direct `az rest` POST calls are faster, more precise, and don't risk duplicating existing content. Use the script for new project setup; use direct API for incremental additions.
204. **Dashboard API URL requires project-level path for project-scoped dashboards** — Team-scoped dashboards use `/{project}/{team}/_apis/dashboard/dashboards/{id}` but project-scoped dashboards only need `/{project}/_apis/dashboard/dashboards/{id}` (no team segment). If a dashboard returns 404, try removing the team context from the URL before assuming it was deleted. When replicating widgets across dashboards in the same project, create team-specific queries first (update the group filter in WIQL), then update all embedded query IDs in widget settings (`groupKey`, `filter`, `queryId`).

## OKR & KPI Dashboards (Apr 1, 2026)

223. **Never fabricate project-specific data in OKR/KPI dashboards** — Sprint numbers, deadlines, milestone names, and scope definitions must come from the user or a verified source (Azure DevOps, project documents, emails). If the information wasn't explicitly provided, ask — don't invent it. Cost: BMS "Sprint 5" with a "June 15" deadline was assumed and placed as KR1.2; neither the sprint number nor the date was verified, requiring correction after delivery.
224. **Show all active projects by default, then recommend — not the other way around** — When building OKRs or dashboards, first list ALL projects with their real DevOps data (iterations, dates, status). Then recommend which ones are critical. Never cherry-pick projects based on assumptions about which are "biggest" or "most active." The user decides the scope, not Claude. Cost: O1 originally had only 3 KRs (RC, BMS, Aqraboon+Wallet) when 8 projects had active Q2 sprints — 5 projects were invisible.
225. **Never merge unrelated projects into a single KR** — Each project has its own sprints, teams, and timelines. Combining "Aqraboon + Wallet" into one KR because they seemed "secondary" hid the fact that they have completely different cadences (Aqraboon Sprint 1 ends Jun 2, Wallet Sprint 6 ends Apr 23). One KR = one project = one measurable milestone.
226. **Always fetch iteration dates from Azure DevOps `_settings/work` before setting KR deadlines** — The source of truth for sprint start/end dates is `https://dev.azure.com/TaqaTechno/{ProjectName}/_settings/work`. Never assume sprint numbers or dates. Fetch first, then build the dashboard.
310. **Adding a new team to OKR dashboard requires 7 atomic updates** — When adding a new team (not just a member), update all 7 locations together: (1) version tag, (2) stats bar team/member counts, (3) CSS header gradient class, (4) HTML team card with full metrics grid + expandable member table, (5) teamDefs array, (6) memberDefs array, (7) teamInfoMap. Missing any one silently breaks drill-downs or WIQL queries. Always create a new version file — never edit in place.

## Executive Summary Reports (Apr 1, 2026)

227. **Trust DevOps closure dates — never second-guess or flag as "carried over"** — The `Custom.ClosureDate` field is the single source of truth for which month a work item belongs to. If the closure date says March, it's March — even if the item appeared in an earlier report. Do NOT cross-reference against previous report contents or create special "Carried Over / Re-dated" categories. Categorize items by their work type only. Cost: Flagged 4 items as "re-dated" and proposed a "Carried Over" category, delaying the March 2026 report and requiring user correction.

## HTML Dashboard Search (Apr 1, 2026)

228. **Search boxes must use multi-word AND matching, not exact phrase matching** — When a user types "Executive Summary Aya 2026", they expect results where ALL words appear anywhere in the content — not as one contiguous substring. Using `text.includes(fullQuery)` fails when words are scattered across different parts of the same card or section. Always split input into words and check each independently: `words.every(w => text.includes(w))`. Cost: Claude Sessions Index search returned zero results for "Executive Summary for Aya Allah Nady Shapan for 2026" even though the data was present in the same card — the words just weren't adjacent.
229. **HTML dashboards that summarize CSV data lose searchability — bridge the gap** — When an HTML report shows curated summaries but the raw data lives in CSV files, any detail not manually written into the HTML becomes invisible to search. Solution: add a "Deep Search" mode that queries the CSV files via a local server endpoint (e.g., localhost:5050/api/search). This keeps the HTML lightweight while making all raw data searchable on demand. Cost: 4 email records about Aya Allah Nady Shapan existed in cat_other_projects.csv but were completely unfindable from the Outlook v12 HTML search panel.
255. **Search results should replace content, not stack on top** — When a dashboard has many sections, showing search results while keeping all other content visible creates noise. Enter key should hide everything (stats, diagrams, tabs) and show only results. Escape or clicking a result restores the normal view. Implemented via a CSS class toggle on the container.

## OKR & KPI Trend Charts (Apr 1, 2026)

230. **Trend charts must render with 1+ data points, not 2+** — A single-week baseline is still valuable data. Use dots for 1 point, lines for 2+. Never gate chart rendering behind a minimum week count without asking the user. Cost: User clicked Save and saw empty charts with misleading placeholder text.
231. **Add data tables below every SVG chart** — SVG dots/lines are hard to read precisely. A small HTML table below each chart with exact values per week makes the data board-ready and accessible when chart labels overlap.
232. **Heatmap cells need text labels (G/A/R), not just colors** — Color-only heatmaps are ambiguous (yellow could mean "at risk" or "no data"). Always include text inside cells and use gray (#e8e8e8) for "no data" to distinguish from amber/yellow status.

## KhairGate Drift Fix & Autonomous Execution (Apr 1, 2026)

233. **When user says "yes do it" — execute autonomously** — Don't ask "should I do it before or after X?" Follow the workflow rules, decide the approach, execute, and present the result for approval. Asking at every fork wastes time and frustrates the user.
234. **Re-fetch origin AFTER completing a drift fix, BEFORE pushing** — Hub V2 moves faster than drift fixes. V14 was built for E19 (947/3,928) but E2 arrived during the fix (+1/+3), making V14 stale before it was even pushed. Required immediate V15.
235. **If version-bump subagent fails, run sed commands directly** — The version-bump agent for V15 failed because it couldn't execute Bash. Don't relaunch — just run the sed replacements manually. Wasted time on a failed agent is worse than 30 seconds of direct sed.
262. **"No drift" Hub V2 pulls still need a HUB_PULL_HISTORY entry** — Even when Hub V2 commits don't affect standalone PM artifacts, document every commit in HUB_PULL_HISTORY.html with a "NO DRIFT" badge. Every pull must be tracked regardless of impact level. This maintains audit completeness and prevents gaps in the commit trail.

## Portfolio Health Scoring Methodology (Apr 1, 2026)

236. **Always cite framework references before recommending a scoring approach** — When recommending a methodology (e.g., worst-case vs. weighted scoring for RAG aggregation), verify which PM standards actually endorse it before presenting it as a recommendation. PRINCE2 and SAFe endorse worst-case; PMI Portfolio Standard endorses weighted scoring; PMBOK prescribes nothing. Never recommend an approach and attribute it to a standard without verification. Cost: Initially recommended "Average + Floor Rule" as the best approach with no standard backing — had to retract and re-research when challenged for references.
237. **When changing a calculation formula, audit every consumer of its output** — Changing the Portfolio Health Overall formula from worst-case to PMI Weighted Scoring caused the badge text to change from `'On Track'` to `'On Track (2.65)'`. Every JavaScript comparison using exact-match (`=== 'On Track'`) broke silently — Projects On Track counter, weekly snapshot saves, and PM Scorecard roll-ups all failed. Always grep for every reference to the output value and update comparisons (e.g., use `indexOf` instead of `===`). Cost: 3 broken counters across Executive Summary, PM Scorecard, and Save/History that would have shown 0/11 projects on track despite all being green.
238. **Bilingual dashboards must translate tooltips (title attributes), not just text content** — The `data-i18n` translation system only handles `textContent` via `el.textContent = T[key]`. HTML `title` attributes (hover tooltips) are invisible to this system and remain English-only when switching to Arabic.
