Solution: add a parallel `data-tip-i18n` attribute system and extend `applyLang()` to swap `title` attributes, storing originals in `data-tip-original`. Cost: 20 tooltips across Executive Summary, Work Item Flow, PM Highlights, and Portfolio Health headers stayed in English while all surrounding text was Arabic — inconsistent bilingual experience for CEO presentation.

## PM Framework Coverage Gaps (Apr 1, 2026)

239. **PM²-Agile covers individual project reporting, not portfolio-level health aggregation** — The PM²-Agile course materials (7 sessions, 33 templates) provide Status Report templates (#19, #20) and Development Status Reports but do not prescribe how to aggregate multiple RAG dimensions (Schedule/Quality/Scope) into an Overall score, or how to roll up health across a portfolio. Don't assume a PM course covers every PM topic — verify before citing it as a reference.
240. **PMBOK does not prescribe a specific RAG aggregation formula** — PMBOK 6th Ed Section 4.5.3 (Work Performance Reports) describes traffic-light reporting but explicitly leaves the aggregation methodology to "organizational process assets." When a stakeholder asks "what standard backs this?", PMBOK is not a valid answer for the specific calculation — only for the concept of using RAG status. Cite PRINCE2 Ch.12 for worst-case or PMI Portfolio Standard for weighted scoring instead.

## Quarterly Report Build & Data Pipeline (Apr 2, 2026)

241. **Read the actual template file before replacing values** — The Q2 Performance Report template had v7 KPI values (3,934.51) not v11 values (4,341.61). The replacement script searched for wrong numbers and left stale data in the KPIs. Always read the target file's actual values before writing a replacement script.
242. **Replace entire HTML blocks, never individual numbers** — Replacing "932" globally hit 9 unrelated locations (bar charts, commit counts, table cells). Use regex to match the full containing element (e.g., the entire KPI grid div) and replace it as a block. Bare number replacement is never safe in HTML.
243. **Regenerate every data section in one pass, not incrementally** — Replacing tables but missing trend bars, chart.js arrays, employee cards, and insight cards produces a broken hybrid of Q1 and Q2 data. Build a single script that regenerates all data-dependent sections from the JSON source in one execution.
244. **Check the data before asking the user** — Questions like "Is Water for Mosques paused?" and "Is IndoQat real?" are answerable from the query data. Don't ask the user to validate what the data already shows. Only ask about decisions (team structure, merge rules) not facts.
245. **Verify team structure from the latest authoritative source before building** — Q2 has 3 teams (Egypt/Qatar/Mobile per OKR/KPI v5), not the Q1 structure of 2 teams. Building reports with wrong team grouping required a full rebuild. Always check the current team definitions from the OKR/KPI dashboard or DevOps_Teams.md before starting.
246. **Get answers before generating — not after** — Generating reports with DRAFT placeholders, wrong team structure, and stale content, then asking for review wastes time on both sides. Collect all inputs (team roster, project status, achievements) before running the build script.
247. **Use MCP tools as PAT fallback, not just PowerShell** — When the PAT expired mid-session, MCP tools (`wit_get_query_results_by_id` + `wit_get_work_items_batch_by_ids`) successfully fetched all 366 items. Always have two data paths: PowerShell script (fast, bulk) and MCP tools (auth-independent, always available).
248. **Never show a report to the user without self-validation first** — If the KPI says 3,934 but the JSON sums to 1,386, that's the builder's bug to catch. Run a validation check (KPI values vs JSON aggregates, no Q1 remnants, correct employee count) before opening the report.
249. **Pre-build checklist is mandatory before any quarterly report** — Before generating: (1) read OKR/KPI for current team roster, (2) check who left/joined from task data, (3) confirm new projects from query, (4) read actual template values, (5) confirm team structure with user. Skipping this caused 1.5 hours of rework in Q2 build.
250. **Patching HTML is always slower than full generation** — Incremental find-replace across a 500K HTML file caused 5 rebuild cycles (wrong KPIs, orphan elements, stale bars, stale cards). A single full-generation script that builds all sections from JSON in one pass eliminates this class of errors entirely.
251. **When user says "okay" or "sure", execute immediately** — "Okay build it" means build it now, not "I designed the concept, want me to build it?" Asking for re-confirmation after the user already approved wastes time. This happened twice in the Q2 session (skill creation and refresh explanation).
252. **Read all 3 PM reports before working on any of them** — Monthly Performance, OKR/KPI Dashboard, and Quarter Plan share interconnected PM insights (risks ↔ KRs ↔ data). Working on one without reading the others produces disconnected insights. Always glob for latest version from each folder — never hardcode version numbers.
253. **ClosureDate is PM action, not developer completion** — PM closes items every Wednesday/Thursday. Developer completion = state changed to "Done" (from revision history). All time metrics (lead time, cycle time) must use the Done date from revisions, NOT ClosureDate/ClosedDate.
254. **Fetch form fields, not query columns** — Query columns can be hidden or changed by the user at any time. Always request fields directly in the API call. This makes the data pipeline independent of query display configuration.
255. **Exclude training/meeting items from time metrics** — Items with predetermined durations (training, courses, meetings, onboarding, KT sessions) have perfect estimation ratios by definition. Including them inflates accuracy scores and skews lead time averages. Filter by title keywords.
256. **Sprint length and iteration settings are per-project in Azure DevOps** — Don't assume 2-week sprints globally. Check `https://dev.azure.com/TaqaTechno/{PROJECT_NAME}/_settings/work` per project (or use MCP `work_get_team_settings`/`work_list_iterations`). Sprint definitions affect velocity calculations, carry-over detection, and completion rate thresholds.
257. **Don't duplicate content across sections in the same report** — If Challenges & Lessons and Key Insights both exist, merge into one section. Creating both caused confusion about where metrics-driven insights belong. One section per purpose, no overlap. In the CEO report, Key Insights is the single home for all PM observations.
258. **Tooltips need bilingual support in every HTML deliverable** — The HTML `title` attribute doesn't switch with `switchLang()`. Use `data-en-title` and `data-ar-title` pairs, and add tooltip switching logic to `switchLang()`. Applies to every bilingual HTML report with tooltips and EN/AR toggle.

## Tool & Environment (Apr 2, 2026)

256. **Paths with special characters break Windows shell open commands** — The `&` character in folder names (e.g., "Taqat Trading & Business Solutions") gets escaped as `^&` causing "file not found" errors in cmd/PowerShell/bash. Always copy files to a clean path (e.g., D:\tmp) before opening programmatically. This failed 3 times before the workaround was established.

## Translation & i18n (Apr 5, 2026)

259. **Hub V2 i18n uses textContent, not innerHTML** — The `applyLang()` function replaces `el.textContent`, so nested HTML tags (bold, links, code) get stripped when toggling to Arabic. For elements with mixed HTML, either put `data-i18n` on text-only spans to preserve formatting, or accept plain-text Arabic. Always check how the i18n system works before adding translations to a new page.

## Claude Automation (Apr 5, 2026)

263. **Subagents for large-scale HTML edits plan instead of executing** — When delegating bulk translation or multi-section HTML modifications to background agents, they consistently produce plans rather than making edits. After 4 failed agent launches in one session, the work had to be done directly. For large HTML modification tasks (100+ elements), do the work directly in the main conversation instead of delegating.
279. **Always verify i18n.js syntax with `node -c` after adding translation entries** — A single missing comma breaks the entire translation dictionary and no Arabic toggle works on any page. Run `node -c path/to/i18n.js` after every edit. Cost: missing comma on line 918 broke all Arabic translations across both assessment and POC readiness pages until caught by syntax check.

## Version Archive & History Preservation (Apr 5, 2026)

264. **Never delete old standalone versions — always MOVE to the archive folder** — When user says "remove" or "delete" a version, MOVE it to `Enhanced standalone version/` archive folder, never `rm -rf`. All versions are pushed to GitHub repo (`melshahedpearl/KhairGate-HUB--Kickoff`) for history. Deleting locally loses the local copy even though git has it. Cost: V14 was permanently deleted, had to be recovered from GitHub. The archive folder preserves V1-V14+ for local reference.

## File Organization & Versioning Hygiene (Apr 6, 2026)

265. **Only the latest version stays in the root folder — archive previous versions immediately** — When creating a new Outlook report version (or any versioned HTML deliverable), move the previous version into the archive subfolder (e.g., `Outlook Email Analysis Report/`) before or right after generating the new one. Only the current/latest version should live in the root working directory. Prevents clutter and confusion about which version is active. Cost: v12 was left in root alongside v13, breaking the v1-v11 archival pattern.
287. **Each document version gets its own Created date — no history chain needed** — Each versioned file (v1, v2, v3, v4) is a standalone snapshot with its own "Created" timestamp. Don't add version history tables tracking all previous versions' dates — the file system and CLAUDE.md already track that. Keep the document clean.
298. **Remove departing employees before provisioning, not after** — Eslam Hafez was leaving in 1 week. If all 17 users had been provisioned at once, a $6/month license would have been wasted. Phased batching naturally catches this — always verify the roster against HR status before each batch.
300. **Dashboard cards with version numbers go stale silently** — The Access Upgrade Summary card pointed to v2 while v5 existed. No one notices until they click and see outdated content. Every time a new version is created, update all referencing dashboards in the same session.

## Phased Rollout & License Management (Apr 7, 2026)

299. **Phased rollout splitting factors are dependency-based, not cost-based** — Cost is the same $142/month regardless of batching. The real factors for splitting: who blocks others (dependency), who trains others (role), who is senior vs junior (seniority), does each team have coverage (team balance), and risk reduction (validate licensing channel with small group first).
302. **Users on different platforms don't need the same tool license** — Walid uses GitHub for task tracking, not Azure DevOps. Don't assume every team member needs the same license. Ask about their actual workflow before including them in an upgrade plan.
303. **Running totals cascade on every single user change** — Removing 1 user from Batch 2 changed: user count, Basic count, monthly cost, annual cost, all 3 scenario cards, all batch running totals, and all 4 modals. One person = 20+ edits across the file. Always use a systematic checklist.

## Dashboard Data Integrity (Apr 6, 2026)

266. **Never assume project status — always pull from the actual source** — Assumptions about Relief Center being "Behind" and BMS being "At Risk" were wrong. Real OKR v5 Portfolio Health data showed both ON TRACK (3.00). Always read the source dashboard before assigning status labels. Cost: had to redo all 12 project badges after user caught the mismatch.
267. **Verify sprint counts from DevOps API, don't estimate** — Initial count said "6 active sprints" but querying all 27 projects with `work_list_team_iterations` revealed **13** had current sprints (timeFrame=1). The first query only checked 6 projects. Cost: presented wrong number to PM before meeting.
268. **One project, one name — check for duplicates before displaying** — "Taqat Property" and "Property Management" are the SAME project (DevOps name vs display name). Dashboard showed both as separate entries with different badges. Always verify project name mapping against DevOps project list before creating cards.
295. **Data gaps are more damaging than bad numbers** — Tasks without Estimated Time or Actual Time trigger direct management questions. Management doesn't look for perfect numbers — they look for understandable, complete numbers.

## Leadership Presentation Design (Apr 6, 2026)

269. **Keep leadership dashboards to one page with expandable cards** — Removing 15+ project tabs and using expandable cards on a single dashboard page was the right call. During a meeting, clicking through tabs is slow and loses the audience. Click-to-expand keeps context visible while allowing drill-down.
270. **Align status badges across all dashboards shown in the same meeting** — If OKR Dashboard v5 says "G — On Track (3.00)", the presentation dashboard must say exactly the same. Using different labels (e.g., "HIGH RISK" vs "On Track") for the same project creates confusion and undermines credibility with management.
271. **Understand WHY you clone a UI template before building** — v12 Outlook Email Analysis Report was chosen for its sidebar+cards+search pattern. Understanding the pattern's strengths (navigation, search, badges, expandable sections) led to better adaptation for the meeting dashboard than blindly copying HTML.
292. **Management evaluates the presenter, not just the presentation** — Semir wasn't just reviewing dashboards — he was assessing whether Mahmoud controls the numbers or just displays them. Every management meeting is a dual evaluation (content + competence).
293. **Use measurement language, never justification language in management presentations** — "Deviation" / "time gap" / "corrective plan" vs. "people" / "circumstances" / "pressure." The words you choose signal whether you're in control or making excuses.
294. **The golden rule for management impression: numbers + understanding + plan** — A beautiful dashboard means nothing. Management wants a manager who controls the numbers, understands the deviations, and has a corrective plan.
482. **Executive portfolio reviews are 15-minute high-level only — drill-downs stay as backup tabs, never lead** — On 2026-04-20 Semir set the style rule for every weekly Project Management Review: ~15 minutes maximum, focus on projects status / cost / schedule / teams / high-level indicators (SPI / CPI, red/amber/green), pattern "Where we are / What's next / Key risks." Deep technical drill-downs are banned during the live walkthrough — they reduce audience engagement and signal operator-mode instead of owner-mode. Paired governance rule: every red/amber indicator must trigger a documented action, not just a display. **Why:** Senior management needs concise, action-oriented signal. Comprehensive reporting shifts the frame from "manager who controls the numbers" (Lesson #294) to "operator who displays the dashboard" (Lesson #292). An exhaustive 45-slide walkthrough actively destroys the impression you are trying to build, regardless of content quality. **How to apply:** (1) When refreshing exec-review decks and talking points, structure for a 15-minute read — cut, don't add. (2) Default the live dashboard walkthrough to Executive Summary + Portfolio Status only; keep SBI / Team Pulse / Data Gate / Insights as expand-on-ask backup. (3) Open with the literal pattern "Where we are / What's next / Key risks (cost, schedule, resources)." (4) Every amber/red indicator must link to an action with owner + reason + follow-up plan. (5) If a future meeting invitation asks for a longer format, treat that as an exception and confirm explicitly rather than assuming. (6) Applies to all executive audiences (board, CEO, sponsor steering) — not PMO-specific. Pair with Lessons #269-271 (one-page dashboards) and #292-294 (management evaluation + golden rule).

## Chrome & Local File Access (Apr 6, 2026)

272. **Chrome blocks file:/// links even from other file:/// pages** — `window.open('file:///')`, `<a href="file:///" target="_blank">`, and `--allow-file-access-from-files` flag all fail in Chrome. Workarounds: (1) programmatic `<a>` element click, (2) `window.location.href` for same-tab navigation, (3) localhost HTTP server (`python -m http.server`). Edge handles file:/// links natively.
273. **Delete large commented-out HTML blocks — don't rely on HTML comments** — Old project tabs inside `<!-- -->` leaked visible text because the content contained `-->` in DevOps URL attributes, breaking the comment boundary. HTML comments cannot be nested. Always delete unused code entirely rather than commenting it out.

## Azure DevOps Dashboard Architecture (Apr 6, 2026)

274. **Azure DevOps dashboards exist at two levels — project and team** — Each of the 12 projects has an "Overview" dashboard (project-level, visible at `/{Project}/_dashboards/`). Additionally, Pearl Pixels has 8 team-level dashboards (Backend, Frontend, Implementation, QA-QC, Dashboard, Details, Monthly Report, Project Changed Date) visible at the dashboards directory. Present both levels separately in governance reviews.
275. **Always cross-reference new dashboards with existing deliverables** — The meeting dashboard must be consistent with OKR v5 (Portfolio Health scores), Q2 PM Plan (milestones/risks), and Performance Report (metrics). If badges, numbers, or project names conflict across dashboards shown in the same meeting, management loses trust. Cost: 3 correction cycles during dashboard build.

## Meeting Documentation & Extraction (Apr 7, 2026)

276. **Meeting chat extraction alone captures zero value when all discussion is verbal** — Teams meeting chat was completely empty; 100% of decisions and actions came from the transcript. Always request transcript extraction alongside chat highlights, or you risk missing all meeting outcomes.
277. **AI-generated meeting notes need a "decisions vs. discussion" split** — The Meeting Result doc mixed 17 discussion points + 4 follow-up tasks in one stream. The Transcript extraction properly separated decisions only. Always extract decisions, actions, and discussion notes as separate outputs to avoid burying actionable items in narrative.
278. **Multiple AI extractions from the same meeting produce overlapping but non-identical outputs** — The Next Actions doc and the Follow-Up Tasks from the Meeting Result doc overlap (e.g., SPI/CPI tracking, bi-weekly reviews) but use different wording and granularity. Consolidate into one dashboard but preserve both sources without merging/deduplicating to avoid losing nuance.
279. **Post-meeting action items must distinguish "what to build" from "what to present"** — Actions like "prepare executive presentation" and "prepare for next presentation in 2 weeks" are presentation tasks; actions like "activate SPI/CPI indicators" and "prepare employee performance reports" are system/report-building tasks. Mixing them without categorization makes prioritization unclear.
290. **Always extract management signals separately from meeting decisions** — Decisions tell you "what was agreed"; signals tell you "what management really expects but didn't formally state." The impression analysis revealed the shift expectation from "Dashboard Operator → Owner of Control & Metrics" which wasn't in any formal decision list.
291. **A Before/During/After checklist should follow every management review meeting** — Preparing for the next presentation starts immediately after the current one ends. The 3-phase structure (data readiness → delivery → control) ensures no gap between meetings.
480. **MOM and Action Items rarely match 1:1 — always reconcile both sources into a single Action Tracker** — On 2026-04-21, parsing the Apr 20 weekly review yielded 5 action items in the MOM document and 3 in the Action Items document (transcript-based extraction). Items 1-3 overlapped; items 4-5 existed only in the MOM. Using only one source would have silently dropped 2 of 5 actions. **Why:** AI-generated MOM and Action Items come from different source pipelines (MOM = formal summary, Action Items = transcript scan) and apply different filters. Their union is the true action set. Trusting one source alone is a coverage-loss bug disguised as a completeness claim. **How to apply:** (1) Extract both documents to MD. (2) Enumerate action items from each independently. (3) Build a reconciliation table with columns MOM# / Action_Items# / unified# / overlap (both, MOM-only, AI-only). (4) Every unique row becomes one entry in the consolidated `Action_Tracker.md`. (5) Source column ("MOM Sec X + Action_Items Sec Y") documents the reconciliation for audit. (6) If counts disagree between sources, do not treat it as an error — treat it as expected and reconcile. Pair with Lessons #277 and #278 — they flag the multi-source problem; this one supplies the resolution pattern.

## Data Extraction & Automation (Apr 7, 2026)

280. **Python DOCX with Arabic content fails on cp1252 Windows console encoding** — Python's python-docx library reads UTF-8 fine but Windows console defaults to cp1252, crashing on Arabic characters. Always wrap sys.stdout with io.TextIOWrapper(sys.stdout.buffer, encoding='utf-8') before printing Arabic text from DOCX extraction scripts.
479. **A `.docx` is a ZIP — extract `word/document.xml` directly, no Word COM needed** — On 2026-04-21, parsing the Apr 20 MOM + Action Items `.docx` files used PowerShell `System.IO.Compression.ZipFile::OpenRead` + `[xml]` parse of `word/document.xml`, iterating `//w:p` elements and joining `//w:t` runs. Runs in <1 s per file, preserves Arabic UTF-8, zero Word dependency. **Why:** Word COM extraction requires Office installed, running, and licensed; it is slow (seconds per file), fragile (COM timeouts), and adds a binary-install prerequisite that kills portability. The underlying `.docx` ZIP structure is stable and documented — the direct-parse path has none of these failure modes. **How to apply:** For any `.docx` → text pipeline, use `System.IO.Compression.ZipFile` (PowerShell) or `zipfile` + `xml.etree` (Python). Wrap in a reusable script (`_extract_docx.ps1` pattern) and reuse it every meeting cycle. Handle `<w:tab/>` and `<w:br/>` as whitespace; join `<w:t>` runs per paragraph. Applies to `.xlsx` (`xl/sharedStrings.xml` + `xl/worksheets/sheetN.xml`), `.pptx` (`ppt/slides/slideN.xml`), and any Office Open XML format. Pair with Lesson #280 (Python + Arabic + Windows console cp1252 — same UTF-8 care needed).

## Dashboard & HTML Architecture (Apr 7, 2026)

281. **Meeting results HTML should be a separate file from the meeting preparation dashboard** — The pre-meeting dashboard (project status, team data, KPIs) serves a different purpose than post-meeting results (decisions, actions, follow-ups). Don't merge outcomes into the preparation file — create a dedicated results file to keep both artifacts clean and independently shareable.
288. **Self-contained deliverables over external references** — Embed the full recommendation as a second tab in the same HTML rather than referencing a separate PDF. One file = complete picture. The audience shouldn't need to open multiple files to understand the full context.
289. **Use clickable popups for drill-down data in dashboards** — Counts like "14 users" or "$130/month for 13 Basic + 1 Test Plans" should be clickable modals showing the full user list with names, emails, and roles. Keeps the main view clean while providing detail on demand without scrolling to another section.
296. **Collapsible sections (details/summary) beat side-by-side cards for detailed data** — 3 batch cards in columns were too cramped for tables with emails and roles. Collapsible rows give full-width tables that expand on demand. Use `<details open>` for the first item, collapsed for the rest.
297. **Popups must match the document's grouping logic** — When the document splits users by batch (not by team), all popups must also split by batch. Showing team-based grouping in popups while the main view uses batch-based grouping confuses the reader.
301. **CLAUDE.md version headers drift from the actual entries below them** — The header said "v3 is the latest" but v4 and v5 entries were already documented below it. Always update the header line when adding new version entries — don't just append.
313. **Scan buttons should auto-copy server CMD and auto-retry connection** — Requiring users to manually start a server, then click scan separately, creates unnecessary friction. Combine "Copy CMD" + "Wait for server" + "Auto-scan" into a single button click so the user only needs to paste in terminal.

## QC Task Management (Apr 8, 2026)

302. **KhairGate Wallet uses 2 QC templates per PBI — always create both** — When a PBI moves to "Ready For QC", create both [QC Test Execution] and [QC Bug Fixing] sub-tasks assigned to Mostafa Ahmed (mahmed@pearlpixels.com). Never create just one; the pair ensures testing and bug-fix tracking are separate work items from the start.
303. **Create QC Bug Fixing tasks proactively, not reactively** — The [QC Bug Fixing] task is created upfront alongside [QC Test Execution] when the PBI enters "Ready For QC", not after bugs are found. This gives the QC team a ready container for tracking fixes without waiting for task creation mid-cycle.
304. **Batch QC sub-task creation for multiple PBIs in the same state** — When several PBIs reach "Ready For QC" simultaneously, create all sub-tasks in parallel and link them to parents in a single batch call. For 4 PBIs × 2 templates = 8 tasks, this saves time vs. creating one-by-one.
305. **Always fetch project task templates before creating tasks — never assume template count** — Each project may have a different number of templates per role (e.g., Relief Center has 2 QC templates: [QC Test Execution] + [QC Bug Fixing], not one generic "QC" task). Always call the templates API first and confirm with the user which templates to use, rather than guessing the structure from role names alone.

## Team Onboarding (Apr 8, 2026)

306. **New team member onboarding requires three updates — memory, teams file, and Azure DevOps** — When adding a new member, update all three: (1) team roster memory for future conversation context, (2) DevOps_Teams.md as the authoritative team directory, and (3) Azure DevOps project team membership via security group API. Skipping any one creates drift between systems.
307. **Use security group membership API to add Azure DevOps team members — not `az devops team member add`** — The CLI does not have a direct `team member add` command. Instead, retrieve the team's security group descriptor via `az devops security group list`, then use `az devops security group membership add` with the group descriptor and the user's identity ID.
308. **Always confirm which Azure DevOps project when names are ambiguous** — "BMS Wallet" could mean KhairGate (BMS) or KhairGate Wallet — two separate projects. Never assume; ask the user to pick the correct one before making changes.

## Access Upgrade & Multi-Reference Document Updates (Apr 8, 2026)

310. **Adding one user to an access upgrade plan requires 20+ cross-reference updates** — A single user addition (Ayush Sakalley to Batch 1) required updating: batch counts, batch costs, running totals, 3 cost scenarios, next steps, implementation timeline, success criteria, 4 modals, user numbering across batches, Tab 2 team counts, appendix summary, action items, footer, and version header. Always enumerate every reference before starting edits to avoid partial updates.
311. **Always collect email address before creating a versioned document — never guess from naming patterns** — Email domains vary across the same organization (e.g., @Taqat.qa vs @taqatechno.com vs @pearlpixels.com). Guessing based on role or team leads to wrong entries that propagate across all modals and tables. Confirm the exact email first, then create the version.

## SLA & Service Agreement Creation (Apr 9, 2026)

314. **Extract all governance facts from source documents before drafting an SLA — never assume metrics, roles, or workflows** — The Relief Center SLA required reading 3 source documents (email + 2 MOMs) to extract 4 provider roles, 4 client roles, authorized requesters, and approval flows. 13 gaps were identified that needed explicit user input before any content could be written. Assumptions in contract documents create disputes later.
315. **When multiple meetings discuss the same topic, cross-reference all sources for contradictions before committing** — The Semir meeting focused on scope tracking and Shafik's bridge role; the Ola meeting defined authorized requesters and reporting lines. Both covered governance but from different angles. Merging without cross-checking would have missed that Ahmad Al-Jamal is the SPOC while Dr. Ola is the approver — two distinct roles confirmed in separate meetings.
316. **Always present multiple SLA metric options when no numbers were explicitly agreed — never commit to a single set** — The Relief Center meetings discussed service categories but zero response/resolution times. Presenting Option A (Aggressive) and Option B (Maximum Commitment) with a selection note lets both parties negotiate rather than the PM unilaterally committing to targets the team cannot meet.
317. **Logo positioning in bilingual HTML must use absolute positioning with RTL override, not centered layout** — A centered logo works for monolingual documents but looks unprofessional in bilingual SLAs. Use `position: absolute; left: 30px` for LTR and `html[dir="rtl"] { left: auto; right: 30px }` to flip the logo to the correct corner per language direction.
318. **UI elements with the same background color as their container become invisible — always use contrasting colors for fixed-position controls** — The EN/AR language toggle had `background: var(--primary)` matching the dark blue header, making it disappear visually. Fixed by switching to white background with dark text. Any fixed-position button overlaying a colored section needs explicit contrast.
319. **When a column has the same value in every row, lift it out into a banner above the table** — Relief Center SLA v6 had "Initial Response: 1 hour" repeated across all 5 priority rows. Moving it to a single info banner above the table reduced visual noise, freed column width, and made the unified commitment more prominent. Rule: a column constant across all rows is a header note, not a column. Applies to SLA tables, KPI dashboards, and status reports.
320. **"Logo" is ambiguous — confirm header logo vs browser favicon vs footer mark before building** — Spent a full round building an inline-SVG header logo when the user actually meant the browser-tab favicon. Three different artifacts (header brand mark, footer mark, favicon), three different sizes, three different file formats. Always ask which one before designing any graphic asset.
321. **Title prefixes (Eng./Dr./م.) must be applied in BOTH languages AND every occurrence — grep both Latin and Arabic name forms before declaring done** — "Eng. Semir Worku" needed updating in 4 places: Section 1 EN, Section 1 AR, Section 12 EN, Section 12 AR. The Arabic equivalent "م." is easy to miss because it's a different character set. When adding/changing any title prefix, search both name spellings before claiming the change is complete.
322. **Client-facing cover emails should avoid the word "support" / "الدعم" — frame the relationship as engagement or collaboration, not a help-desk transaction** — The Relief Center cover email originally read "post-sales support… during the support period". The client preferred a partnership tone, so all four occurrences (2 EN + 2 AR) were rewritten to "engagement period" / "فترة التعاون". Also avoid the substitute "post-sales services" — it still echoes the support framing. Always grep both languages (`support`, `الدعم`) and any euphemisms before delivering. Versioned as `Email_to_Client_SLA_Bilingual_v2.html`.

## Team Assessment & Hiring (Apr 12, 2026)

323. **Reduce follow-up assessment duration when prerequisites are passed and AI tools are permitted** — When candidates have already passed a prior level (e.g., Junior → Mid-Level), they've proven setup familiarity (project structure, Django CLI, models). Combined with AI-assisted coding, boilerplate (forms, views, URLs, auth) is fast. Cut Mid-Level from 2 working days to 5 hours. The real evaluation is the interview where candidates explain every line — duration should reflect coding complexity minus known skills, not a generous buffer.

## Source Attribution & PM Deliverable Rigor (Apr 12, 2026)

324. **Never invent a person's name — grep the repo or ask before putting it in a deliverable** — In the Al-Aqraboon PM Summary popup, I wrote "Ahmed El-Afify" twice (header + validation gate). The correct name is "Mahmoud El-Afify". I had conflated him with "Ahmed Lakosha" (an unrelated commenter in the source file). One `grep "El-Afify"` across the repo would have caught the guess. Rule: before any name ships in a deliverable, verify against a source file or ask the user — don't pull from conversational memory alone. Names in PM artifacts get quoted in meetings and cascade into other documents; the cost of correction is never zero.
325. **Display before push on shared repos — preview is mandatory for client-visible deliverables** — Mid-session on Al-Aqraboon, the user said "display before you push". By then two commits had already landed (121f128, aea60c2). For any edit to a file under version control that's visible to stakeholders (dashboards, proposals, email drafts, HTML reports), the flow must be: draft → show content in chat → wait for explicit approval → commit → push. Even small corrections. The cost of previewing is 30 seconds; the cost of a wrong name or wrong number landing in front of a client is far higher.
326. **Flag unverifiable numbers instead of passing them through as if sourced** — The Al-Aqraboon PM Summary showed "10 Days Stalled" in a KPI card. Arithmetic from the review date (2026-03-26) to today (2026-04-12) was 17 days, not 10. The number was passed through in the first answer without flagging the mismatch. Rule: if a headline number can't be traced to a computation you can show, say so explicitly in the answer ("this number is from the dashboard; I can't verify the start date"). Don't launder unverified counts into authoritative answers — stakeholders will cite them.
327. **Quoted verdicts beat paraphrases when the owner will cite them upward** — The Al-Aqraboon remediation popup embeds an exact quote from El-Afify's Section 11: *"This backlog CANNOT enter any sprint. Estimated remediation: 3–4 weeks of dedicated BA/PO work before Sprint 0."* Originally it was a paraphrase. The verbatim quote is more useful because the owner (Mohammed Salah) can cite it in meetings without re-verifying. Rule: for accountability items where an owner must defend a timeline or scope decision, embed a verbatim quote + source attribution, not a cleaned-up summary.

## HTML Asset Loading & Image Fallbacks (Apr 12, 2026)

328. **Always null-guard `onerror` on `<img>` fallback handlers — `onerror="this.src='fallback.jpg'"` is an infinite-loop bug** — Relief Center SLA v8 had `<img src="../Logo.jpg" onerror="this.src='../Logo -.jpeg'">`. When the primary failed, `onerror` reassigned `src`; if the fallback also failed (or the reassignment re-triggered the error), the handler kept firing because `onerror` was never cleared. The browser entered a load→fail→reassign loop, producing rapid flicker (logo appearing and disappearing multiple times per second). **Rule**: every `onerror` fallback MUST clear itself first — `onerror="this.onerror=null;this.src='fallback.jpg'"`. Apply to all `<img>`, `<script>`, `<link>` fallback patterns.
329. **Inline critical visual assets as base64 data URIs in client-facing HTML deliverables** — Relative paths like `../Logo.jpg` break when (a) the parent folder name contains spaces (`Relief Center` → URL-encoding inconsistency across browsers/file:// contexts), (b) the file is moved or emailed, or (c) the client opens it from a different location. Embedding the logo as `data:image/jpeg;base64,...` directly in the `<img src>` makes the HTML self-contained and portable. Cost: +44 KB on a single-file deliverable — negligible. Benefit: zero broken images regardless of where the file lives. Apply to: logos, signature marks, small icons in any standalone HTML deliverable sent to clients.
330. **Spaces in parent folder names silently break relative asset paths in file:// HTML** — Even when the file exists at the resolved path, some browser/OS combinations fail to load `../Logo.jpg` from a folder named `Relief Center` because of inconsistent space handling (`%20` vs raw space vs `+`). The failure is intermittent and hard to reproduce, which is the worst kind. **Two fixes**: (1) URL-encode spaces explicitly in `src` attributes (`../Logo%20-.jpeg`), or (2) inline the asset as base64 to eliminate the path entirely. Prefer #2 for client deliverables.
331. **When patching a versioned deliverable, also patch every co-existing sibling copy carrying the same bug — `glob` the folder before declaring done** — The Relief Center SLA logo flicker was fixed in `Relief_Center_SLA_v9.html`, but a parallel client-named copy `Relief Center & Pearl Pixels Services level Agreement (SLA).html` lived in the same folder with the identical buggy `<img>` tag. The user opened the client-named copy (not v9), the loop ran for hours in their browser tab, and 111,138 requests accumulated before they reported it again. **Rule**: after fixing a bug in a versioned file, immediately `grep` the entire deliverable folder for the buggy pattern (`onerror`, the broken path, the bad function call) and patch every match in the same batch. Versioning the canonical file does NOT propagate fixes to client-named or email-attachment copies. Applies to: SLA documents, dashboards, proposals, any folder where multiple naming conventions co-exist.
332. **Browser tabs with infinite-loop bugs keep running until the tab is killed — fixing the file on disk does not stop the loop** — After patching the buggy HTML on disk, the user's browser tab was still racking up requests because the page in memory was the pre-fix version. Reload alone may not be enough if the tab is hung; the fix must include "close the tab completely, then reopen". When reporting any infinite-loop fix to the user, the verification instructions MUST start with "close the existing tab" — never assume a refresh is sufficient.

## PM Authority & Tone (Apr 12, 2026)

333. **PM does not "request" review windows from EM — PM defines and informs** — A Project Manager and an Engineering Manager are peers in the project chain, not subordinate/superior. Replace "I am formally requesting a 3–5 day review window" with "I will align with the PM Director on the brief in parallel." The PM defines the validation cycle and informs other stakeholders; they do not ask permission to do their job. In the Al-Aqraboon kickoff reply, the v3 draft asked Basha for a review window — the user rejected it because the tone made the PM look subordinate to the EM. The v4 draft removed the request and replaced it with a definitional statement.

334. **Frame quality gates as validation cycles, not approval requests** — Instead of "until the brief is approved by me and the PM Director", say "once the scope is stabilized through BA, FL, and QA validation". This frames the gate as a quality engineering practice (BA → FL → QA pipeline) that protects delivery, not as a top-down approval bottleneck that blocks the project. Validation language is constructive; approval language is obstructive. Same gate, different perception.

335. **Engage the QA gate AFTER BA finalizes scope, never in parallel or after sprint start** — When a Business Analyst is rebuilding scope from scratch, the QA/QC engineer must be brought into the cycle once the BA completes the rebuild — to identify business gaps and edge cases before the brief is locked. Skipping QA at this gate (or running QA in parallel with BA) lets gaps surface in later sprints, where they cost 10x more to fix. In Al-Aqraboon, Mostafa Ahmed was named in the brief's team table but had zero engagement when Basha sent the kickoff — the PM's reply explicitly called this out as a delivery quality risk and committed to engaging Mostafa after the BA's 3 May milestone.

336. **Don't acknowledge binding commitments before formally reviewing them** — Lines like "the 4-month commitment is noted" or "scope is acknowledged" implicitly accept a timeline, scope, or risk profile that has not been validated. Remove acknowledgment phrases for any commitment that is part of an unreviewed brief. Use neutral openers ("Thank you for the formal kickoff") that thank the sender without binding the PM to anything in the attachment. Implicit acceptance of unreviewed commitments is the #1 way PMs get trapped into impossible deliveries.

## Stakeholder Communication & Emails (continued — Apr 12, 2026)

337. **Never Reply-All on a sensitive governance message — manually narrow recipients** — When replying to a kickoff or commitment email that contains scope concerns, pushback, or naming of underperforming team members, manually set TO=sender + CC=chain-of-authority only. Reply-All exposes the sender publicly to peers/reports and names internal team gaps to people who don't need to know. In the Al-Aqraboon kickoff reply, Reply-All would have aired "scope not clear to the team" and "Mostafa not yet engaged" to 5 unrelated recipients (Hatem, Amr, Jassim, Agha, Hamza), embarrassing Basha publicly and exposing Mostafa by name. Send privately, with PM Director on CC for authority closure.

338. **Conclusion-first for executive emails — under 200 words, fits one mobile screen** — Lead with the PM observation (what's wrong + evidence), not formalities or section breaks. The Al-Aqraboon v3 draft (700+ words, 7 numbered sections, governance precedent paragraph, items-needed list) was rejected as "more huge for management to read on email." The v4 draft (under 200 words, conclusion-first, two-bullet evidence) was accepted. Management reads on mobile in 30 seconds — design for that. Long analytical sections belong in the supporting HTML file, not in the email body.

339. **First-time receipt disclosure is a governance baseline statement** — When a binding document (brief, contract, scope, commitment) is shared with the PM for the first time, explicitly state "this email is the first time X has been shared with me." It establishes the legal/operational baseline that the PM had no input during drafting and protects against implicit acceptance. Distinguish carefully from "the project is new to me" — the project may be 6 weeks old in the repo, but the formal binding document is new. State the receipt fact precisely.

## Memory & Cross-Project Verification (continued — Apr 12, 2026)

340. **Always grep ALL project memories for team roles before answering — not just the current project's memory** — When asked about a person's role or responsibility, run `Grep pattern="<name1>|<name2>" path="C:\Users\DELL\.claude\projects" glob="**/memory/*.md"` BEFORE answering. The master roster lives at `d--KhairGate--HUB--xhatem-Github\memory\project_team_roster.md` and covers all KhairGate + Taqat + Pearl Pixels people across projects. Single-project memory grep is not sufficient — many people appear only in other projects' memories. The Al-Aqraboon Email session required two corrections ("deep search your memory") because the first answer was guessed from email domains instead of cross-project memory grep. Reinforces lesson 227.

341. **Read project-specific status files before drafting any reply email** — When drafting a response to a project email, search the project repository for current status files (`pm_summary.html`, `Project_Readiness_Report.html`, `orientation_gaps_v2.html`, `backlog_validation.html`). These contain the team's most recent verified work and completely change the response framing. In Al-Aqraboon, the BA's 3-wave plan (Mar 30) and FL's 20 findings (Apr 12, same day as kickoff) transformed the reply from "acknowledge and stall" to "acknowledge and cite active progress." Drafting without reading these files produces a generic, weak response that misses the strategic angle.

## Document Sensitivity & Internal Numbers (continued — Apr 12, 2026)

342. **Never quote internal harsh numbers (readiness scores, gap counts, effort variance) in public replies** — Internal readiness scores (e.g., 1.5/10 backlog), gap counts (+190 missing stories), and effort variance estimates (40-50% underestimate) belong in private 1:1s with the PM Director, not in Reply-All emails to the engineering manager. Save those numbers for a private attachment to the PM Director, alongside the full readiness report. Public replies should reference team work (BA plan, FL findings) as constructive evidence instead. Quoting harsh internal numbers publicly humiliates the document owner and poisons the working relationship.

343. **First time you see an attachment ≠ first time the project exists — phrase the disclosure precisely** — Distinguish between "I'm seeing this brief for the first time" (governance fact about the document) and "the project is new to me" (which would be wrong if the project has prior history). The Al-Aqraboon project had 6 weeks of repository history (20 commits, 48 pages) when the formal brief was first shared with the PM. State the receipt fact precisely without implying ignorance of the project, otherwise the disclosure becomes self-undermining.

## Iterative Email Drafting (Apr 12, 2026)

344. **Complex governance reply emails require 4+ iterations with strategic feedback — plan for refinement, not one-shot drafts** — A complex Al-Aqraboon kickoff reply went through: v1 (generic acknowledgment, no context) → v2 (Apr 9 BA-rebuild context integrated) → v3 (brief approval gate framing, governance precedent paragraph) → v4 (executive short, PM-tone shift, BA/FL progress evidence). Each iteration was driven by user feedback I had not had context for at the previous step. Save each version's design intent as an inline callout so the user can compare. Don't try to nail it in v1; plan for iterative refinement. The user reveals the most important context (e.g., "I'm seeing this for the first time", "the team is rebuilding scope from scratch") incrementally as they review your draft.

## Counts vs Itemized Lists (Apr 12, 2026)

345. **Never fabricate itemized lists from cited counts — quote the count, name the source artifact, flag the gap** — When a status report cites a count ("10 remediation actions", "31 dependencies", "+190 missing stories") but does NOT enumerate the items, never invent the list to satisfy a stakeholder request. Quote only what the source literally says (count + buckets/categories), name the missing artifact by filename + commit hash (e.g., `backlog_validation_v2.html` @ `7f6eb9d`), and offer to fetch the real source before the user acts. **Why:** Stakeholders act on whatever you hand them. A guessed list of "10 remediation actions" becomes a fake commitment tracker — worse than saying "I don't have it." Triggered by Apr 12, 2026 Al-Aqraboon Readiness Report review where Salah asked for the 10 backlog remediation items; the report named only the count (10) and four buckets (acceptance criteria, SOP mapping, dependencies, DoR), and the itemized list lived in a file outside the working directory. **How to apply:** (1) Quote the count + buckets verbatim. (2) Name the source artifact precisely (file + commit). (3) Offer to fetch before answering definitively. (4) Never paraphrase a count into an enumeration.

## Source-Root Index & Project Context Discipline (Apr 12, 2026)

346. **Treat the user-visible index as the canonical drift signal, not the JSON baseline** — Index drift can hide when `scan_baseline.json` is fresher than the user-facing `Claude_Sessions_Index.md/.html`. On Apr 12, 2026 the baseline already contained Afriqat, IndoQat, Herbal-Gardens, and AI Call Center, but the index never picked them up — the gap was invisible if you only diffed baseline vs disk. **Why:** The baseline is a staging file, not a deliverable. Stakeholders, future sessions, and the dashboard read the .md/.html index. **How to apply:** After every D:\ scan, diff index counts against the baseline (not just baseline against disk). Any folder in the baseline missing from the index is drift, even if disk and baseline agree.

347. **Every project root needs a tailored CLAUDE.md — no exceptions, no boilerplate** — On Apr 12, 2026 only 1 of 18 indexed project folders (`Water for Mosques`) had a root CLAUDE.md. The other 17 forced every new session to rediscover purpose, key subfolders, and standing rules from scratch. **Why:** Without root CLAUDE.md, Claude burns turns re-exploring things the index already knows, and standing rules (e.g., "Department Workflows = HTML only, never DOCX") get violated by accident. **How to apply:** Every top-level project folder gets its own CLAUDE.md with purpose, key subfolders, standing rules, current file counts, and links to sibling/sub CLAUDE.md files. Tailored from the index entry — never a generic template.

348. **Latest version subfolder gets a CLAUDE.md; historical versions stay frozen** — When a project has versioned subfolders (Udemy v1/v2/V3, Enhanced Standalone v2–v15, khairworld V.1/V.4, Claude_Courses_Dashboard v1–v31), only the *current* version needs its own CLAUDE.md. Adding context files to v1/v2/old snapshots invites editing frozen artifacts and breaks the "version = immutable snapshot" contract. **Why:** Historical versions are reference material, not active workspaces. **How to apply:** When creating per-version context files, scan for the highest-numbered/latest folder and create CLAUDE.md only there. Mention older versions by path with a "frozen, do not edit" note inside the latest version's CLAUDE.md.

349. **Two folders differing only in punctuation need explicit cross-reference CLAUDE.md files** — `D:\KhairGate (Wallet)\` and `D:\KhairGate Wallet\` are different projects with different owners (Sponsorship Module vs. Team Aya), but the index treated them as one for months. **Why:** Filesystem-similar names create governance hazards: edits land in the wrong folder, MEMORY entries collide, drift compounds silently. **How to apply:** Whenever two folders differ only in punctuation, parens, casing, or spacing, both must have CLAUDE.md files that explicitly cross-reference each other ("Not to be confused with X, which is owned by Y"). Update the Source-Root index to disambiguate them as separate sessions.

350. **When you find drift mid-task, write the flag into the artifact, not just the chat** — While creating the v15 Enhanced Standalone CLAUDE.md, I discovered the Source-Root index only tracked v9–v12. The flag was added as an "⚠️ Index Drift" section *inside* the v15 CLAUDE.md, not just verbally to the user. **Why:** Chat messages disappear; future sessions reading the artifact get the warning automatically without needing to re-discover the gap. **How to apply:** When drift is discovered as a side-effect of another task, embed a short "⚠️ Drift" callout in the file you're already creating/editing. Reference the canonical index location and what needs reconciling.

351. **Plan mode can re-engage mid-session — verify state before each non-trivial edit** — On Apr 12, 2026 a `Claude_Sessions_Index.md` edit slipped through after plan mode had silently re-activated. The edit was correct content but violated plan mode's read-only contract. **Why:** Plan mode toggles are easy to miss inside long tool-use chains, especially after a system reminder. **How to apply:** After any system reminder mentioning "Re-entering Plan Mode" or "Plan mode is active", explicitly confirm state in your next thought before any write. Treat plan mode like a transactional flag: re-check, don't assume.

## PM Dashboard Layout & Role Semantics (Apr 12, 2026)

352. **Arrow notation `A → B` in owner/responsibility labels needs semantic agreement before shipping** — I interpreted `A → B` as *"A reports/flows output to B"*. The PM's actual semantic was *"work flows FROM source A TO the person who must act B"*. Same glyph, opposite meaning. Shipping Action #3 with `Mohammed Salah → PM + Ahmed Lakosha` was wrong — the correct form was `Ahmed Lakosha → Mohammed Salah` (Lakosha issued the findings; Salah must review them). The PM called it out directly: "you don't understand the status". Rule: before any arrow goes in a PM label, state the semantic explicitly to the user (flow-of-work vs reporting direction) and confirm in one word. The glyph is free; the ambiguity is expensive.
353. **Nested reply threading inside a table cell beats adding columns for 3-party feedback** — When Basha posted 11 counter-comments to El-Afify's 10 remediation items and the PM then replied to each, the cleanest UI was a light-blue indented block with a `↳ PM Reply — [Name]` label sitting directly under the reviewer's quoted comment inside the same `<td>`. Reads as a conversation thread, no horizontal real estate lost, bilingual-safe, degrades gracefully on mobile. Rule: for 3-party feedback (author → reviewer → PM) inside a data table, use nested reply blocks in the same cell before reaching for extra columns.
354. **Restructure by role-owner beats grouped sub-blocks when a section contains inputs from multiple roles** — When the "Team Inputs Requiring Integration" section had Block A (Lakosha/FL), Block B (El-Afify/BA), and a Readiness Summary all stacked inside one `<div class="sec">`, the cleanest fix was splitting them into two parallel sections — one per role-owner — with distinct color coding (BA=red, FL=purple). Each section then has one owner, one source, one status, one action. Rule: when a section contains inputs from 2+ distinct role-owners, promote each to its own top-level section with its own color identity before the audience has to ask "who owns what".
355. **Replace an unverifiable KPI card instead of defending it** — The "10 Days Stalled" KPI was the counter that triggered the whole Basha review chain, but the arithmetic (2026-03-26 → 2026-04-12 = 17 days, not 10) meant the number couldn't be traced to any source. Rather than hiding it or keeping it and flagging, the right move was replacing it with a factual count: "30 Open Findings" (20 Lakosha + 10 El-Afify). Same card slot, same urgency color, sourced from two real files. Rule: when a dashboard KPI can't be traced to a computation you can show, replace the card with a factual count from a known source rather than defending the unverifiable number or deleting the card entirely.
356. **Disambiguate same-first-name collisions explicitly in client-visible UI** — Al-Aqraboon has two people named "Mahmoud" — *Mahmoud Elshahed* (PM) and *Mahmoud El-Afify* (Senior BA) — plus *Mohamed Basha = Mohammed Salah* (same person, two names in use). Every label that references them must be fully surname-qualified, and where a person has two names in daily use, both must appear (e.g. `Mohamed Basha (Mohammed Salah) — Engineering Manager`). Ambiguity in a single name cost one push cycle today when a validator was labeled "Ahmed El-Afify" instead of "Mahmoud El-Afify". Rule: in any PM-owned artifact that goes to stakeholders, never refer to a team member by first name alone. Surname + role at first mention, consistent through the document, dual identity shown if applicable.

## External Deliverables vs Repository Canonicalization (Apr 13, 2026)

357. **External deliverables drift silently from the repo — variance-check before distribution, never after** — On 2026-04-13, Al-Aqraboon's external brief (`D:\Alaqraboon\Email\AQ_project_brief.html`) was authored by the same solution architect who maintains the repo, but from an earlier snapshot. Headline totals matched (244 stories, 1,111 SP, 6 epics, 12-member team, 4 months) — enough to pass a cursory glance. Six epic-level SP distributions had drifted (E0 −10, E1 −35, E2 −10 stories/−151 SP, E3 −34, E4 −10 stories/−15 SP, E5 +14), and the brief carried business-case data the repo never had (60→10 min processing KPI, 1,200/840 capacity gap, QAR approval tiers, Sandi integration). None of this was visible without a line-by-line diff. **Rule:** any external deliverable authored outside the version-controlled repo must be variance-checked against repo v2 files before distribution. Compare at minimum: story totals, SP totals, per-epic breakdown, team roster, integration list, workflow count, approval tiers, tech stack, headline KPIs. Declare the repo as canonical source of truth. Document the variance in a dedicated bilingual HTML report committed to the repo alongside the deliverable. **Why:** headline-total matches create false confidence; the damage only surfaces when a stakeholder quotes a number the team can't defend.

358. **Git log is free, defensible evidence — use it for provenance tables in trust-critical reports** — When building a variance or audit report, every "source" claim gains defensibility if it carries `git log --follow --format="%ai %h %s"` dates (created + last-updated + commit hash). On 2026-04-13 the Alaqraboon variance report added a Section 12 "Evidence Pages — Source Dates" table with 8 rows, showing that the oldest evidence file (Al_Aqraboon_Workflows.html, 2026-02-25) predated the brief by nearly 2 months, and the newest repo updates (2026-04-12) were 1 day before the report. This single table shifts the report from "Mahmoud's opinion" to "git's verdict" — the reader cannot dismiss the timeline. **Rule:** any report that argues "source A is more current/authoritative than source B" must include a git-sourced provenance table. Cost: one `git log --follow` per file. Benefit: the argument becomes machine-verifiable. Applies to variance reports, audit reports, readiness reports, brief-vs-repo comparisons, scope reconciliation docs.

359. **Bilingual footer stamps use direction-neutral styling — border-top, not border-left/right — to avoid RTL override hell** — On 2026-04-13 we added `Created / Last Updated / Commit` footer stamps to 8 Alaqraboon pages. The stamps use inline-styled `<div>` with `border-top:2px solid #1565C0` instead of `border-left/right`, so no RTL override is needed. Text uses `data-en` / `data-ar` attributes which every page's existing `toggleLang()` function picks up automatically (confirmed working with both `textContent`-style toggles and `innerHTML`-style toggles). Inline styles avoid CSS variable dependencies, so the stamp works regardless of the host page's theme. **Rule:** for any cross-cutting UI element added to pages with different CSS themes and bilingual requirements, prefer (a) direction-neutral CSS properties (border-top/bottom, text-align:center, no margin-left/right), (b) inline styles (no class dependencies), (c) `data-en`/`data-ar` attributes (picked up by existing toggle handlers). Test on at least one page using `innerHTML`-style toggling AND one using `textContent`-style toggling before declaring the pattern safe.

360. **When adding a 3rd dropdown to a 2-way toggle handler, upgrade to N-way arrays — don't hardcode pairs** — Alaqraboon `nav.js` originally had `['v1-dd', 'v2-dd'].forEach(...)` with `otherId = ddId === 'v1-dd' ? 'v2-dd' : 'v1-dd'` hardcoded. Adding a 3rd dropdown (`it-pm-dd`) with the same pattern would have required 3 pairwise branches. The right fix was to promote the list to a single `DD_IDS = ['v1-dd', 'v2-dd', 'it-pm-dd']` array and loop: on click, toggle current, then close all others in the array. Adding a 4th dropdown now requires one array entry — zero logic change. **Rule:** when any binary pair is about to become a triple, stop and generalize to an array. Pairwise special-casing is a maintenance debt that compounds with each addition.

361. **Plan mode re-enters silently after a browser review break — stage files survive but commit is blocked until ExitPlanMode** — On 2026-04-13, after I opened the variance report in the user's browser for visual review (via `start ""`), the harness re-entered plan mode. The user then approved the commit message, and I ran `git add` (which succeeded — the hook fired and staged 12 files). But `git commit` was still blocked by plan mode's read-only contract; I had to call `ExitPlanMode` mid-flow to complete the commit. The plan file was outdated (described the pre-review state), so I also had to edit it before exiting. **Rule:** after any user interaction that might trigger a plan-mode re-entry (opening files in external apps, long pauses, approval breaks, tool-use chains spanning hours), explicitly check for plan-mode state before the next write operation. If plan mode is active, edit the plan file to reflect current state FIRST, then call `ExitPlanMode` to request re-approval. Never try to commit or push while plan mode is active — the harness will not stop you on `git add` but will stop you on `git commit`.

362. **Commit messages: drop narrative framing, keep structural sections — shorter beats rhetorical** — On 2026-04-13 I drafted a commit message with an opening justification paragraph ("Reports that the external brief ... diverges ... Flags repo v2 as canonical source of truth"), followed by 5 structural sections (New deliverable / Navigation / Search / Git provenance stamps / Housekeeping). The user refined it to drop the justification paragraph, drop Git provenance stamps, drop Housekeeping — keeping only subject line + 3 structural sections (New deliverable / Navigation / Search) + co-author trailer. The final message was shorter, purely mechanical, and readable as a changelog entry. **Rule:** commit message bodies should describe WHAT changed (files, categories, counts) in structural bullet sections, not WHY it changed (the "why" belongs in the PR description, the variance report, or the linked issue). Narrative framing ages poorly in `git log`. Keep the subject line for the headline and the body for a changelog-style breakdown. If you find yourself writing a paragraph, move it to the deliverable itself.

## Source-Root RAG Sync & Live-Scan Discipline (Apr 13, 2026)

363. **"No new folders" is one check out of three, not the whole coverage audit** — On 2026-04-13 I answered "no new folders on D:\" when the user asked if RAG needed updating. That was technically correct but missed two other classes of drift: (a) existing indexed folders missing their root `<folder-name>.md` because the last batch script skipped edge cases (see lesson 365), and (b) new *sub-folders* created inside already-indexed parents since the last baseline scan — which is what held up `D:\Project Management\Project management Quarter Plan\Projects Handling Review Meeting 6-4-2026` that day. **Rule:** every RAG coverage audit must run all three checks: (1) new top-level folders vs `scan_baseline.json`, (2) every HTML-bearing folder's root `<folder-name>.md` via Glob, (3) diff the live RAG list (`curl /ui/projects/list`) against the current index sessions — not just the baseline file. Skipping any of the three yields false-negative answers that look thorough but aren't.

364. **RAG entries are per sub-session, not per top-level folder — map accordingly, never add root entries on top of sub-entries** — On 2026-04-13, checking the RAG at `http://127.0.0.1:21420/ui/projects/list` revealed 42 entries organized per sub-session (KhairGate HUB = 11 entries, Project Management = 9 entries, DevOps = 4, HR = 2, Personal = 3), not one-per-D:\-folder. After creating a new root-level `D:\KhairGate (HUB)\KhairGate (HUB).md` to close a gap from the Apr 12 batch, I initially planned to register it as a new RAG project — but that would have duplicated chunks already covered by the 11 existing sub-entries, bloating the index and degrading search relevance. **Rule:** before adding any new RAG entry, check whether its path is already covered recursively by an existing entry. If yes, the folder MD becomes documentation-only (for humans and future Claude sessions), not a RAG entry. Map RAG granularity to `Claude_Sessions_Index.md` sub-section level, never to top-level folder.

365. **WebFetch auto-upgrades HTTP→HTTPS — it WILL fail on any localhost dev server** — On 2026-04-13, `WebFetch http://127.0.0.1:21420/projects` returned `ECONNREFUSED` despite the RAG server being up and the URL loading fine in Chrome. The tool silently upgrades HTTP to HTTPS (documented in its description), and local dev servers don't bind HTTPS. Switching to `curl -s -m 5 http://127.0.0.1:21420/...` via Bash worked immediately. **Rule:** for any HTTP-only localhost service (RAG UI 21420, Outlook Update Server 5050, Standalone Update 5051, Taqat 5052, D Drive Scan 5053, or any PowerShell HttpListener), always use `curl` via Bash, never WebFetch. Document the port-to-service map in MEMORY.md so future sessions don't waste turns rediscovering this.

366. **Baselines go stale the day they're written — always cross-check against the live source** — `scan_baseline.json` last updated Apr 12, 2026 missed a folder created Apr 13 at 06:50 (20 hours later). Any "compare against baseline" audit on Apr 13+ gives false negatives for same-day additions. **Rule:** treat `scan_baseline.json` as a fixed point-in-time snapshot, not a live truth source. Every coverage check must also query the live state: `ls D:/<folder>` for disk truth, `curl /ui/projects/list` for RAG truth, `grep Claude_Sessions_Index.md` for index truth. Reconcile disagreements by trusting the live source and updating the baseline last. Never answer "nothing new" based on a baseline older than ~24 hours without live verification.

367. **Batch scripts that operate on folder names silently skip names with parentheses, spaces, `&`, or Arabic characters** — On 2026-04-12 a batch created 37 `<folder-name>.md` files across every indexed session folder. On 2026-04-13 a coverage check revealed `D:\KhairGate (HUB)\KhairGate (HUB).md` was missing — almost certainly because the script's glob or regex didn't escape the parentheses, and the folder was silently skipped without raising an error. **Rule:** any batch operation over the filesystem must be tested against edge-case folder names *before* the batch runs (parens, spaces, `&`, single quotes, Arabic characters like `D:\Global Lessons\مشاريع`). Add an explicit post-run verification step that counts output files against expected-input count and reports any gap — silent skips are the worst failure mode because nothing surfaces until a coverage audit weeks later.

368. **Remove-then-re-add for RAG updates; direct-add for new entries** — On 2026-04-13 the user clarified the rule: when a RAG entry already exists and its source MD has changed, remove the existing entry first then re-add, because RAG caches metadata and in-place updates can serve stale chunks. This rule applies only to *existing* entries — brand-new entries (like the `project-management-quarter-plan` added the same day) go straight through `/ui/projects/add` with no removal needed. **Rule:** (a) before any RAG add operation, check via `curl /ui/projects/list` whether the target path or ID already exists; (b) if it exists, DELETE first via `/ui/projects/<id>/remove`, confirm deletion, then POST the add; (c) if it doesn't exist, POST directly. Never update in-place. Document both the remove and re-add in the session changelog so future sessions can trace what was re-indexed when.

## Publish vs Memory Classification (Apr 13, 2026)

369. **Every piece of user information has one of two destinations — classify before acting, default to memory, ask when unclear** — On 2026-04-13 while building the Al-Aqraboon Change Request HTML (CR-2026-04-13), I embedded an internal PM governance rule (about which files are "PM governance artifacts" vs "scope sources") directly into Section 2 of the team-facing CR document. The rule was true and useful as internal discipline, but it was NOT supposed to be visible to team owners reading the CR — it was background context for me, not content for the deliverable. The user had to explicitly correct me: *"remove this word ... it's for your memory only, not to publish."* Then the user taught me the meta-rule: *"Let's agree that there are things that can be published and that there are other things to teach you and provide you with information to keep in your memory. You should keep that in mind when I talk to you."* **Rule:** Every piece of information the user shares during a conversation has one of two destinations — (a) **Publish:** goes into a deliverable (HTML report, email, commit message, public document) where stakeholders will see it; or (b) **Memory only:** stays in internal memory files, plan notes, or mental context, never appears in any user-visible artifact. **How to apply:** (1) When user provides context or rationale → default to **memory only** unless they explicitly say "add this to the file" or the context is obviously part of the deliverable being built. (2) When user requests a specific content change → default to **publish**, but confirm whether associated reasoning is meant to be published alongside. (3) When I discover a justification or exclusion rule during exploration → treat it as memory by default; surface it in chat for user review but do NOT auto-embed it in the deliverable. (4) When user says "remove X from the file" → interpret it as "keep X in memory, don't show stakeholders" unless they explicitly say "forget X entirely". (5) When in doubt, **ASK** — better to confirm "is this for the file or for your memory?" than to over-publish internal context or under-publish intentional content. **Publish signals:** add / include / show / mention / note in the document. **Memory signals:** remember / keep in mind / for context / understand that / to teach you / so you know. **Ambiguous — always ask:** "note that" / "by the way" / "also" / "additionally". Applies across ALL projects and sessions. Saved to `feedback_publish_vs_memory.md` in Alaqraboon project memory and as a standing rule in both `d:\Alaqraboon\CLAUDE.md` and `C:\Users\DELL\.claude\CLAUDE.md`.

## PM Governance Boundaries & Assumption Discipline (Apr 13, 2026)

370. **PM orchestrates, never authors team-owned artifacts** — On 2026-04-13 I proposed that the PM directly edit `backlog_v2.html`, `architecture.html`, `technical_proposal_v2.html`, `proposal_v2.html`, `Al_Aqraboon_Workflows.html`, and `basis_of_calculation_v2.html` to backport business-case data and fix title references. The user correctly pushed back: *"you need me as IT project manager to make these changes on the pages that team created and updated on repo, right?"* The answer is no. Each of those files has a specific authoring owner (BA, FL, SA, EM) who maintains them through their own PR workflow. When the PM edits a team-owned artifact directly, it (a) overrides the owner's authority on their own file, (b) violates the canonical-source rule ("repo v2 is team-authored"), and (c) creates future drift when the real owner next touches the file and overwrites or disagrees with the PM's changes. **Rule:** PM's role on team-owned files is **flag → request → review**, never author. Use a formal Change Request document (HTML or Markdown) to assign items to owners with acceptance criteria; owners push their own PRs; PM reviews each. PM may directly author only: (a) files the PM created (PM Summary, variance reports, change requests, dashboards), (b) governance / memory / CLAUDE.md / plan files, (c) files where the author explicitly delegates edits back to PM. The Change Request pattern from 2026-04-13 (`CHANGE_REQUEST_2026-04-13.html`) is the canonical example — it tells each owner WHAT to edit, WHERE to edit it, WITH what acceptance criteria, and nothing more. PM does not embed owner's content inside the Change Request.

371. **Never propagate PM assumptions across multiple documents — caught early or corrected with discipline** — On 2026-04-13 I invented deadlines (5 / 10 / 15 working days) for the 6 CR items when drafting `CHANGE_REQUEST_2026-04-13.html`. Those invented numbers then propagated into `pm_summary.html` (3 locations), the variance report Section 7, `change_request_status.md` memory, and the plan file. The user eventually caught them: *"who is add the Deadline for every topic? me or someone of the team or your assumption?"* Full admission + removal across every document took a dedicated commit (`001489a`) to undo. **Rule:** Any numeric commitment — deadlines, effort estimates, story points, dollar amounts, capacity figures, dates — MUST come from a real decision-maker (PM, BA, owner, customer) OR be explicitly labeled `TBD — owner to confirm` at first mention. Never invent AND never propagate invented numbers across documents where they become load-bearing. **Detection signal:** if you can't answer "where did this number come from?" with a specific person + date, it's an invented number and needs either (a) removal, (b) TBD marker, or (c) a rationale + disclaimer. **Prevention:** when drafting governance documents (CRs, RFCs, scope summaries), separate "facts known from source files" from "values that need decisions." Mark the second category clearly BEFORE any other file references them.

372. **Verify the team's communication model before designing handoffs** — On 2026-04-13 I offered to draft Slack / Teams / email handoff messages for the CR items, assuming push-style communication. The user clarified: *"the team have access on the report and they get pull and do his works"* — the team pulls the repo and reads `pm_summary.html` + `CHANGE_REQUEST_2026-04-13.html` directly. The repo IS the communication channel. This required a new commit to fix pm_summary.html Next Actions item 9 and add a "Pull-based Workflow" info-box. **Rule:** before designing any handoff / notification / coordination mechanism, explicitly ask or verify HOW the team receives information. Three models exist: (a) **Push** — PM sends messages via Slack/Teams/email and team reacts, (b) **Pull** — team checks the repo/dashboard on their schedule and reads updates there, (c) **Sync** — updates happen only at standups/meetings and the team brings back notes. Each model dictates different content placement: push needs standalone messages with context, pull needs centralized hub pages that the team bookmarks, sync needs agenda items and meeting minutes. Wrong model = wrong content structure = extra commits to fix later. **How to verify:** ask "does the team see repo changes automatically, or do I need to tell them?" The answer determines whether content belongs in a message OR in the repo.

373. **Silent fix patterns extend by category — maintain consistency once established** — On 2026-04-13 the user established "silent fix" as the norm for Mohammed Salah title corrections (pm_summary.html lines 270 + 354). Over the next several turns, the silent pattern extended organically to `basis_of_calculation_v2.html` (3 locations), `AQ_project_brief.html` (Section 3 table + Action #11), variance report Section 7 (the Action cell for Salah's row), variance report line 169 author label, and `pm_summary.html` line 440 (role enumeration). Each extension was a user decision, but the user expected consistency — if I had marked one of them explicit while others stayed silent, the git log would become inconsistent and confusing. **Rule:** once a content category is marked "silent" (commit message does not name the change, even though the diff shows it), all future work in that same category stays silent unless the user explicitly reverses the policy. Track the silent-fix category in memory (e.g., `silent_fix_category_title_corrections = [pm_summary.html, basis_of_calculation_v2.html, AQ_project_brief.html, Brief_vs_Repo_Variance_Report_v1.html]`) and check against it before naming any future fix. **Why consistency matters:** git log readers infer intent from patterns. If 5 silent fixes are followed by 1 explicit one, the explicit one looks anomalous and raises questions the PM may not want asked.

374. **"What about X?" questions need antecedent clarification, not interpretation** — On 2026-04-13 the user asked *"what about if highlighted this in the file pm_summary.html?"* with no clear antecedent for "this" (could have meant: the pending task list, the handoff instructions, a specific CR item, a summary status, or something I couldn't guess). I jumped to interpretation based on the most recent topic in the conversation. Multiple clarification rounds followed before the real intent surfaced: integrate CR items into the existing Section 1 BA Stream, Section 2 FL Stream, and Next Actions. **Rule:** when a user question contains ambiguous pronouns ("this", "that", "it") without a clear antecedent, ASK — don't interpret. Offer 3–4 candidate interpretations via `AskUserQuestion` tool or plain text. Cost of asking: 1 turn. Cost of guessing wrong: 2–4 turns of backtracking + potentially wasted file edits. **Signal phrases:** "what about this", "should we add that", "maybe do X" — all antecedent-light, all should trigger a clarification before action.

## Commit Discipline & Content Governance (Apr 13, 2026)

375. **Git diff ≠ commit message — "silent" means unnamed, not invisible** — On 2026-04-13 when silently fixing Mohammed Salah's title, I framed the action as "silent" because the commit message did not name the change. But the git diff still showed every modified character. When the user asked "will owners see this?", the honest answer was YES in the diff (anyone running `git show <hash>` sees it) but NO in the one-line summary. "Silent" is a property of the commit MESSAGE, not the commit CONTENT. **Rule:** when discussing silent edits with a user, always clarify: silence applies only to the commit subject line and body text. The diff remains fully visible to anyone viewing the commit. Silence is for de-emphasis, not for concealment. If the user wants true concealment (changes that don't appear anywhere), that requires NOT committing at all — which defeats the purpose of version control. Be explicit about this distinction the first time silence is proposed in a session.

376. **Squash related small commits BEFORE the first push, not after** — On 2026-04-13 three small commits (`db3f3d2` silent line 440 fix, `001489a` deadline removal + CR highlights, `3fefee3` pull-workflow reframe) stacked locally before any push. The user requested squash-then-push, which was trivial because nothing had reached `origin/master` yet (`git reset --soft` + recommit, zero force-push risk). If those three commits had each been pushed individually first, the squash would require `git push --force` — a dangerous operation on `master`. **Rule:** when related work in a single session produces multiple small commits touching overlapping files, offer to squash them into one clean commit BEFORE the first push. Check with the user: "these 3 commits are all on the same topic — squash into one before push, or keep separate?"
