Without the close+quit+release sequence, every script run leaves a Word.exe process running. **Why:** COM objects are reference-counted by .NET on the runtime side, but the underlying Word process doesn't auto-quit when references are released — it stays running invisibly. Hundreds of orphan Word processes can accumulate over time. **How to apply:** (1) Always `$word.Visible = $false` before any Documents.Open. (2) `Documents.Open(path, $false, $true)` — third param `$true` is ReadOnly mode. (3) `try/finally` block: open in try, close+quit in finally. (4) `ReleaseComObject` in finally too. (5) Same pattern for Outlook COM, Excel COM. (6) Test by running script 100x and checking Task Manager for orphan processes. (7) Reference: May 2 — `Extract-MOM.ps1` lines 30-43 (COM lifecycle pattern).

605. **Bilingual EN/AR Board Pack reuses STP v3 paired-span pattern + built as ONE HTML string then saved as standalone file via Blob URL — opens in new tab without server roundtrip** — On 2026-05-02 Phase 6 Board Pack generator builds the entire HTML doc as a string (with embedded CSS + 1 toggleLang script + paired EN/AR spans), then offers two actions: "Save to disk" (POSTs to `/api/pmo/save-board-pack` which writes `_data/board-pack/Q2-2026/{date}.html`) AND "Open in new tab" (creates Blob URL via `var blob = new Blob([html], { type: 'text/html' }); var url = URL.createObjectURL(blob); window.open(url, '_blank');`). **Why:** standalone HTML output is portable — Mahmoud can email the saved file to Tier A as attachment, or print-to-PDF via browser. Embedding CSS inline (not external stylesheet) makes it work offline + email-attachable. Blob URL avoids the (XSS-flagged) inline-write pattern. The bilingual toggle button + `html[dir="rtl"]` CSS exactly mirrors STP v3 — proven pattern. **How to apply:** (1) For any "Board-quality" output, generate complete standalone HTML with inline CSS + paired EN/AR spans + lang toggle script. (2) Use Blob URL for in-browser preview. (3) NEVER use inline-write methods — XSS hooks flag them. (4) Always escape user-controlled data before HTML interpolation: `function _esc(s) { return String(s).replace(/&/g,'&amp;').replace(/</g,'&lt;').replace(/>/g,'&gt;'); }`. (5) Reference: May 2 — `window.generateBoardPack` in v13 emits ~3 KB bilingual HTML.

606. **Windows Task Scheduler "Run task as soon as possible after a scheduled start is missed" + "Wake the computer to run" gives D2 first-wake-after-8AM semantics without leaving laptop awake overnight** — On 2026-05-02 Phase 8 Daily Briefing needs 8 AM email push but Mahmoud doesn't want laptop awake all night. Solution: configure Task Scheduler with: (a) Trigger Daily at 7:55 AM, (b) Conditions → Wake the computer to run this task = ON, (c) Settings → Run task as soon as possible after a scheduled start is missed = ON. Task fires at 7:55 if laptop awake; if not, fires within 30 min of next wake. **Why:** matches user's actual usage pattern — laptop sleeps overnight, wakes when Mahmoud opens it in the morning. Task Scheduler handles the "fire if missed" logic natively. No need for service/daemon. **How to apply:** (1) For 8 AM daily push tasks on user laptops, use Task Scheduler with both wake-to-run + run-on-missed-start enabled. (2) Trigger 5 min before target time (7:55 for 8 AM goal) — gives buffer for COM startup. (3) Action: `powershell.exe -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File "C:\path\to\script.ps1"`. (4) Document the exact Task Scheduler GUI clicks in setup notes. (5) Reference: May 2 — `Send-DailyBriefing.ps1` + setup instructions in v13 Daily Briefing tab card.

607. **Browser localStorage `Storage.setItem` can be patched globally to add side-effects (auto-save to JSON file) without modifying every caller — but ONLY for one specific key, never globally** — On 2026-05-02 Phase 5 Day 7 wired auto-save: every time `localStorage.setItem('pmo-action-log-v1', ...)` is called, fire `/api/pmo/save-action-log` 1500ms later (debounced). Implementation: patch `Storage.prototype.setItem` to call original then trigger debounced side-effect IF key matches the target. **Why:** the existing Action Log code uses `localStorage.setItem` in many places. Patching `setItem` once intercepts all writes without touching each caller. Filtering by specific key ensures we don't accidentally trigger save on UNRELATED localStorage keys. **How to apply:** (1) Patch `Storage.prototype.setItem` ONCE per IIFE — preserve original via `var origSetItem = Storage.prototype.setItem`. (2) ALWAYS filter by specific key — never apply side-effect to all setItem calls. (3) Debounce the side-effect (1500ms) so rapid sequential writes coalesce into one server call. (4) Make the side-effect non-blocking — call original setItem first, then trigger side-effect via setTimeout. (5) Document the patch in a comment so future maintainers don't get confused by the mysterious server calls. (6) Reference: May 2 — v13 `_wireAutoSaveJSON` IIFE.

608. **Phased build artifacts grow IIFE-by-IIFE in single file — each phase = its own IIFE = self-contained closure + console.log signature for diagnostic** — On 2026-05-02 v13 grew from ~13K lines (Phase 4) to ~16K lines (Phase 8 complete) via 4 new IIFEs (Phase 5 + Phase 6 + Phase 7+8). Each new IIFE has its own startup `console.log('[v13 Phase N] ... ready. Helpers: window.X, window.Y. Endpoint: /api/pmo/...')`. **Why:** when debugging "is feature X working?", DevTools console immediately shows which phase IIFEs initialized successfully (signatures present) vs which failed (signature missing → IIFE didn't load → check for syntax error). Self-contained closures prevent cross-phase variable pollution. Each phase has its own `'use strict'` + own helpers + own endpoints. **How to apply:** (1) For multi-phase additive builds in a single HTML file, use one IIFE per phase. (2) Start every IIFE with `'use strict';`. (3) End every IIFE with `console.log('[file vN Phase M] ready. Helpers: window.X, window.Y, ...')`. (4) Use phase-specific local helpers inside the IIFE — don't pollute global scope. (5) Expose only the public-API helpers via `window.helperName = helperName`. (6) When debugging, check console for ALL phase signatures present — missing one = silent loading failure. (7) Reference: May 2 — v13 has 5 IIFEs total: main (Phase 1-3a), Phase 4 finish, Phase 5, Phase 6, Phase 7+8.

609. **Action Log entry schema scales by adding optional fields per phase — `scope` field becomes the discriminator for routing logic + UI filters** — On 2026-05-02 Action Log entries grew from Phase 1 base shape to Phase 5+ enriched shape with optional fields: `scope` ('project' / 'team' / 'assignee' / 'email' / 'meeting'), `meetingId`, `meetingSubject`, `meetingDate`, `emailKey`, `emailCategory`, `emailUncategorized`, `boardAsk`, `autoCappedAt`, `closedAt`. **Why:** every phase added a new entry-type (email-reply, meeting-action, etc.) without breaking existing entries. The `scope` field is the discriminator: filter logic checks `e.scope === 'meeting'`, render logic checks `e.scope === 'email'`, etc. Backwards compat: entries from before Phase N don't have the new fields, so checks must use `=== value` not `!== value` (avoid false positives from undefined). **How to apply:** (1) Add a `scope` (or equivalent discriminator) field early in the schema design — never remove old values, only add new. (2) New phase fields = optional, with sensible defaults when missing. (3) Filter logic: `if (e.scope === 'meeting') ...` — explicit equality check, never negate-check on missing values. (4) Migration logic: when changing default values, migrate ALL legacy patterns (see lesson #595). (5) UI filters: scope dropdown grows with each phase but defaults to "All Scopes" so old entries still appear. (6) Reference: May 2 — v13 Action Log entries now have 5 scope values + ~10 phase-specific optional fields.

610. **Demo deck per bi-weekly reveal — clone-and-edit pattern STP_v(N) → STP_v(N+1) — keeps history + lets each deck stand alone — but watch for stale pacing math + Phase Roadmap "today" markers** — On 2026-05-02 built STP v5 from STP v4 via `cp` + targeted MD edits + HTML edits. Saved ~80% of the work because v4's 16-tab structure + 8 anticipated Q&As + bilingual EN/AR paired spans + Phase Roadmap table all carried over. Edits required: (a) page title + sidebar subtitle + meta strip date/version, (b) opening narrative replaced (was "compressed reveal" → "Phase 5 reveal"), (c) NEW dedicated tab section for the keystone reveal (Tab 8 Meetings became today's keystone, replacing Email's keystone marker), (d) 4 NEW Phase-N-specific Q&A blocks added, (e) Phase Roadmap table — mark TODAY's row with green-highlight + ✅ marker + "(today)" label + remove "(same file)" tag (it's already obvious), (f) Closing commits to N+1 phase by next demo date, (g) Change Log adds new row + de-emphasizes prior row (remove the green-highlight from v(N-1) row, transfer it to v(N) row), (h) pacing math recomputed if tab count changed (was "14 tabs × 30s" — for v5 became "16 tabs × 25s"), (i) links_manifest.json + .js bumped current. **Why:** stakeholders see the same audit-trail of decks across the cadence (proven during v3→v4 transition for compressed reveal); each deck stands alone if Mahmoud emails it externally; pacing math drift is the most common bug — adding 1 new tab without recomputing throws the deck-walkthrough timing off. **How to apply:** (1) Always `cp v(N) → v(N+1)` instead of writing from scratch. (2) Use a 9-item edit checklist (the (a)-(i) above) so nothing is missed. (3) Recompute pacing math whenever tab count changes — formula: `total = opening_seconds + (tab_count × seconds_per_tab) + keystone_demos + Q&A + closing`. (4) Always update both MD + HTML in the same session (drift = chaos). (5) Always bump links_manifest.json + .js together (auto-sync via check-link-drift.ps1 if it's run; manual otherwise). (6) Reference: May 2 — STP v5 built from STP v4 in ~30 min, all 9 checklist items completed.

611. **Bulk pre-build N future decks in one session via cp + surgical-edits batch — minimum-edit-set is title/subtitle/meta-strip/opening-narrative/Phase-Roadmap-row-highlight/Closing-commitment/Change-Log-row — full Q&A + tab-content can stay as v(N-1) stubs** — On 2026-05-02 built STP v6/v7/v8 (3 future decks for Jun 14 / Jun 28 / Jul 12 demos) in single ~30-min batch via `cp v5 → v6,v7,v8` + 7-edit minimum set per deck. **Why:** when the calendar is locked (4 known demo dates with known phase reveals), pre-building all decks at once eliminates the "Sunday morning crunch" pattern where each deck competes with weekend prep time. Future-Claude sessions opening these decks see the locked deck for that Sunday — no need to remember context. The minimum-edit-set is 7 items (vs 9 in lesson #610) because Q&A blocks + tab content sections can stay as v(N-1) stubs — the live demo uses the v13 dashboard, not the deck content; the deck is a reference/audit document. **How to apply:** (1) When N future demo dates with locked phase reveals are known, batch-build all N decks at once. (2) Per-deck minimum set: (i) `<title>`, (ii) sidebar subtitle line, (iii) meta strip 5 fields (Date/Cadence/Reveal labels EN+AR), (iv) opening narrative paragraph (~3 sentences), (v) Phase Roadmap row highlight (✅ today + de-highlight prior), (vi) Closing commitment (commits to N+1 phase by next demo date), (vii) Change Log row (highlighted + prior de-highlighted). (3) Skip per-deck: Q&A blocks (carry over fine), tab-content sections (carry over fine — live demo IS the content), pacing math text (recompute is optional — 1-min drift across 6 weeks is acceptable). (4) Spend 5-10 min per deck max — anything more means scope creep on the bulk build. (5) Reference: May 2 — STP v6+v7+v8 built in ~25 min total via 7-edit min-set per deck, 4 demos pre-locked end-to-end through Jul 12.

612. **Windows COM cross-integrity-level mismatch — admin-elevated PowerShell cannot connect to user-level Outlook (HRESULT 0x80080005 / CO_E_SERVER_EXEC_FAILURE) — match server integrity to client integrity** — On 2026-05-02 evening, after fixing every visible code path, calendar extraction still failed silently. Dashboard auto-recovery surfaced the real script error in a black code block: `FATAL ERROR: Retrieving the COM class factory for component with CLSID {0006F03A-0000-0000-C000-000000000046} failed due to the following error: 80080005 Server execution failed (Exception from HRESULT: 0x80080005 (CO_E_SERVER_EXEC_FAILURE))`. Diagnostic chain: csv missing → JS auto-recovery → script output surfaced → real error visible → root cause identified. Root cause: PowerShell HTTP listener was launched as **Administrator** (window title prefix "Administrator: PMO Server (port 5050)"), while Outlook runs as the **regular user**. Windows COM cannot bridge integrity levels — an elevated process is forbidden from instantiating a COM class factory hosted by a non-elevated process. Fix: close the elevated PowerShell window, re-launch the same script WITHOUT "Run as administrator" (just plain double-click the .bat or the .ps1). After plain-user re-launch, calendar extraction immediately returned 22 events, populating Meetings tab successfully. **Why:** Windows User Account Control (UAC) enforces process integrity levels (Low / Medium / High). COM activation cross-IL is blocked by design as a security boundary — otherwise any unprivileged process could puppet an elevated process. The error message is opaque (CLSID GUID) and HRESULT 0x80080005 ("Server execution failed") sounds like a server bug, not a permissions issue. The diagnostic clue is buried in the window title bar. **How to apply:** (1) For ANY COM-based local integration (Outlook / Excel / Word / IE / Access / PowerPoint), the calling process AND the COM server must be at the **same integrity level**. (2) When automating user-level apps (Outlook etc.), launch the calling script as the **regular user** — never as Administrator. (3) When debugging "ClassFactory failed 0x80080005" on Windows, FIRST check: does the PowerShell window title start with "Administrator:"? If yes, re-launch without elevation. (4) When the COM server requires elevation (rare — e.g., system-level WMI), then the caller must also elevate. (5) Add a startup self-check to the PS script: detect elevation via `[Security.Principal.WindowsPrincipal][Security.Principal.WindowsIdentity]::GetCurrent()).IsInRole([Security.Principal.WindowsBuiltInRole]::Administrator)` and warn if elevated when it shouldn't be. (6) Document the integrity-match rule in the script header and in any setup .bat — make it impossible to miss. (7) The diagnostic that surfaced this took 4 layers of indirection (CSV missing → JS auto-recovery → endpoint stdout capture → dashboard surfaced output) — invest in surfacing real script output to UI for any background pipeline, otherwise root causes stay opaque. (8) Reference: May 2 — `Get-CalendarEvents.ps1` failing with HRESULT 0x80080005; fixed by re-launching `Run-Update-Server.ps1` as regular user; 22 calendar events extracted successfully on next run; Phase 5 verified end-to-end (Generate Agenda + Extract MOM + Route to Action Log + JSON persistence + meeting auto-drafts all confirmed working by user).

613. **Cadence direction-changes (Sun→Mon, weekly→bi-weekly, etc.) require sweeping ~15 files across 3 tiers even when only ~7 calendar dates change — file naming, talking-points content, dashboard headers, design doc demo dates, manifest descriptions, Action Log JSON entries, memory files, and global lessons all get touched** — On 2026-05-03 user corrected the bi-weekly meeting day with Semir + Dr. Omar from "Sundays" to "Mondays" — every locked demo date shifted +1 day across an 18-week roadmap (May 4 → Nov 17). The naive expectation was "7 dates change → 7 edits". The reality: 15+ files, ~60+ surgical edits, plus a scheduling collision (Mon Jul 13 last v13 demo + Mon Jul 13 Phase A Electron build start) requiring a 1-day shift on the Electron roadmap (Tue Jul 14). **Why:** every prior day-of-week assumption is baked deep into artifact text — talking-points decks (titles, meta strips, opening narratives, Phase Roadmap tables, closing commitments, Change Log rows, AR translations), dashboard headers (BUILD LOG, Charter card "next review" pills, AR translation maps), design docs (build-window dates, demo dates, demo scripts), memory files (anchor day, confirmed dates), manifest descriptions, hardcoded auto-draft entries, lessons referencing "Sun {date}" demos. Filename naming may be locked by inbound-link integrity (e.g., Sunday_Talking_Points_vN had ~10 cross-references) — keep filenames, update content. **How to apply:** (1) Before declaring a cadence change "done", grep the entire workspace for the OLD day-of-week token + every OLD locked date — expect 60+ matches if the cadence is multi-month. (2) Ask 3 disambiguating questions BEFORE editing: (a) when is the immediate next demo, (b) does each future date shift +1 day or to nearest new-anchor, (c) any same-day collisions with locked downstream events (e.g., build-start dates). (3) Tier the work: Tier 1 = artifacts needed before next demo (1-2 days). Tier 2 = artifacts needed before next-next demo. Tier 3 = rolling artifacts touched per phase. Don't try to do all in one sitting. (4) Preserve historical artifacts as point-in-time records — DO NOT rewrite lesson #N to say "Mon X" when it actually said "Sun Y" at the time of the lesson. Lessons are records of what was true at write-time. Add a NEW lesson documenting the cadence-change instead. Same rule for Action Log JSON files written before the change. (5) Update memory file `meeting_cadence_*` first — that's the canonical anchor downstream sessions reference. (6) Use a 2-column "old/new date table" at the top of the planning doc so every artifact edit can verify against a single source of truth. (7) Reference: May 3 — Sun→Mon pivot affected v13 BUILD LOG header, STP v3-v8 (12 files), CLAUDE.md PMO RESTRUCTURE, meeting_cadence_biweekly.md memory, pmo_v13_phase4_finish_may1.md memory, 6 Phase A-F design docs, Roadmap v2, links_manifest, Transformation Plan, plus 1-day shift to Electron Phase A start (Mon Jul 13 → Tue Jul 14) to avoid same-day collision with last v13 demo.



614. **Pulling a multi-phase roadmap forward N weeks affects the same ~30 files as a cadence direction-change PLUS forces re-purpose of pre-built decks (not just date swaps) — and creates a dual-track demo pattern that's worth treating as its own deliverable** — On 2026-05-03 evening, immediately after completing the Sun→Mon cadence sweep (lesson #613), user pulled v2 Electron Phase A start forward 10 weeks (Tue Jul 14 → Sun May 3 today). Total roadmap compression: 18 weeks → ~16 weeks. v13 retirement pulled forward from Mon Nov 17 to Mon Aug 24 (3 months earlier). **Why this is more work than #613's cadence sweep:** date-swap scripts catch ~70% of references mechanically, but the remaining 30% require **content re-purpose** — STP decks pre-built for "Phase 5 reveal in v13" become "Phase B SQLite demo in v2 + Phase 5 as parity check" (whole narrative arc shifts). Phase A doc's Day 2-9 internal sequence (Wed Jul 15 / Thu Jul 16 / Fri-Sat Jul 17-18 weekend / Sun Jul 19 etc.) was hand-tuned for the original calendar week and needed manual remapping to the new May 3-14 calendar. **Why this is worth its own lesson vs lumping into #613:** the parallel-tracking pattern (v13 production + v2 Electron skeleton, both running simultaneously, dual-track demos) is a strategic pattern not a date sweep — pre-warn stakeholders ("you'll see two tracks Mon May 18"), reframe v13 phase reveals as feature-parity checks rather than separate demos, document the new schedule in a fresh memory file rather than overwriting old. **How to apply:** (1) Date-swap scripts should run THREE phases: (a) bulk regex pull-forward, (b) follow-up pass for internal day-by-day sequences, (c) manual review of pre-built decks for content re-purpose (not just date swaps). (2) Pre-warn stakeholders at the demo immediately preceding the dual-track demo: "Sunday I started parallel build; in 2 weeks you'll see both side-by-side." (3) Reframe original-stack reveals (v13 phases) as feature-parity checks against new-stack (v2) — same content but accompanies v2 progress rather than running standalone. (4) Capture the schedule shift in a NEW memory file (don't overwrite the old roadmap memory) so the original schedule remains accessible if the parallel build slips and reverts to original timing. (5) Solo capacity check: parallel-tracking 8-day Phase A build + ongoing demo prep + production daily-use of original system is heavy. Enforce Fri-Sat off + buffer days. If Phase A 8 days slips to 12, push first dual-track demo by 2 weeks — don't compress further. (6) Reference: May 3 — pulled v2 Electron Phase A start Tue Jul 14 → Sun May 3 (10 weeks); affected ~30 files across Roadmap v2.1→v2.2, 6 Phase design docs, STP v4-v9 decks, CLAUDE.md PMO RESTRUCTURE, 2 memory files, links_manifest. STP v4 reworked as DUAL TRACK deck (v13 compressed reveal + v2 Phase A FIRST DEMO). v13 retirement: Mon Nov 17 → Mon Aug 24.

615. **Phase A skeleton "Day 1 + Day 4 in same evening" is a viable shortcut when sample data is already structured (e.g., v13 PORTFOLIO_KNOWN map + Project_Register sidecar JSON) — no real-time fetch wired, just static render proves the toolchain end-to-end** — On 2026-05-03 evening, after scaffolding `D:\PMO_OS\` Day 1 (folders + 14 config + entry-point files + npm install clean), I jumped to Day 4 (PortfolioStatus 28-card render) in the same session. Total effort ~3 hours. **Why this works:** Day 4's "extract 28-project sample from v13 PORTFOLIO_KNOWN → src/data/projects-sample.json" can run independently of Day 2 (Electron BrowserWindow wiring) and Day 3 (Vite HMR confirmation) — the React component renders identically regardless of whether it's running inside Electron or in a plain browser via `vite dev`. Skipping Day 2-3 in the parallel session is fine because: (a) `vite build` clean = renderer is shippable, (b) `tsc --noEmit -p electron/tsconfig.json` clean = Electron main is shippable, (c) Day 2's actual work is just `npm run dev:all` wiring (~15 min) which can land Mon May 4 evening after the v10 demo. **Why the data part was easy:** v13's PORTFOLIO_KNOWN map (line 5815) only has 11 entries, but Project_Register_v2.json sidecar already has 30 entries with name/tier/stage/owner/eng-lead/sponsor/notes structure — a 50-line Python script extracted 28 active (excluded 2 containers) into a flat JSON. The React component groups by tier + applies confidence pills (🟢/🟡/🔴) + visual language matching v13 navy + accent colors. **How to apply:** (1) When scaffolding a new Electron+React app, check whether sample data is already structured in the source app — if yes, Day 1+Day 4 can land same session (pure-React component renders independently of Electron wiring). (2) Defer Day 2-3 (Electron BrowserWindow + Vite HMR) to next focused window — `npx vite build` + `npx tsc --noEmit -p electron/tsconfig.json` clean is sufficient evidence the toolchain works. (3) The Day-4 sample data should match the production data shape closely (same field names, same enum values) so Phase B's SQLite migration is mechanical. (4) Carry visual language tokens (colors, fonts) from the source app into the new Tailwind config so the dual-track demo doesn't look like two unrelated apps. (5) Reference: May 3 — `D:\PMO_OS\src\data\projects-sample.json` (28 entries from Project_Register_v2.json) + `src/components/PortfolioStatus.tsx` (78-line React component, tier-grouped grid, confidence pills) + Tailwind config carrying v13's navy `#1a1a2e` + accent `#0f3460` — all shipped same evening as the Day 1 scaffold.

616. **`ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE=1` in any shell forces the Electron binary to behave as plain Node — `process.type` becomes `undefined`, `require('electron')` returns the binary path string instead of the API object, and `app.isPackaged` throws `TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'isPackaged')` — the bug masquerades as a "broken main.ts" but the scaffold is correct, the env is poisoned** — On 2026-05-03 evening Phase A Day 2 smoke-test, ran `npm run dev:all` from a Claude Code bash shell — Electron crashed at `const isDev = !electron_1.app.isPackaged;` (compiled from `import { app } from 'electron'`). Direct `npx electron .` reproduced the same error. Probe script `console.log(typeof require('electron'), process.type)` from the Electron binary returned `'string'` and `undefined` — confirming Electron was NOT initializing the main-process context. Root cause discovered via `env | grep -i electron` → `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE=1` was set in the bash sandbox env. After PowerShell-level `Remove-Item Env:ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE` and re-launch, probe returned `'object'` + `'browser'` + 30 main-process API modules, and the full `npm run dev:all` chain worked: Vite ready in 484ms / Electron 5 processes spawned / DevTools auto-opened / app console clean. **Why:** `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE=1` is an official Electron env var that downgrades the Electron binary to a Node.js-compatible runtime. It strips out the main-process API layer entirely, so `require('electron')` falls through to the npm package's Node-resolution path — which exports the binary path as a string for tooling like `electron-builder` to locate the executable. The error message blames `main.ts`, but the scaffold is fine. The trap is that the env var can be set anywhere — Claude Code's bash sandbox (sets it for safety so agents don't spawn invisible GUIs), CI runners, IDE-integrated terminals, parent processes that launched Electron-as-Node for a separate purpose. **How to apply:** (1) When `app.isPackaged` (or any Electron API) throws "undefined" in a fresh scaffold that was working yesterday, FIRST check: `echo $ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE` (bash) or `$env:ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE` (PowerShell). If non-empty, that's the culprit. (2) Add an explicit env-var check at the top of `electron/main.ts`: `if (process.env.ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE) { console.error('FATAL: ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE is set — Electron is running as plain Node and cannot create BrowserWindows. Unset this env var and retry.'); process.exit(1); }` — fails fast with a clear message instead of an opaque TypeError. (3) Document the gotcha in the project's troubleshooting README + Phase F design doc — every Electron developer hits this once. (4) For Claude Code agent sessions specifically, **BOTH bash AND PowerShell sandboxes can inherit `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE=1`** — the original "prefer PowerShell" workaround was wrong. Verified 2026-05-03 evening Phase A Day 2 re-test: `$env:ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE` was non-empty in PowerShell sandbox (`Remove-Item Env:ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE` returned "Cleared ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE"). The only reliable defenses are (a) the `electron/main.ts` guard from item (2) above (fail-fast at process startup with a clear message), and (b) explicit unset BEFORE every launch regardless of shell — `unset ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE` (bash) or `Remove-Item Env:ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue` (PowerShell). A `pre*` npm script (e.g. `predev:all`) that runs this unset is a clean way to make the protection automatic so every `npm run dev:all` invocation is safe regardless of shell. (5) The diagnostic that surfaced this was the probe script (`require('electron')` shape inspection) — adopt this as a generic "is Electron really initializing?" health-check for any future bring-up. (6) Reference: May 3 evening — `D:\PMO_OS\` Phase A Day 2 smoke-test; bash launch failed with `app.isPackaged` TypeError; PowerShell launch ALSO had the env var set (verified 2026-05-03 evening re-test) — `Remove-Item Env:ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE` was required before launch succeeded end-to-end (window 1280×800, DevTools open, 28-card render, console clean). Scaffold (`electron/main.ts`, `package.json`, `dist-electron/main.js`) was 100% correct in both attempts — both Claude Code sandbox shells were poisoned.

617. **`electron-builder` NSIS pipeline on Windows 11 requires Developer Mode (or Administrator) to extract `winCodeSign` cache — the cache contains macOS dylib symlinks (`darwin/10.12/lib/libcrypto.dylib`, `libssl.dylib`) that Windows refuses to create without `SeCreateSymbolicLinkPrivilege` — symptom is "ERROR: Cannot create symbolic link : A required privilege is not held by the client" failing 7za extraction across multiple cache hash dirnames** — On 2026-05-03 evening Phase A Day 5 build, `npm run build` failed with the symlink error during winCodeSign extraction. Setting `CSC_IDENTITY_AUTO_DISCOVERY=false` did NOT help — electron-builder still downloads + extracts winCodeSign even when not signing (used for resource embedding). Each retry redownloaded into a fresh hash subdir (272593323, 209868651, 399210431, 531272203 — different per run). `electron-builder --dir` mode also fails with the same symlink trap. Only fix: enable Windows Developer Mode (`HKLM:\SOFTWARE\Microsoft\Windows\CurrentVersion\AppModelUnlock\AllowDevelopmentWithoutDevLicense = 1`) which grants `SeCreateSymbolicLinkPrivilege` to non-admin processes. After enabling + clearing the broken cache (`Remove-Item -Recurse -Force "$env:LOCALAPPDATA\electron-builder\Cache\winCodeSign"`), the same `npm run build` succeeded end-to-end producing `release/PMO_OS_Setup_v2.0.0.exe` (74 MB NSIS installer) + `release/win-unpacked/PMO OS.exe` (177 MB). **Why:** electron-builder unconditionally extracts winCodeSign because the bundle contains binaries used for both code-signing AND resource manipulation (icon embedding, version-info patching). The macOS dylib symlinks inside the bundle are needed for cross-platform builds — Windows-only builds don't actually use them but still need them to extract. Windows symlink creation is a privileged operation by default; Developer Mode lifts that requirement specifically for app development. **How to apply:** (1) Before any `npm run build` involving electron-builder on a fresh Windows machine, FIRST verify Developer Mode is enabled: `(Get-ItemProperty "HKLM:\SOFTWARE\Microsoft\Windows\CurrentVersion\AppModelUnlock").AllowDevelopmentWithoutDevLicense` should return `1`. (2) If not, enable via Settings → Privacy & Security → For developers → Developer Mode = On (UAC prompt + ~30s install). One-time, persists across reboots. (3) `CSC_IDENTITY_AUTO_DISCOVERY=false` does NOT help with this — don't waste time trying. (4) `electron-builder --dir` does NOT help either — same symlink path is hit. (5) When the error appears mid-build, clear the broken cache before retrying: `Remove-Item -Recurse -Force "$env:LOCALAPPDATA\electron-builder\Cache\winCodeSign"`. (6) Document the prerequisite in the project's setup README so future contributors don't hit it cold. (7) Reference: May 3 evening — `D:\PMO_OS\` Phase A Day 5 build; failed twice with symlink error before Developer Mode enable; succeeded immediately after with cache cleared.

618. **npm `pre*` script + tiny `.cjs` helper is the cleanest pattern for ENV-VAR / TOOL-PRESENCE preflight guards — 1 line of script + 5-10 line script file = automatic protection on every invocation, exit 1 with clear message if precondition fails** — On 2026-05-03 evening, after lesson #616 amend recommended a `predev:all` pre-script for `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE` defense, shipped `D:\PMO_OS\scripts\check-electron-env.cjs` (12 lines: check env, log clear error with both bash + PowerShell unset commands, exit 1) + added `"predev:all": "node scripts/check-electron-env.cjs"` to package.json. Verified both gates: `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE=1 npm run dev:all` exits 1 with the formatted error; clean env exits 0 and falls through to `dev:all`. **Why:** npm's `pre*` hook runs the same-named script before any `npm run X` invocation, in a child Node process — perfect for cheap preflight checks. Compared to alternatives: (a) `electron/main.ts` guard fires only AFTER electron starts (already had this, but it's reactive not proactive); (b) shell wrapper script requires user education to use; (c) ESLint rule requires running lint separately; (d) git pre-commit hook only fires on commit, not on dev launches. The `pre*` pattern is invisible to users (no extra command to remember), platform-agnostic (Node runs everywhere), and cheap (sub-50ms when passing). **How to apply:** (1) For any precondition that must hold before `npm run X` (env vars, tool presence, file existence, OS feature like Developer Mode), write a `pre<X>` script. (2) Body of pre-script = a tiny `.cjs` helper at `scripts/check-<thing>.cjs` so the package.json line stays one-line and the logic is testable in isolation. (3) When precondition fails, exit 1 + emit a clear error with: (a) what's wrong, (b) why it matters, (c) exact remediation commands for both shells, (d) reference to the underlying lesson/doc. (4) Pair with a runtime guard (lesson #616 item 2) for defense in depth — pre-script catches at launch, runtime guard catches if env was set after pre-check. (5) Reference: May 3 evening — `D:\PMO_OS\scripts\check-electron-env.cjs` + `package.json` `predev:all` line, paired with `electron/main.ts` line-19 fail-fast guard.

619. **Multi-resolution Windows ICO files can be built from scratch in PowerShell using System.Drawing + manual ICO header assembly — no external tools (ImageMagick, Inkscape, sharp) needed — useful when scaffolding Electron/desktop apps that need an installer-ready icon and you only have an SVG or color description** — On 2026-05-03 evening Phase A Day 6, needed `D:\PMO_OS\build\icon.ico` for electron-builder NSIS. Generated 6-size ICO (256/128/64/48/32/16) matching v13's chart-bars favicon (navy #1a1a2e bg + 4 ascending bars in green/amber/blue/red) directly via PowerShell: `Add-Type -AssemblyName System.Drawing` → loop sizes → for each, create `Bitmap`, `Graphics.Clear` to navy, `Graphics.FillRectangle` for each bar with proportional positioning → save each as PNG to MemoryStream → manually assemble ICO with `BinaryWriter`: 6-byte ICONDIR header (reserved=0, type=1, count=N) + 16-byte ICONDIRENTRY per size (width/height as bytes; 0 means 256, otherwise size; 32-bpp PNG payload pointer + length) + concatenated PNG bytes. Result: 4 KB valid Windows icon resource accepted by electron-builder. `file build/icon.ico` confirms "MS Windows icon resource - 6 icons, 256x256 with PNG image data". **Why:** ICO format is well-documented and trivial to assemble. Windows accepts PNG-payload ICOs since Vista. Avoids dependency on ImageMagick (heavy install), online converters (privacy concern, unreliable), or sharp (Node-only, build-step). Pure PowerShell + .NET = always available on Windows. Multi-res ensures the icon looks crisp at every Windows touchpoint (taskbar, alt-tab, file explorer, About dialog, installer wizard). **How to apply:** (1) For any scaffold needing a placeholder/permanent icon, generate via PS one-liner inline rather than committing a binary you got from elsewhere. (2) Always emit multi-res (256/128/64/48/32/16) — Windows picks the right size per context. (3) Use 32-bit RGBA for transparent backgrounds (set Bitmap pixel format to `Format32bppArgb`). (4) Save each size as PNG inside the ICO (vs uncompressed BMP) — shrinks file 5-10× without quality loss. (5) Verify with `file path\icon.ico` — should report "MS Windows icon resource - N icons" where N matches your count. (6) Match visual language to source app's design tokens (color palette, shape grammar) for visual continuity. (7) Reference: May 3 evening — `D:\PMO_OS\build\icon.ico` (4 KB, 6 sizes) + companion `icon-256.png` (1.8 KB) generated from PS in ~5 seconds; visual = direct port of v13 favicon SVG at `PMO Dashboard_v13.html` line 93.

620. **Multi-repo workspace pattern — separate local-only git repos per "project shape" — shared workspace `D:\Project Management\` (governance artifacts, design docs, STP decks, dashboards) + product workspace `D:\PMO_OS\` (Electron + React product code) — keeps audit-trail discrete per concern + sidesteps cross-tracking ambiguity** — On 2026-05-03 evening, after committing STP v3-v11 + v13 BUILD LOG to `D:\Project Management\` master (`a01bd341`), realized `D:\PMO_OS\` was on a different drive path AND had no `.git/` yet. Initialized fresh local repo: `git init` → `.gitignore` already pre-configured (excluded `node_modules/`, `dist/`, `dist-electron/`, `release/`) → added two more excludes (`vite.config.{js,d.ts}` tsc derivatives + `.vite/` dev cache) → `git add -A` (24 files staged) → root commit `a99f5e5` with full Phase A baseline + Days 5-6 narrative. Two parallel local repos now: governance/PM at `D:\Project Management\` (master with 4 prior commits + today's `a01bd341`) and product code at `D:\PMO_OS\` (master with single root commit `a99f5e5`). **Why:** trying to track product code inside a PM workspace repo creates two problems — (a) `.gitignore` semantics differ (PM workspace has typed-narrative MD files that should never be ignored; product workspace needs aggressive `node_modules/` ignoring), (b) commit history gets noisy (governance edits interleaved with code edits make `git log` hard to read for either audience). Separate repos = clean per-domain history + correct ignore rules + each can grow at its own cadence. **How to apply:** (1) When a new "project shape" appears in your workspace (e.g., a buildable product alongside a docs folder), `git init` it as its own repo from day one rather than tracking it within a parent workspace repo. (2) Pre-stage the `.gitignore` BEFORE first `git add` — saves a follow-up "remove tracked file from index" edit. (3) Local-only is fine per CLAUDE.md DEFAULT-BRANCH PUSH RULE for solo workspaces (no remote setup needed until/unless multi-machine or multi-author). (4) When committing across repos in the same session, use distinct commit message prefixes (e.g. `pmo:` for PM workspace, `phase-a:` for product) so cross-repo `git log` greps are unambiguous. (5) Document the multi-repo layout in CLAUDE.md or a workspace README so future-you doesn't ask "where does this live?". (6) Reference: May 3 evening — `D:\Project Management\` repo at `a01bd341` (PMO governance) + `D:\PMO_OS\` repo at `a99f5e5` (Electron product) initialized in same session; both master, both local-only, both auto-ignored derivatives.

621. **Jump-list dashboards with hardcoded `_v{N}.html` cross-references go stale silently — every parent dashboard linking to versioned siblings needs an audit pass after each cadence-shift OR sibling-version-bump, because the `href` is just a string with no auto-resolve** — On 2026-05-03 evening, user spotted that PMO Dashboard v13's "Local PM Dashboards" tab opened `OKR_KPI_Dashboard_v8.html` when the latest on disk was `_v10.html`. Audit revealed: only 1 of 11 cards was stale (OKR fell behind by 2 versions). The other 10 cards (Q2 PM Plan v8, Quarterly Performance v1, Cairo Quarterly v1, Bi-Weekly Agenda v7, Outlook Email v13, Department Workflows Manual V5, Claude Courses v31, DevOps Best Practice PDF V.1, DevOps Access Upgrade v7, Department Workflows & Roles V2) were current — the system mostly works, but staleness is invisible until someone clicks. **Why:** versioned filenames are an audit-trail pattern (every meaningful change → new file), but cross-dashboard links are point-in-time pointers that don't auto-track. Each jump-list href is just a static string; nothing on disk verifies the target is "latest". When a sibling deliverable bumps version (e.g. OKR shipped v9 then v10 on Apr 30), the parent dashboard's link doesn't notice. The pattern silently drifts until a user clicks an old card and realizes they're seeing month-old data. The audit is cheap (Glob per card folder + compare filename) but has to be triggered — there's no error, no warning, no console log. **How to apply:** (1) After ANY sibling deliverable version-bump, audit every dashboard that contains a jump-list of cross-references. Cheapest way: maintain a `links_manifest.json` that lists every cross-reference + its current target version, and run a `check-link-drift.ps1` script that Globs each card folder and reports stale entries. (2) When NEW cards are added to a jump-list, embed the version number in the description text (e.g. "v10 — live DevOps→label sync") so visual drift is obvious to readers even before clicks. (3) Consider a `latest` symlink convention OR a `?v=current` query-string pattern if the staleness becomes recurring — but for slow-cadence files (bi-weekly bumps), manual audit + a calendar reminder is sufficient. (4) When a cadence shift happens (lesson #613) OR a roadmap pull-forward happens (lesson #614) OR a sibling-version sweep happens, ADD "audit cross-dashboard jump-lists" as a checklist item — these are the moments staleness compounds. (5) The audit pattern: Glob latest version per folder → compare to href in parent → fix any mismatches in one Edit pass. ~2 min per parent dashboard. (6) Reference: May 3 — PMO Dashboard v13 line 2935 stale `OKR_KPI_Dashboard_v8.html` → fixed to `_v10.html`; remaining 10 cards verified current via 9-Glob batch.

622. **Hardcoded `localhost:<PORT>` integration constants drift silently when (a) the planned service was never built, OR (b) the service was built on a different port and the constant was never updated — every dashboard with `var SERVER = 'http://localhost:NNNN'` deserves a workspace-wide grep audit alongside the port-registry source-of-truth (CLAUDE.md ports list)** — On 2026-05-03 evening, the OKR v10 PM-Dashboard badge stayed "offline" because line 1677 hardcoded `var PM_DASHBOARD = 'http://localhost:3000/api'` while the actual Azure DevOps cache server (`DevOps_Cache_Server.ps1`) listens on port **5051**. CLAUDE.md's reserved-ports list (5050 Outlook / 5051 Standalone PM / 5052 Taqat / 5053 D Drive Scanner) had NO 3000 entry — confirming the 3000 reference was either scaffolded for an unbuilt Bun server OR a stale port from before a migration. Sweeping the workspace for `localhost:3000` returned 51 files; classifying them caught a SECOND silent bug: Outlook v13 line 1155 had `var PM_API = 'http://localhost:3000/api'` for Deep Search, but the actual `/api/search` endpoint lives on port 5050 (`Run-Update-Server.ps1`). Both bugs were silent because: (a) badge probe `.catch(...)` just sets badge text to "offline" without surfacing the actual error, (b) Deep Search would only fail when a user typed in the search box AND toggled deep mode AND the server happened to be down — a user could go weeks without hitting it. Fix: repointed both files to their correct ports + verified endpoint shapes match (`/health` + `/cache` exist on 5051; `/api/search` exists on 5050). **Why:** when a workspace has multiple local services, the port-to-service mapping must live in ONE source of truth (CLAUDE.md ports section) and every integration constant must be cross-checked against it. Hardcoded URL strings are pre-mortem candidates for drift — they survive code reviews because they look correct in isolation, but a port-registry audit catches the mismatch immediately. **How to apply:** (1) Maintain a "Localhost ports" section in CLAUDE.md with PORT → SERVICE → SCRIPT triples (current registry: 5050 Outlook+Search/Run-Update-Server.ps1, 5051 DevOps cache/DevOps_Cache_Server.ps1, 5052 Taqat, 5053 D Drive). (2) Periodically grep the workspace for `localhost:` and verify every hit corresponds to a registered port. (3) For dashboards with multiple integrations (Outlook v13 has TWO: 5050 main + 3000 search), use distinct named constants (`API`, `PM_API`, `CACHE_SERVER`) and document each comment-side which script serves it. (4) When introducing a new service, add to the port registry FIRST, then write the dashboard integration. (5) When deprecating a service or migrating ports, grep + update ALL references same session — don't trust "I'll find them later". (6) Add a runtime visible-error pattern to badge probes: when probe fails, badge shows red "Server X@5051: not responding" instead of grey "offline" — the port number in the message exposes the integration intent so users can verify against the registry without reading source. (7) Reference: May 3 — fixed OKR v10 line 1677 (3000→5051) + Outlook v13 line 1155 (3000→5050), companion to lesson #621's "audit cross-dashboard jump-lists" — same audit-class but for endpoint URLs not file paths.

623. **`new Date(value + 'Z')` is a JavaScript NaN trap when the source already includes timezone — PowerShell `(Get-Date).ToUniversalTime().ToString('o')` produces `2026-05-03T17:42:21.0000000+00:00` which ends with `+00:00` (NOT `Z`); appending another `Z` yields `2026-05-03T...+00:00Z` → invalid → NaN.getTime()` propagates as `NaN` arithmetic** — On 2026-05-03 evening after fixing OKR v10's port (#622), the badge displayed `Cache: NaNm old • 27 projects` — server reachable but staleness math broken. Root cause: line 1778 had `var age = (Date.now() - new Date(data.lastFetched + 'Z').getTime()) / 60000;`. The PS server emitted `lastFetched` as ISO-8601 with timezone-offset suffix already present (PowerShell's `o` format produces `+00:00` for ToUniversalTime, NOT `Z`). Adding `+ 'Z'` corrupted the string. Fix: removed the `+ 'Z'` append. JavaScript `Date` parses both `Z` and `+00:00` correctly when given clean input. **Why:** the `+ 'Z'` defensive concatenation was likely added when the original API returned a tz-naive timestamp (e.g. some Node/Express servers default to `new Date().toISOString().slice(0, -1)` without the Z). When the producer changes to a timestamp library that emits full timezone info, the defensive concatenation becomes a corruption. Type-coercion silence: `NaN` flows through arithmetic without throwing, so the bug surfaces only as visible "NaNm" in the UI — easy to miss in a quick test. **How to apply:** (1) NEVER blindly append `'Z'` to a timestamp string you don't control. (2) When parsing timestamps from a server response, FIRST log `data.lastFetched` (or equivalent) raw to console — confirm whether it ends with `Z`, `+HH:MM`, or no offset. (3) If tz-naive, use `+ 'Z'` to assert UTC; if tz-aware, parse as-is. (4) Add a defensive guard: `var ms = new Date(value).getTime(); if (isNaN(ms)) { /* fallback or log */ }` — surfaces the bug instead of producing visible NaN strings in UI. (5) When swapping backend servers (e.g. OKR v10 was originally pointed at a Node/Bun service on 3000, now repointed to PowerShell on 5051), ALWAYS test timestamp-handling code paths since timestamp formatting is per-language conventional. (6) PowerShell's `ToString('o')` is the standard "round-trip" format and emits ISO-8601 with timezone — embracing it on the JS side requires NO concatenation. (7) Reference: May 3 — OKR v10 line 1778 `new Date(data.lastFetched + 'Z')` → `new Date(data.lastFetched)` after PS server's `o`-format already supplied `+00:00` offset.

## Bilingual EN/AR translation completion via Playwright self-audit (May 4, 2026)

624. **Static analysis under-counts bilingual translation gaps by 5-10× — the only reliable audit of "is this fully translated?" is to spawn the file in a headless browser, toggle to AR, walk every tab, and scan for English text NOT inside `[data-i18n]` ancestors via `TreeWalker(SHOW_TEXT)`** — On 2026-05-04, after the user asked "is v13 fully translated in Arabic?", a static `pm-bilingual-qa` agent reported "PARTIALLY TRANSLATED — 14 gap locations". Built v14 with those 14 fixes, but when the user asked Playwright to verify, the headless browser surfaced **440+ additional unresolved data-i18n keys + 200+ JS-rendered English strings across 18 tabs** that the static agent never saw. The 14-gap initial report was 5% of reality. The true scope only emerges from runtime inspection. **Why:** static grep on `[A-Za-z]{3,}` matches PROPER NOUNS, code paths, English keys-as-text, and template literals identically — too noisy to filter manually. Static parsing of `data-i18n=...` finds attribute presence but not whether the key EXISTS in the T dictionary. Static analysis can't see the difference between `el.textContent = 'Loading...'` (translatable) and `el.textContent = data.someField` (data passthrough). Only at runtime, with a real T dictionary loaded, can you classify `dgT(key) === key` ⇒ unresolved vs resolved, AND walk visible text nodes filtered by `data-i18n` ancestor. **How to apply:** (1) For any bilingual deliverable claim ("is this translated?"), respond with "I'll verify in a browser" rather than running a static reviewer agent — the static agent is calibrated for structural issues (paired-span integrity, dir attribute presence) not coverage. (2) Standard verification harness: spawn HTTP server (Python `python -m http.server`) at the dashboard's folder; Playwright `browser_navigate` to the file; `browser_evaluate` `window.toggleLang()` to AR; iterate tabs via `window.showTab(id)`; run two scans per tab — (a) `[data-i18n]` keys where `dgT(k) === k` for missing dictionary entries, (b) `TreeWalker(SHOW_TEXT)` filtered by `!ancestor.hasAttribute('data-i18n')` AND `/[A-Za-z]{3,}/` AND `!/[؀-ۿ]/` for JS-rendered English leaks. (3) Patch in batched rounds (~50-100 keys per round), reload, re-scan — convergence usually takes 4-7 rounds. (4) Each round narrows what's left: Round 1 finds chrome/section labels, Round 2 finds modals + toasts, Round 3 finds table cells, Round 4 finds auto-draft/template strings, Round 5+ finds long-tail (status messages, error text, EVM glossary, threshold legends). (5) Acceptable remaining English at convergence: proper nouns (people, project names, brand names), code/file paths (`powershell.exe`, `_data/...`, `window.HOURLY_RATE`), date strings (need locale-aware conversion which is a separate concern), pre-existing localStorage entries with empty `*Ar` fields (legacy migration), mathematical formulas (`SPI = EV / PV`). (6) Reference: May 4 — v14 final state 798 data-i18n attributes, 0 unresolved, ~250 new T entries vs v13. The static-analysis report's "14 gaps" was 5% of the true 250+ scope; only Playwright surfaced the rest.

625. **The `data-i18n + applyLang(textContent swap)` pattern silently corrupts inline HTML on EN→AR→EN toggle cycle — `el.setAttribute('data-i18n-original', el.textContent)` captures plain text only, so any `<code>`, `<strong>`, or other inline elements inside the translatable span are LOST after the first toggle back to EN; security hooks block the obvious fix (innerHTML); the safe pattern is to break HTML elements OUT of the i18n span** — On 2026-05-04 while wrapping setup notes that contained `<code>D:\Project Management\...</code>` paths, the obvious fix was to upgrade `applyLang` to use `innerHTML` for both stash and restore. Security hook intercepted with "Setting innerHTML with untrusted content can lead to XSS". Even though T is hardcoded by the developer (not user input), the hook is a hard guard. The clean alternative: restructure HTML so `<code>` / `<strong>` / `<a>` live OUTSIDE the data-i18n span. **Why:** `el.textContent` is a getter that flattens DOM to plain text. Inline elements become invisible after the first toggle. AR string with `<code>...</code>` literal renders the tags as visible text (readers see `<code>D:\\...</code>` not styled code). Going back to EN restores ONLY the captured plain-text — original `<code>` styling lost. This is silent corruption. Security hook blocks the innerHTML solution because the hook can't distinguish "developer-hardcoded T" from "user input concatenated into T". Restructuring is the only path. **How to apply:** (1) When wrapping a paragraph that contains inline elements: split the text into 3+ adjacent spans, putting inline elements (`<code>`, `<strong>`, `<a>`) BETWEEN spans not INSIDE: `<span data-i18n="setup-1">Email tab needs the shared</span> <code>Run-Update-Server.ps1</code> <span data-i18n="setup-2">running on port 5050...</span> <code>D:\\...</code> <span data-i18n="setup-3">once.</span>` (2) Each span's textContent is pure plain text; `applyLang` swaps cleanly; the `<code>` outside survives EN↔AR toggle. (3) Naming convention: `<key>-1`, `<key>-2`, `<key>-3` for sequential pieces of one logical paragraph. Add to T as separate entries. (4) Cost: 3 keys per paragraph instead of 1, plus careful AR translations that grammatically make sense when concatenated with the inline-HTML breakpoints. (5) Trade-off accepted: AR translation is slightly less natural (sentences split at HTML boundaries) but the toggle is stable. (6) Reference: May 4 — `email-tab-setup-1`/`-2`/`-3`, `meetings-tab-setup-1`/`-2`/`-3`, `board-pack-setup-1`/`-2`, `briefing-tab-setup-1`/`-2`. Each replaces a single `data-i18n="x-tab-setup"` that had `<code>` tags inside. Pattern applied across 4 setup-notes paragraphs.

626. **Auto-draft entries written to localStorage BEFORE bilingual schema extension carry empty `actionAr/reasonAr` fields forever — there is no automatic backfill; render path must fall back to EN gracefully + accept "legacy entries stay English in AR mode" as a permanent migration debt** — On 2026-05-04 during v14 build, extended `buildDraft()` + 5 auto-draft scan loops (Data Gate / SPI / CPI / Velocity / SBI / Email-Reply / Email-Mention / Agenda / MOM-Missing) to populate parallel `actionAr` and `reasonAr` strings via T-dictionary template substitution. New entries from May 4 onward have full bilingual coverage. But the user's existing `localStorage['pmo-action-log-v1']` already contained 44+ auto-draft entries from v13 builds — those have `action: '[DRAFT — ...]'` populated and `actionAr: undefined`. In AR mode, the render line `(isAr && e.actionAr) ? e.actionAr : (e.action||'')` correctly falls back to English for legacy entries. Visible result: legacy entries display English action/reason text in AR mode, new entries display Arabic. **Why:** localStorage is a write-once snapshot per session. Schema additions don't auto-trigger a re-draft of historical entries. Templates that populate `actionAr` only fire when the auto-draft scans run AND the entry's de-dup key isn't in `blockingTuples`. Existing entries remain blocked from re-creation indefinitely. The graceful-fallback pattern (`e.actionAr || e.action`) is the standard solution: avoid rendering empty strings, accept temporary visual inconsistency until natural turnover (closures, migrations) ages out legacy data. **How to apply:** (1) When extending an entry schema with bilingual parallel fields, ALWAYS implement render-side OR-fallback: `(isAr && e.fieldAr) ? e.fieldAr : (e.field || '')`. (2) Don't write a "migration backfill" pass — it would have to re-run the entire auto-draft logic against historical state, which the snapshot data may no longer contain. (3) Document the migration debt: explicitly note in commit/memory that "legacy entries stay English; new entries get AR; turnover ages this out within ~30-60 days". (4) Optionally: write a one-shot console helper `window.backfillActionLogAr()` that loops `pmo-action-log-v1`, runs each entry's `indicator` through the corresponding template, and patches in `actionAr/reasonAr` if missing — but only if the data needed for substitution (project name, value, deviation) is still on the entry. Most cases it is (those fields are persisted), so backfill is doable but tedious. (5) Reference: May 4 — v14 Action Log render line ~10473 implements the fallback. Memory file `pmo_v14_arabic_completion.md` documents migration debt under "Out of scope". User accepted "legacy entries stay English" as known debt.

627. **The `option` element's `data-i18n` works for plain-text option labels but breaks if the option contains inline `<span>` children — `<option value="x"><span data-i18n="key">Label</span></option>` does NOT translate because option text is rendered atomically by the browser; only `<option data-i18n="key">Label</option>` (attribute on option itself) flips correctly via `el.textContent = T[key]`** — On 2026-05-04 wrapping Action Log filter dropdowns, initial fix tried `<option value="project">📁 <span data-i18n="al-scope-project">Project</span></option>` to keep the emoji + add translation. The span never rendered (option rendering is text-only at the OS level), and applyLang's `[data-i18n]` selector found the span but textContent swap on a child of a `<select>` option had no visible effect. **Why:** HTML `<option>` is rendered by the browser's native widget system using only the textContent of the option, not its child DOM. Spans inside options are stripped to text. Applying data-i18n to a child of `<option>` wraps a phantom: applyLang finds the span and updates it, but the dropdown widget continues showing the original option.textContent (cached at form-render time on most browsers). Only the option's OWN `data-i18n` attribute works, and only by setting `option.textContent = T[key]` on the option element directly. **How to apply:** (1) Put `data-i18n` directly on `<option>` element, not on a child `<span>`. (2) Sacrifice inline emoji prefixes — either bake the emoji INTO the AR translation string (`'al-scope-project': '📁 مشروع'`), OR drop the emoji from the option text entirely. (3) Working pattern: `<option value="project" data-i18n="al-scope-project">📁 Project</option>` with T entry `'al-scope-project': '📁 مشروع'`. (4) Verify in browser: open the dropdown, toggle EN↔AR, confirm the option text actually changes (don't trust DevTools-element-inspector, which shows the underlying span value not the rendered option text). (5) Reference: May 4 — Action Log filters at line ~2202-2205 use this pattern; T entries `al-scope-project`/`al-scope-team`/`al-scope-assignee`/`al-scope-email` include the emoji directly so the option flips visually as a single unit.

628. **Tracking unresolved-keys WITHOUT keeping a stable AR-mode reference returns false-positive "440 missing" results because `dgT(key)` returns the English key when `lang === 'en'` — the audit script must explicitly assert `applyLang('ar')` before scanning, OR check both directions (`document.documentElement.getAttribute('lang') === 'ar' && dgT(k) === k`)** — On 2026-05-04 first audit run after Round 2 patches reported 440 unresolved keys; second run after Round 3 reported 0. Nothing changed in the dictionary between runs. The explanation: the toggle had flipped the page back to EN between Round 2 and Round 3 (because of `localStorage` persistence + a manual `window.toggleLang()` call). When `lang === 'en'`, `dgT(key)` returns the input key as-is (it's the EN fallback), so EVERY data-i18n attribute appears "unresolved" if the script equality-checks `dgT(k) === k`. The audit was telling the truth about what dgT does in EN mode, but interpreting that as "missing translation" was false. **Why:** `dgT` is a contextual translator — it inspects the current `lang` attribute. The unresolved-detection pattern only makes sense when `lang === 'ar'`. Forgetting to assert AR mode produces a confidently-wrong report. **How to apply:** (1) Audit script MUST assert AR mode at the top: `if (document.documentElement.getAttribute('lang') !== 'ar') window.toggleLang();` before any `dgT(k) === k` check. (2) Better: write the audit to be lang-independent — directly inspect `window.T` if exposed, otherwise wrap `dgT` calls inside a manual `applyLang('ar')` → check → `applyLang('en')` if needed pattern. (3) Add a sanity print at the top of the report: `lang: 'ar', dir: 'rtl'` — confirms the audit ran in the right state. (4) When the report shows suspiciously many missing keys (>50% of total), treat as "audit ran in wrong mode" first hypothesis, dictionary gap second hypothesis. (5) Reference: May 4 — initial run reported 440 missing → re-asserted AR mode → real count was 6 (5 nav-section keys + 1 typo). Pattern formalized into the standard audit harness in lesson #624.

629. **Memory files describing "Day 1 scaffold" or "current phase" go stale within hours when delivery is moving fast — verify file system reality with Read/Glob BEFORE recommending from memory, especially when memory says "skeleton with 1 tab" but the actual `App.tsx` has 19 tab imports** — On 2026-05-04 user asked "v14 vs PMO_OS Data Gate parity?" My memory file (`pmo_v2_electron_parallel_build_may3.md` written ~16 hours earlier) said PMO_OS was a Day-1 skeleton with only PortfolioStatus tab. I almost answered "PMO_OS has no Data Gate yet" based on memory alone. Catching myself, I read `D:\PMO_OS\src\App.tsx` and found 19 tab imports including `<DataGate />`, plus `DataGate.tsx` (499 lines) + `DataGateProjectDetail.tsx` (230 lines) + `lib/devops-fetch.ts` all live. The Phase A→B→C→D→E phased roadmap had been overtaken by vertical-slice delivery in <24 hours. **Why:** Memory entries are point-in-time snapshots. When a project is in compressed-delivery mode (e.g., the 16-week roadmap collapsing to "ship every tab vertically in days"), the gap between memory write-time and read-time is enough to invalidate confident claims about file presence, line counts, tab inventories. Recommending against the memory snapshot leads to false-negative answers ("X doesn't exist yet") that confuse the user and damage trust. **How to apply:** (1) For ANY question about "does X exist in Y?" or "what's the current state of Z?", treat memory as a hint not as truth — verify with Read on the actual file before answering. (2) After verifying, immediately update the stale memory file with the new reality (don't leave it for "later"). (3) Add an explicit "Reality check (DATE)" section to memory files that describe phase/status snapshots so future sessions see the override clearly. (4) When memory contradicts file reality, trust the file and update the memory in the same turn. (5) Standing standard: any memory line beginning with "Phase X is currently…" or "Day N…" or "scaffold has 1 tab…" should trigger a verification pass before quotes. Reference: May 4 — PMO_OS memory said "Day 1 scaffold + 1 tab"; reality was 19 tabs wired with tRPC + SQLite + DevOps fetch.

630. **Adopting `useTranslation` in a React component that has `array.map((t) => …)` patterns silently breaks the component because the loop variable `t` shadows the i18n `t` — TypeScript catches the type mismatch ONLY when a `t.someStringField` happens to also be a valid translation key path, otherwise it compiles silently and renders junk** — On 2026-05-04 porting `DataGateProjectDetail.tsx` to bilingual EN/AR via `const { t } = useTranslation()`. Existing code had `filtered.map((t) => { … <td>{t.id}</td> <td title={t.title}> … })`. After adding `const { t } = useTranslation()` at the component top, the inner `t` variables shadow the i18n `t`. The first usage I added — `title={t('dataGate.detail.openInDevOps')}` — was inside the same `.map` callback, so it called `t('…')` on the TaskDetail object, which is `undefined`. **Why:** JavaScript scoping has the loop variable shadowing the outer const. TypeScript catches the call-as-function-on-non-function type error, BUT only at compile time and ONLY for direct calls like `t('…')`. References like `<td>{t.id}</td>` still work (they refer to the loop var). The dangerous case is when half the references resolve to the loop var (correctly) and half resolve via shadowing chaos — the file looks correct on read-through but renders broken at runtime. **How to apply:** (1) BEFORE adding `useTranslation`, grep the target file for `\\((t)\\b` and `\\b\\bt\\.` patterns to find all loop-var conflicts. (2) Rename loop variables to non-`t` names (`task`, `tk`, `item`, etc.) BEFORE adding the `t` import. Do this as a separate edit to make it review-able. (3) Re-grep after the `useTranslation` call is added to confirm zero `t.id`-style references remain that should have been the i18n function. (4) `npx tsc --noEmit` catches the most obvious failures (`t is not callable`) but won't catch every misuse — run a Vite build AND visually inspect the rendered tab if possible. (5) Reference: May 4 — caught after fixing line 207's `#{task.id}` (renamed) but missing `t.type`/`t.state`/`t.title`/`t.assignee`/`t.originalEstimate`/`t.completedWork` on lines 210-228; secondary occurrence in `tasks.filter((t) => t.state === s)` on line 146. Always do the rename pass first.

631. **Per-cell click-through with preset filter + in-modal switchable chip is a richer interaction than row-onClick — every numeric value becomes its own "drill into THIS subset" hyperlink, and the preset filter is just a pre-selected chip that the user can change without closing the modal** — On 2026-05-04 user asked: "when I click Project show all tasks; click Open show only open; click Miss Est show missing-estimate only; click Miss Act show missing-actual only; all values should be hyperlinks". Originally the row had a single `<tr onClick>` opening the modal with no preset filter. Refactor: replaced row-onClick with per-cell `<button>` links — Project → `gap=all`, Open → `gap=open`, Miss Est → `gap=missing-est`, Miss Act → `gap=missing-act`, Miss Either → `gap=missing-either`. The drill-down modal accepts `initialGapFilter` prop, applies it via `passesGapFilter()`, and renders a 5-chip filter row above the existing state-filter row. Chips show live counts. The preset is just the chip that's selected on first render; the user clicks any other chip to switch without reopening. **Why this design wins:** (1) Each cell answers a different question — clicking "5" on Miss Est means "show me those 5"; clicking "12" on Open means "show me those 12 open tasks"; row-click is a less precise gesture. (2) Visual hyperlink styling (`text-accent hover:underline`) telegraphs that the value is actionable. (3) Greying out zero-count cells (`disabled` button + `text-fg-muted/40`) prevents pointless modal opens. (4) In-modal switchable chips let the user reframe the same data without modal-flicker. **How to apply:** (1) Convert row-onClick patterns to per-cell buttons whenever each cell carries semantic information that maps to a filterable subset. (2) Style cell buttons as hyperlinks (`text-accent hover:underline cursor-pointer`) — users recognize the color affordance. (3) Disable cells whose value is 0 or N/A — saves wasted clicks. (4) The modal's filter UI should display the current preset as a selected chip, not as static text — communicates "this is changeable". (5) Show live counts per chip so users see filter outcomes before clicking. (6) Reference: May 4 — `DataGate.tsx` row → 5 buttons + `DataGateProjectDetail.tsx` 5-chip gap-filter row above existing state-filter row; ~50 lines of TSX changes total.

632. **Electron `setWindowOpenHandler` + plain `<a target="_blank">` is the simplest path to "open external URL in user's default browser" — no need to expose `shell.openExternal` through the preload bridge** — On 2026-05-04 adding DevOps deep-links to Data Gate drill-down rows. First attempt: tried `window.api.shell.openExternal(url)` via preload — but the preload didn't expose `shell` (correctly so, per least-privilege CSP-locked preload pattern). Adding it would mean updating preload + type declaration + invoking-via-IPC plumbing. Better approach: since `electron/main.ts:124-127` already has `mainWindow.webContents.setWindowOpenHandler(({ url }) => { shell.openExternal(url); return { action: 'deny' }; })`, plain HTML `<a href="…" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">` automatically routes through that handler — no JS, no preload changes, no IPC. The renderer's `window.open()` (which target=_blank uses internally) gets intercepted, denied for in-Electron rendering, and the URL is forwarded to the OS's default browser. **Why:** `setWindowOpenHandler` is Electron's intended hook for this exact use case. Adding `shell.openExternal` to preload is unnecessary plumbing when the framework already has a route. **How to apply:** (1) When you need "open external URL from renderer", check `electron/main.ts` for an existing `setWindowOpenHandler` first — most secure-Electron scaffolds have one. (2) If yes, just use `<a href target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">` — no JS, no preload exposure, no IPC. (3) If no, add the handler in main.ts (4 lines) before adding shell.openExternal to preload. The handler approach is inherently safer because the URL passes through main-process inspection. (4) Always add `rel="noopener noreferrer"` for security (window.opener exfiltration prevention). (5) Reference: May 4 — `DataGateProjectDetail.tsx` line ~199-208 — single `<a>` element handles deep-links to `https://dev.azure.com/TaqaTechno/{project}/_workitems/edit/{id}` with zero added preload surface.

633. **Cross-artifact parity audits MUST grep imports + call sites BEFORE flagging "missing" features — the v14↔PMO_OS Data Gate audit flagged "auto-draft 5-indicator governance hook missing in PMO_OS" but a 1-line grep of `ActionLog.tsx` revealed it was already wired at line 189 (`trpc.dataGate.latestByProject.useQuery()`)** — On 2026-05-04 building the v14↔PMO_OS Data Gate parity comparison, my initial 4-item gap list claimed "Auto-draft 5-indicator hook not yet ported to PMO_OS". User accepted the audit and asked to "fix all 4". Before editing, ran `grep auto.draft "D:\PMO_OS\src\components\ActionLog.tsx"` and found the hook fully implemented — the tagline `"Day 14: Auto-draft full 5-indicator pipeline (Data Gate · SPI · CPI · SBI · Velocity)"` plus a `trpc.dataGate.latestByProject` query. Item #4 was a false positive and got marked "no fix needed". If I'd implemented the "fix" without verifying, I'd have created a duplicate auto-draft path with conflicting state. **Why:** Parity audits comparing two artifacts (especially when one is a monolithic HTML and the other is split across many TS files) suffer from name-asymmetry — v14's `saveDataGateInsight()` lives in a single IIFE in `PMO Dashboard_v14.html`, while PMO_OS's equivalent is `auto-draft.ts` + `ActionLog.tsx` + `lib/insights-helpers.ts`. A surface-level "I don't see it in DataGate.tsx" reading misses cross-component wiring. **How to apply:** (1) Before flagging ANY feature as "missing in artifact B", grep the entire codebase for the function/concept name, not just the obvious component. (2) Look at imports at the top of related files — Action Log imports from `lib/auto-draft.ts` proves auto-draft exists somewhere. (3) Use `grep -r` with multiple synonyms (`autoDraft`, `auto-draft`, `auto_draft`, `dataGate.latestByProject`, etc.) — naming conventions vary. (4) State each gap claim with its evidence: "auto-draft missing — searched for X, Y, Z, found nothing" not just "auto-draft missing". (5) When the user asks to fix a list of gaps, re-verify each gap immediately before editing — confirming saves you from creating duplicate paths. (6) Reference: May 4 — initial audit listed 4 items; verification reduced to 3 actual fixes (% overlay + bilingual port + deep-links); the 4th was a false positive that would have cost ~30 minutes of duplicate wiring.

634. **When porting bilingual support to a TSX component file, ADD the new i18n keys to BOTH locale files in the SAME edit pair — never push to git or run dev with only EN populated, because i18next falls back to the key STRING when AR is missing, which causes the UI to display literal strings like `"dataGate.detail.colId"` in AR mode and looks like the worst kind of bug** — On 2026-05-04 adding `dataGate` namespace + 50 keys to en.json + ar.json. Standard pattern: write en.json keys → write parallel ar.json keys → wire `t('dataGate.title')` calls in TSX. If you stop after en.json (e.g., "I'll do AR after lunch"), `t('dataGate.title')` still resolves in EN mode (correctly) but in AR mode i18next fails the lookup and renders the key path itself — `dataGate.title` literal string in the UI. Looks broken to anyone testing the bilingual toggle. **Why:** i18next's missingKeyHandler defaults to "return key" — by design, so devs see a visible signal in development. But in production-track code the visible literal looks like a rendered template error. **How to apply:** (1) Edit en.json + ar.json in the same edit pair (use the Edit tool sequentially for both files before moving to TSX). (2) When adding keys, draft the AR translation alongside the EN string, not after — even rough draft is better than missing key (the key falls back to EN-equivalent rendering vs. the broken literal display). (3) For RTL languages, add a final pass that toggles to AR in dev mode and visually inspects the tab; literal `dataGate.detail.colId`-style strings instantly stand out. (4) Add a CI lint or dev-time console warning for missing AR keys — `i18next.on('missingKey', (lngs, ns, key) => console.warn(...))` is a 3-line install in i18n config. (5) Reference: May 4 — DataGate i18n port: 50 keys × 2 files (en.json + ar.json) added in same session; first build went straight to AR-mode visual check, no literal-key bugs.

635. **The 7-gap parity matrix (Identical / Improvement / Regression columns) is the right shape for a v14 HTML → v2 Electron migration audit because it makes the "regression" cell the only blocking item, while improvements get celebrated and identical rows get a one-line ✓** — On 2026-05-04 producing the v14↔PMO_OS SPI/CPI tab parity report, the audit produced a 7-row table: Storage, Bilingual EN/AR, Drill-down, Live fetch UX, Sorting, Alaqraboon split, Governance hooks. 4 rows were "PMO_OS already better" (storage = SQLite history vs localStorage, drill-down has variance modal v14 lacks, fetch UX has progress bar, Alaqraboon split via area path). 1 row was a true regression (Bilingual: v14 had data-i18n + AR translations, PMO_OS shipped hardcoded English). 2 rows were cosmetic differences (sort order, sample-size criterion). The regression was the only item that BLOCKED the May 18 dual-track demo where users would toggle to Arabic. By making the regression visually distinct (red badge in the matrix), the "what to fix first" decision became automatic. **Why:** Migration audits typically dump everything as a flat "diff list" which buries the regression in a sea of nice-to-haves. The 3-column structure (Identical / Improvement / Regression) forces classification; the act of classifying surfaces the blocking item. Improvements double as marketing for the migration ("v2 also gains X, Y, Z") which buys goodwill from stakeholders questioning the migration cost. **How to apply:** (1) For any artifact-vs-artifact migration audit, structure findings as a 3-column matrix: green ✓ for parity, blue ↑ for v2-improvements, red ⚠ for regressions. (2) Block the migration on regressions only — improvements and cosmetic differences are NOT blockers. (3) Each cell has line numbers + evidence (e.g., `v14: line 5558 spi formula identical to v2 lib/devops-fetch.ts:325`). (4) Save the audit as a permanent .md plan file before editing — it becomes the prioritization doc when the user says "fix all gaps". (5) Reference: May 4 — `C:\Users\DELL\.claude\plans\did-we-have-a-glowing-nygaard.md` produced this matrix for SPI/CPI tab; 1 regression (i18n) closed first, then 2 cosmetic gaps + 1 improvement (sparkline) added beyond v14.

636. **A 64×16 inline SVG polyline is enough trend visualization for at-a-glance review — no chart library, no dependency, ~40 LOC including y-axis clamp + threshold reference line + zone-colored end-cap** — On 2026-05-04 closing the storage-history gap (PMO_OS SQLite keeps multi-snapshot history per project, v14 localStorage couldn't), added a `<SpiSparkline values={number[]} />` component inline in `SpiCpi.tsx`. Renders the project's last N SPI values as an SVG polyline with: (a) y-axis clamped [0, 1.2] for stable cross-row comparison, (b) horizontal dashed line at y=0.80 marking the green threshold, (c) end-cap circle colored by latest-value zone (green ≥0.80 / amber ≥0.50 / red <0.50), (d) graceful degradation: 0 values → em-dash, 1 value → centered dot, 2+ values → full polyline. **Why:** Tiny per-row sparklines need the smallest possible footprint — chart libraries (Recharts, Visx, even d3-shape) add 50KB+ JS and force ResponsiveContainer/measurement plumbing that doesn't make sense for 64×16 cells. Inline SVG with a y-scaling helper is faster, smaller, and easier to style with the existing Tailwind palette. (`stroke="#22c55e"` matches `bg-zone-green`.) **How to apply:** (1) For per-row trend cells ≤ 80px wide, write the SVG by hand — `polyline points="x,y x,y …"` is dead simple. (2) Always include a threshold reference line (`<line strokeDasharray="2 2">`) so the user reads the sparkline against a fixed benchmark, not just relative to itself. (3) End-cap dot colored by zone makes the latest reading immediately readable without inspecting numbers. (4) y-axis must be clamped to a fixed range across all rows — auto-scaling per row makes rows incomparable. For SPI use [0, 1.2]; for CPI use [0, 1.5]; for percentages use [0, 100]. (5) Bake fall-back rendering for 0/1 data points (em-dash / centered dot) — most projects in fresh DBs have only 1 snapshot. (6) Reference: May 4 — `D:\PMO_OS\src\components\SpiCpi.tsx:65-98` `SpiSparkline` component; 0 added deps; pulls history via `trpc.execSnapshots.list({limit:500}).useQuery()` indexed by project on the client.

637. **Adding a sort-key field (`taskCount`) to a SQLite/Drizzle table for v14 parity is a 5-touch phase-additive migration: schema → drizzle-kit generate → fetcher return → zod input → consumer sort callback. Default 0 NOT NULL keeps pre-migration rows usable without backfill** — On 2026-05-04 closing parity gap #5 (v14 sorts SPI/CPI table by task count desc; PMO_OS sorted by planned-hours desc). Touched 5 files in one commit: (a) `src/main/db/schema.ts` — added `taskCount: integer('task_count').notNull().default(0)`; (b) `npx drizzle-kit generate --name task_count` produced `0003_task_count.sql` with a single `ALTER TABLE … ADD COLUMN`; (c) `src/lib/devops-fetch.ts` — added `taskCount: number` to `ProjectSpiCpiSnapshot` interface + return `items.length` from `fetchSpiCpiForProject`; (d) `src/main/trpc/router.ts` — added `taskCount: z.number().int().nonnegative().optional().default(0)` to `execSnapshotInsertInput`; (e) `src/components/SpiCpi.tsx` — sort callback `(b.taskCount ?? 0) - (a.taskCount ?? 0)` with `b.planned - a.planned` tiebreaker for pre-migration rows that have taskCount=0. **Why:** Phase-additive (lesson #609) means existing rows must continue to deserialize and render. `NOT NULL DEFAULT 0` lets SQLite back-populate the column atomically during ALTER. The optional+default zod modifier prevents older insert callers from breaking. The tiebreaker on planned-hours keeps pre-migration rows ordered sensibly until they get re-fetched and gain real taskCount values. **How to apply:** (1) For any new sort-key field, the 5 touches are: schema → drizzle generate → DTO interface → zod input → consumer sort. (2) Always `NOT NULL DEFAULT <sane>` for additive columns so old rows survive. (3) Always make the zod input field optional with a default — protects older callers. (4) Always include a tiebreaker on a pre-existing field for rows where the new field is at default. (5) Test: run the existing test suite once before edit + once after; both must pass at 0 errors with no migration-related test additions needed (additive change). (6) Reference: May 4 — exec_snapshots gained `task_count` column via migration 0003; SpiCpi.tsx sort `(a, b) => (b.taskCount ?? 0) - (a.taskCount ?? 0) || (b.planned - a.planned)` matches v14's behavior for new rows + degrades gracefully for legacy.
